Stratified Hyperbolicity of the moduli stack of stable minimal models, I
Junchao Shentu

Abstract
. In this paper, we introduce a birationally admissible stratification on the Deligne-Mumford stack of stable minimal models (e.g., the KSBA moduli stack), such that the universal family over each stratum admits a simple normal crossing log birational model. We further demonstrate that each stratum is hyperbolic in the sense that every schematic generically finite covering of any closed substack is of logarithmic general type. This provides a partial answer to C.Birkar’s question regarding the global geometry of the moduli of stable minimal models.



1.
Introduction

The construction of compact moduli spaces for algebraic varieties is a foundational problem in algebraic geometry. Since the groundbreaking work of P. Deligne and D. Mumford
[
8
]
on the moduli of stable curves, significant efforts have been devoted to extending the construction of compact moduli spaces to higher-dimensional varieties. The primary goal is to establish a robust framework for studying families of algebraic varieties with prescribed properties. These endeavors have resulted in the development of a rich and diverse theory of moduli spaces, which has found applications across various areas of algebraic geometry and related fields. Notable examples include meaningful compactifications of moduli spaces for polarized abelian varieties
[
1
]
, plane curves
[
19
]
, manifolds of general type
[
25
,
27
,
30
]
, polarized Calabi-Yau manifolds
[
26
]
, and
K
-polystable Fano manifolds
[
53
]
, among others.
Given
d\in\mathbb{N}
,
c\in\mathbb{Q}^{\geq 0}
, a finite set
\Gamma\subset\mathbb{Q}^{>0}
, and
\sigma\in\mathbb{Q}[t]
, C. Birkar
[
4
]
introduced the concept of a
(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)
-stable minimal model as a triple
(X,B),A
, where
X
is a projective scheme of finite type over
{\rm Spec}(\mathbb{C})
, and
A\geq 0
,
B\geq 0
are
\mathbb{Q}
-divisors on
X
satisfying the following conditions:

•

\dim X=d
,
(X,B)
forms an slc projective pair, and
K_{X}+B
is semi-ample,

•

the coefficients of
A
and
B
lie in
c\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0}
,

•

(X,B+tA)
is slc, and
K_{X}+B+tA
is ample for some
t>0
,

•

{\rm vol}(K_{X}+B+tA)=\sigma(t)
for
0\leq t\ll 1
, and

•

{\rm vol}(A|_{F})\in\Gamma
, where
F
denotes any general fiber of the fibration
f:X\to Z
determined by
K_{X}+B
.

The moduli stack
\mathscr{M}_{\rm slc}(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)
of
(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)
-stable minimal models (see
[
4
]
or Section
5.1
) is complete and admits a projective good coarse moduli space (
[
4
, Theorem 1.14]
), which provides a meaningful compactification of the moduli of birational equivalence classes of projective manifolds with arbitrary Kodaira dimension. In this article, we investigate the global geometry of
\mathscr{M}_{\rm slc}(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)
, with particular emphasis on its stratified hyperbolicity.

Research in this area was initiated by the pioneering works of A. N. Paršin
[
38
]
, S. Ju. Arakelov
[
2
]
, and a series of contributions by Viehweg-Zuo
[
48
,
49
,
50
]
, focusing on the principal stratum of certain moduli spaces where the fibers are smooth varieties. In this paper, we establish the stratified hyperbolicity properties of
\mathscr{M}_{\rm slc}(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)
, encompassing both the principal stratum and the boundary strata.

The main result of this paper is the following theorem:

Theorem 1.1
.

Let
f^{o}:(X^{o},B^{o}),A^{o}\to S^{o}
be a birationally admissible family of
(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)
-stable minimal models over a smooth quasi-projective variety
S^{o}
, defining a generically finite classifying map
\xi^{o}:S^{o}\to\mathscr{M}_{\rm slc}(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)
. Then
S^{o}
is of log general type.

A family of stable minimal models
(X^{o},B^{o}),A^{o}\to S^{o}
is said to be
birationally admissible
if
(X^{o},A^{o}+B^{o})
admits a simple normal crossing log birational model (Definition
5.9
). Informally, the birationally admissible condition implies that the family does not possess degenerate fibers in the context of birational geometry. This condition is essential for Theorem
1.1
, as illustrated by the following examples:

(1)

The presence of a degenerating fiber.
Let
f:X\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
be a Lefschetz pencil with
S\subset\mathbb{P}^{1}
denoting the set of its critical values, such that the general fibers of
f
are canonically polarized
d
-folds with volume
v
. Then
f:(X,0),0\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
forms a family of
(d,\Phi_{0},\{1\},v)
-stable minimal models. While
\mathbb{P}^{1}
is not hyperbolic,
\mathbb{P}^{1}\setminus S
is hyperbolic (
|S|\geq 3
, as shown in
[
48
]
).

(2)

The presence of a degenerating polarization.
Let
E
be an elliptic curve and
x_{0}\in E(\mathbb{C})
. Define
X=E\times E
, and let
f:E\times E\to E
denote the projection onto the first component. Let
A=\frac{1}{2}(E\times\{x_{0}\}\cup\Delta_{E})
, where
\Delta_{E}\subset E\times E
is the diagonal. Then
f:(X,0),A\to E
forms a family of
(1,\Phi_{\frac{1}{2}},\{1\},t)
-stable minimal models. Although the underlying family of elliptic curves is trivial, the family of polarizations is non-isotrivial and degenerates at
x_{0}
. Consequently, the entire base
E
is not hyperbolic, whereas
E\setminus\{x_{0}\}
is hyperbolic.

Example 1.2

(KSBA-stable families)
.

A projective pair
(X,B)
is said to be a KSBA-stable pair if
(X,B),0
is a stable minimal model. A family of KSBA-stable varieties is considered admissible if it admits a simple normal crossing log birational model. The base of a maximally variational admissible family of KSBA pairs satisfies the hyperbolicity properties stated in Theorem
1.1
.

1.1.
Stratified hyperbolicity of moduli of stable minimal models

Given a functorial semi-log desingularization procedure (c.f.
[
3
]
,
[
29
, §10.4]
), one can associate with
\mathscr{M}_{\rm slc}(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)
a stratification such that over each stratum
\mathscr{S}
, the universal family is strictly birationally admissible (i.e., any base change admits a simple normal crossing log birational model). Such a stratification is referred to as a
birationally admissible stratification
(Definition
6.5
). A birationally admissible stratification always exists but is typically not unique. It is determined by the choice of the functorial desingularization functor. A typical example arises in the KSBA compactification of the moduli space of principally polarized manifolds. In this case, the open substack parameterizing smooth fibers constitutes a birationally admissible stratum, while the birationally admissible stratification on the boundary is determined by the semi-log desingularization process.

Let
S
be a smooth variety. We say that
S
is of
log general type
if there exists a smooth compactification
\overline{S}\supseteq S
such that
D=\overline{S}\setminus S
is a divisor and
K_{\overline{S}}+D
is big. The condition of being of log general type is independent of the choice of compactification.
A smooth Deligne-Mumford stack
\mathscr{S}
is said to be
hyperbolic
if, for every generically finite morphism
S\to\mathscr{S}
from a quasi-projective smooth variety
S
, the variety
S
is of log general type.

A direct corollary of Theorem
1.1
is the following result.

Theorem 1.3
.

Each stratum of a birationally admissible stratification of
\mathscr{M}_{\rm slc}(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)
is hyperbolic.

For
\overline{\mathscr{M}}_{g,n}
, the moduli stack of genus
g
stable curves with
n
marked points satisfying
2g-2+n>0
, the boundary simple normal crossing divisor
\partial\mathscr{M}_{g,n}:=\overline{\mathscr{M}}_{g,n}\setminus\mathscr{M}_{g,n}
induces a
canonical stratification
. The distinct strata of this stratification correspond precisely to the distinct topological types of the fibers. This stratification is birationally admissible. Consequently, we derive the following result, which is novel as it elucidates the hyperbolicity of the strata on the boundary.

Corollary 1.4
.

Every stratum of the canonical stratification of
\overline{\mathscr{M}}_{g,n}
is hyperbolic. In particular,
\mathscr{M}_{g,n}
is hyperbolic.

1.2.
Historical accounts

Significant efforts have been devoted to investigating the hyperbolicity properties of various family of polarized projective manifolds (which are automatically admissible). For an incomplete list, see
[
32
,
33
,
36
,
48
,
34
,
35
,
22
,
23
,
24
,
39
,
41
,
6
,
47
,
52
]
.

In the case of families of canonically polarized manifolds (known as the Viehweg hyperbolicity conjecture), the result was first established by Campana-Pǎun
[
6
]
, building upon their work on foliations and the Viehweg-Zuo sheaves constructed in
[
50
]
. By employing Saito’s theory of Hodge modules
[
42
]
, Popa-Schnell
[
41
]
extended the construction of Viehweg-Zuo sheaves to base spaces of arbitrary families of projective varieties with a geometric generic fiber admitting a good minimal model, thereby proving the corresponding hyperbolicity property for these families. Popa-Schnell’s result was subsequently generalized by Wei-Wu
[
52
]
to log smooth families of pairs of log general type. Additionally, there exist other admissible conditions ensuring hyperbolicity; see, for example,
[
20
,
37
]
.

1.3.
Strategy and organization

The main strategy of this paper builds upon the work of Viehweg-Zuo
[
48
,
50
]
, employing variations of Hodge structures to construct a Higgs sheaf, while incorporating the foliation theory developed by Campana-Pǎun
[
6
]
. A significant challenge arises from the presence of singularities and boundary divisors on the fibers, even after performing semi-resolutions of singularities. This motivates us to utilize variations of mixed Hodge structures to analyze families of pairs. However, there exist multiple types of mixed Hodge structures associated with singular pairs, such as singular cohomology, intersection cohomology, cohomology with compact supports, Borel-Moore cohomology, and others. Inspired by Fujino-Fujisawa’s work
[
15
]
, which employs cohomology with compact supports to study families of simple normal crossing pairs, leading to the proof of the projectivity of the coarse moduli space of KSBA pairs
[
17
,
31
]
, we choose in this paper the Borel-Moore cohomology (the dual of cohomology with compact supports), along with its mixed Hodge structure and its variation within families, to construct a Higgs sheaf of Viehweg-Zuo type.

Given a datum
(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)
, an integer
r\gg 1
, and a rational number
0<a\ll 1
, the coherent sheaves
f_{\ast}(\mathscr{O}_{X}(rK_{X/S}+rB+raA))
associated with families of
(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)
-stable minimal models
f:(X,B),A\to S
over reduced base schemes
S
yield a locally free coherent sheaf on the Deligne-Mumford stack
\mathscr{M}_{\rm slc}(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)
. The determinant line bundle descends to an ample
\mathbb{Q}
-line bundle on the coarse moduli space (
[
17
,
31
]
), thereby ensuring the projectivity of the coarse moduli space via Kollár’s criterion
[
28
]
.

The main contribution of this paper is the observation that the Kollár-type polarization
\det f_{\ast}(\mathscr{O}_{X}(rK_{X/S}+rB+raA))
encapsulates substantial information about the moduli stack. More precisely, it is demonstrated that certain admissible variations of Borel-Moore cohomology contain
\det f_{\ast}(\mathscr{O}_{X}(rK_{X/S}+rB+raA))
. This variation of mixed Hodge structures encodes critical information about the family. In particular the associated logarithmic Higgs bundle induces a non-zero morphism

\det f_{\ast}(\mathscr{O}_{X}(rK_{X/S}+rB+raA))\otimes K\to\Omega^{\otimes k}_{S}(\log D),

where
m>0
is some integer,
D
is a simple normal crossing divisor on
S
over which
f
fails to be birationally admissible, and
K
is a weakly positive coherent sheaf. As a result,
K_{S}+D
is big due to the work of Campana-Pǎun
[
6
]
. In a subsequent paper, we will utilize this Viehweg-Zuo type Higgs sheaf to explore additional global geometric properties of
\mathscr{M}_{\rm slc}(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)
.

In Section 2, we conduct an in-depth analysis of the Borel-Moore cohomology of simple normal crossing projective pairs, their mixed Hodge structures, and their variations within families. The primary result of this section is that the Borel-Moore cohomologies of a projective simple normal crossing family of pairs carry a graded polarizable variation of mixed Hodge structures (Theorem
2.14
).

In Section 3, we examine the lower canonical extension of the variation of Borel-Moore cohomologies. One of the key results of this section is the admissibility of the variation of Borel-Moore cohomologies associated with a simple normal crossing family of projective pairs (Theorem
3.13
). Although Fujino-Fujisawa
[
15
]
have established the theory of variations of cohomologies with compact supports, we still require a detailed investigation of the variation of Borel-Moore cohomology due to the necessity of constructing its Deligne’s lower canonical extension geometrically (Theorem
3.16
). In this section, we do not assume unipotency of local monodromies, which enhances applicability.

In Section 4, we provide a general construction of a Higgs sheaf of Viehweg-Zuo type associated with a projective morphism from a simple normal crossing pair to a smooth variety.

In Section 5, we review the foundational constructions of Birkar
[
4
]
on stable minimal models and their moduli. Subsequently, we introduce the birationally admissible condition on families of stable minimal models (Definition
5.9
) and construct a Higgs sheaf of Viehweg-Zuo type associated with an admissible family of stable minimal models (Theorem
5.10
).

The proofs of Theorem
1.1
and Theorem
1.3
are presented in Section 6.

2.
Variation of Borel-Moore cohomology

2.1.
Basic notations

Definition 2.1

(Simple normal crossing pair)
.

Let
S
and
X
be reduced schemes of finite type over
\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{C})
, and let
D\geq 0
be a Weil
\mathbb{Q}
-divisor on
X
. Let
f:X\to S
be a morphism. The morphism
f:(X,D)\to S
is said to be
simple normal crossing over
S
at a point
x\in X
if there exists a Zariski open neighborhood
U
of
x
in
X
that can be embedded into a scheme
Y
, which is smooth over
S
. In this embedding,
Y
admits a regular system of parameters
(z_{1},\dots,z_{p},y_{1},\dots,y_{r})
over
S
at the point corresponding to
x=0
, such that
U
is defined by the monomial equation
z_{1}\cdots z_{p}=0
and

D|_{U}=\sum_{i=1}^{r}a_{i}(y_{i}=0)|_{U},\quad\text{where }a_{i}\geq 0,

over
S
.

The pair
f:(X,D)\to S
is said to be a
simple normal crossing family over
S
if it satisfies the simple normal crossing condition over
S
at every point of
X
. A family is referred to as a
log smooth family over
S
if it is a simple normal crossing family over
S
and each fiber
X_{s}:=f^{-1}(s)
, for
s\in S
, is smooth (not necessarily connected).

In the special case where
S=\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{C})
, the pair
(X,D)
is referred to as a
simple normal crossing pair
if
(X,D)
forms a simple normal crossing family over
\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{C})
. In this context,
X
has Gorenstein singularities and possesses an invertible dualizing sheaf
\omega_{X}
. The canonical divisor
K_{X}
is defined up to linear equivalence via the isomorphism
\omega_{X}\simeq\mathscr{O}_{X}(K_{X})
.

A simple normal crossing pair
(X,D)
is called a
log smooth pair
if
X
is smooth (note that
X
is not necessarily connected). In this case, the support
\operatorname{Supp}(D)
has simple normal crossings.

Definition 2.2

(Semistable morphism)
.

Let
f:(X,\Delta)\to(S,D_{S})
be a morphism from a simple normal crossing pair
(X,\Delta)
to a log smooth pair
(S,D_{S})
. The morphism
f
is called
semistable
if the following conditions are satisfied:

(1)

D_{S}
is a reduced, smooth (not necessarily connected) divisor on
S
such that
\operatorname{Supp}(f^{-1}(D_{S}))
is a simple normal crossing divisor contained in
\Delta_{\text{red}}
. Note that the components of
f^{-1}(D_{S})
may have multiplicities.

(2)

f:(X,\Delta)\to S
is a simple normal crossing family over
S\setminus D_{S}
.

(3)

Each stratum of
(X,\Delta)
lying in
f^{-1}(D_{S})
is mapped submersively by
f
onto an irreducible component of
D_{S}
.

Remark 2.3
.

Let
X
be a reduced scheme of finite type over
\operatorname{Spec}(\mathbb{C})
, and let
D\geq 0
be a Weil divisor on
X
. Let
S
be a smooth variety and
f:(X,D)\to S
be a projective morphism such that each stratum of
X
dominates
S
.
By
[
3
]
or
[
29
, §10.4]
, there is a
semi-log resolution
of singularities
\mu:(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})\to(X,\Delta)
such that the following conditions hold.

•

(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
is a simple normal crossing pair.

•

Let
X_{\rm sn}\subset X
be the maximal Zariski open subset such that
(X_{\rm sn},X_{\rm sn}\cap D)
is a simple normal crossing pair.
\mu
is an isomorphism over
X_{\rm sn}
, and
\Delta^{\prime}
is the strict transform of
\Delta
.

•

There is a dense Zariski open subset
U\subset S
and a smooth (but not necessarily connected) divisor
D_{U}
on
U
such that
{\rm codim}_{S}(S\backslash U)\geq 2
and
(f^{-1}(U),{\rm supp}(\Delta)\cap f^{-1}(U))\to(U,D_{U})
is a semistable morphism.

2.2.
The mixed Hodge structure on a log smooth projective pair

Let
M
be a complex manifold. Let
DFW(M)
denote the derived bi-filtered category on
M
(cf.
[
10
,
11
,
40
]
). Consider an object
H=(K^{\bullet},F^{\bullet},W_{\bullet})\in DFW(M)
and let
m\in\mathbb{Z}
. The shift of
H
by
m
, denoted as
H[m]
, is defined as

H[m]=(K^{\bullet}[m],F^{\bullet}(H[m]),W_{\bullet}(H[m]))

where

F^{p}(H[m])=F^{p}[m],\quad W_{k}(H[m])=W_{k-m}[m].

Its Tate twist
H(m)
is defined as

((2\pi\sqrt{-1})^{2m}K^{\bullet},F^{\bullet}(H(m)),W_{\bullet}(H(m)))

where

F^{p}(H(m))=F^{p+m},\quad W_{k}(H(m))=W_{k+2m}.

Let
(X,D)
be a log smooth projective pair where
X
is a projective smooth variety and
D
is a simple normal crossing divisor on
X
. We now recall the mixed Hodge complex associated with
(X,D)
.

Let
X^{o}:=X\setminus D
, and let
j:X^{o}\to X
be the open immersion. The rational structure is given by the complex of constructible sheaves
Rj_{\ast}\mathbb{Q}_{X^{o}}
together with the canonical filtration (the rational weight filtration)
\tau=\{\tau_{m}:=\tau^{\leq m}(Rj_{\ast}\mathbb{Q}_{X^{o}})\}_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}
.

Let
\Omega^{\bullet}_{X}(\log D)
be the logarithmic de Rham complex of
(X,D)
. This complex is endowed with the naive filtration (the Hodge filtration)
F^{\bullet}=\{F^{p}:=\Omega^{\geq p}_{X}(\log D)\}_{p\geq 0}
and the weight filtration
W_{\bullet}
, characterized by

\displaystyle W_{m}\Omega^{p}_{X}(\log D)=\begin{cases}0,&m<0\\
\Omega^{p}_{X}(\log D),&m\geq p\\
\Omega^{p-m}_{X}\wedge\Omega^{m}_{X}(\log D),&0\leq m\leq p\end{cases}.

Proposition 2.4
.

(
[
40
, §4.3]
)

\left((Rj_{\ast}\mathbb{Q}_{X^{o}},\tau),(\Omega^{\bullet}_{X}(\log D),W_{\bullet},F^{\bullet})\right)
is a mixed Hodge complex of sheaves.

Let
D=\bigcup_{i\in I}D_{i}
. For every non-empty subset
J\subset I
and integer
k>0
, denote

D_{J}=\bigcap_{i\in J}D_{i}\quad\text{and}\quad D_{[k]}=\coprod_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\subset I\\
|J|=k\end{subarray}}D_{J}.

We also set
D_{[0]}=X
. The Hodge filtration
F^{\bullet}
induces a filtration on
\operatorname{Gr}^{W}_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X}(\log D)
, which is denoted by
F^{\bullet}\operatorname{Gr}^{W}_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X}(\log D)
.

Lemma 2.5
.

(
[
40
, §4.3]
)

The filtered complex
(\operatorname{Gr}^{W}_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X}(\log D),F^{\bullet}\operatorname{Gr}^{W}_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X}(\log D))
is filtered quasi-isomorphic to
(\Omega^{\bullet}_{D_{[m]}},F^{\bullet})(-m)[-m]
, where
(\Omega^{\bullet}_{D_{[m]}},F^{\bullet})
is the standard Hodge complex of sheaves on
D_{[m]}
. In particular, there is a natural isomorphism

\operatorname{Gr}^{p}_{F}\operatorname{Gr}^{W}_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X}(\log D)\simeq\Omega^{p-m}_{D_{[m]}}[-p].

2.3.
The Gysin map

Let
(X,D)
be a log smooth pair, and let
Y
be a smooth divisor on
X
that intersects transversally with every stratum of
D
. Consequently,
(Y,D\cap Y)
forms a log smooth pair. For notational convenience, we use
j
to denote both the open immersions
X^{o}:=X\backslash D\to X
and
Y^{o}:=Y\backslash D\to Y
. In this section, we establish that the Gysin map
Rj_{\ast}\mathbb{Q}_{Y^{o}}(-1)[-2]\to Rj_{\ast}\mathbb{Q}_{X^{o}}
, which is the Verdier dual of the restriction map
Rj_{\ast}\mathbb{Q}_{X^{o}}\to Rj_{\ast}\mathbb{Q}_{Y^{o}}
, constitutes a morphism between mixed Hodge complexes of sheaves.

Consider the residue sequence

(2.1)

\displaystyle 0\to\Omega^{\bullet}_{X}(\log D)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\iota}}{{\to}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X}(\log(D+Y))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm Res}_{Y}}}{{\to}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{Y}(\log(D\cap Y))[-1]\to 0.

This sequence preserves the Hodge filtrations and weight filtrations in the following sense:

\iota(F^{p})\subset F^{p},\quad\iota(W_{m})\subset W_{m},

and

{\rm Res}_{Y}(F^{p})\subset F^{p-1}[-1],\quad{\rm Res}_{Y}(W_{m})\subset W_{m-1}[-1].

Lemma 2.6
.

The sequence (
2.1
) is bi-filtered exact, meaning that the induced sequence

0\to{\rm Gr}^{p}_{F}{\rm Gr}^{W}_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X}(\log D)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\iota}}{{\to}}{\rm Gr}^{p}_{F}{\rm Gr}^{W}_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X}(\log(D+Y))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm Res}_{Y}}}{{\to}}{\rm Gr}^{p-1}_{F}{\rm Gr}^{W}_{m-1}\Omega^{\bullet}_{Y}(\log(D\cap Y))[-1]\to 0

is exact for all
m
and
p
.

Proof.

According to Lemma
2.5
, the sequence is isomorphic to

0\to\Omega^{p-m}_{D_{[m]}}[-p]\to\Omega^{p-m}_{D_{[m]}}[-p]\oplus\Omega^{p-m}_{Y\cap D_{[m-1]}}[-p]\to\Omega^{p-m}_{Y\cap D_{[m-1]}}[-p]\to 0,

which is exact for all
m
and
p
.
∎

As a consequence, (
2.1
) yields a distinguished triangle

\Omega^{\bullet}_{X}(\log D)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\iota}}{{\to}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X}(\log(D+Y))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm Res}_{Y}}}{{\to}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{Y}(\log(D\cap Y))(-1)[-1]\to\Omega^{\bullet}_{X}(\log D)[1]

in
DFW(X)
. The boundary map induces the Gysin map

\Omega^{\bullet}_{Y}(\log(D\cap Y))(-1)[-2]\to\Omega^{\bullet}_{X}(\log D)

in
DFW(X)
, which is compatible with the topological Gysin map

Rj_{\ast}\mathbb{Q}_{Y^{o}}(-1)[-2]\to Rj_{\ast}\mathbb{Q}_{X^{o}}.

2.4.
Borel-Moore cohomology

Let
X^{o}
be an algebraic variety of pure dimension
n
. Its Borel-Moore cohomology is defined by

H^{i}_{\rm BM}(X^{o},\mathbb{Q}):=H^{2n-i}_{c}(X^{o},\mathbb{Q})^{\ast},\quad\forall i,

where
H^{\ast}_{c}
denotes the compactly supported cohomology. Now assume that
X^{o}=X\backslash D
, where
(X,D)
is a simple normal crossing projective pair. We are particularly interested in its Borel-Moore cohomology because
H^{0}(X,\mathscr{O}_{X}(K_{X}+D))
embeds into
H^{i}_{\rm BM}(X^{o},\mathbb{Q})\otimes\mathbb{C}
as the lowest Hodge piece, as observed by Fujino-Fujisawa
[
15
]
. We will elaborate on this observation, as it provides insight into the construction we employ in the Viehweg-Zuo Higgs sheaves associated with a simple normal crossing family.

For the remainder of this section, we assume that
(X,D)
is a simple normal crossing projective pair, where
X
is of pure dimension
n
. Let
X^{o}:=X\backslash D
, and let
j:X^{o}\to X
be the open immersion. Denote the irreducible decompositions as follows:

X=\bigcup_{i\in I}X_{i}\quad\text{and}\quad D=\bigcup_{\lambda\in\Lambda}D_{\lambda},

where
I
and
\Lambda
are finite sets. For every non-empty subset
J\subset I
and integer
k>0
, define

X_{J}:=\bigcap_{i\in J}X_{i}\quad\text{and}\quad X_{[k]}:=\coprod_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\subset I\\
|J|=k\end{subarray}}X_{J}.

Let
X^{o}_{J}:=X_{J}\backslash D
for
\emptyset\neq J\subset I
, and set
X^{o}_{[k]}:=\coprod_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\subset I\\
|J|=k\end{subarray}}X^{o}_{J}
. Denote by
i^{o}_{[k]}:X^{o}_{[k]}\to X^{o}
the natural morphism. We then have the following quasi-isomorphism:

(2.2)

\displaystyle\mathbb{Q}_{X^{o}}\simeq_{\rm qis}i^{o}_{[1]\ast}\mathbb{Q}_{X^{o}_{[1]}}\to i^{o}_{[2]\ast}\mathbb{Q}_{X^{o}_{[2]}}\to\cdots,

where the map
\mathbb{Q}_{X^{o}_{[k]}}\to\mathbb{Q}_{X^{o}_{[k+1]}}
is the alternating sum of restriction maps for each
k>0
. To simplify notation, we still use
j
to denote the immersions
X^{o}_{[k]}\to X_{[k]}
. Applying
Rj_{!}
on both sides of (
2.2
) yields

(2.3)

\displaystyle Rj_{!}\mathbb{Q}_{X^{o}}\simeq_{\rm qis}{\rm Tot}\left(Rj_{!}i^{o}_{[1]\ast}\mathbb{Q}_{X^{o}_{[1]}}\to Rj_{!}i^{o}_{[2]\ast}\mathbb{Q}_{X^{o}_{[2]}}\to\cdots\right).

Note that

H^{i}_{c}(X^{o},\mathbb{Q})\simeq\mathbb{H}^{i}(X,Rj_{!}\mathbb{Q}_{X^{o}}).

By Verdier duality, we have

H^{i}_{\rm BM}(X^{o},\mathbb{Q})\simeq\mathbb{H}^{i}(X,\mathbb{D}_{X}(Rj_{!}\mathbb{Q}_{X^{o}}[2n])),

where
\mathbb{D}_{X}
denotes the Verdier duality operator on
D(X)
. Applying Verdier duality to (
2.3
), we obtain

(2.4)

\displaystyle\mathbb{D}_{X}(Rj_{!}\mathbb{Q}_{X^{o}}[2n])

\displaystyle\simeq_{\rm qis}{\rm Tot}\left(\cdots\to\mathbb{D}_{X}Rj_{!}i^{o}_{[2]\ast}\mathbb{Q}_{X^{o}_{[2]}}\to\mathbb{D}_{X}Rj_{!}i^{o}_{[1]\ast}\mathbb{Q}_{X^{o}_{[1]}}\right)[-2n]

\displaystyle\simeq_{\rm qis}{\rm Tot}\left(\cdots\to Rj_{\ast}i^{o}_{[2]\ast}\mathbb{D}_{X^{o}_{[2]}}\mathbb{Q}_{X^{o}_{[2]}}\to Rj_{\ast}i^{o}_{[1]\ast}\mathbb{D}_{X^{o}_{[1]}}\mathbb{Q}_{X^{o}_{[1]}}\right)[-2n]

\displaystyle\simeq_{\rm qis}{\rm Tot}\left(\cdots\to i_{[2]\ast}Rj_{\ast}\mathbb{Q}_{X^{o}_{[2]}}(-1)[-2]\to i_{[1]\ast}Rj_{\ast}\mathbb{Q}_{X^{o}_{[1]}}\right),

where
i_{[k]}:X_{[k]}\to X
(
k>0
) is the natural morphism, and
\mathbb{Q}_{X^{o}_{[k+1]}}[-2k]\to\mathbb{Q}_{X^{o}_{[k]}}[-2k+2]
(
k>0
) is the alternating sum of Gysin maps. Note that
i_{[1]\ast}Rj_{\ast}\mathbb{Q}_{X^{o}_{[1]}}
lies in the 0th term of the complex on the right-hand side of (
2.4
).

2.5.
Mixed Hodge structure on the Borel-Moore cohomology

(
2.4
) suggests the canonical mixed Hodge structure on
H^{i}_{\rm BM}(X^{o},\mathbb{Q})
. In fact, the complex

(2.5)

\displaystyle\cdots\to i_{[3]\ast}Rj_{\ast}\mathbb{Q}_{X^{o}_{[3]}}(-2)[-4]\to i_{[2]\ast}Rj_{\ast}\mathbb{Q}_{X^{o}_{[2]}}(-1)[-2]\to i_{[1]\ast}Rj_{\ast}\mathbb{Q}_{X^{o}_{[1]}}

(where
i_{[1]\ast}Rj_{\ast}\mathbb{Q}_{X^{o}_{[1]}}
is placed in the 0th term) underlies a complex of mixed Hodge complexes (or a complex of mixed Hodge modules).

2.5.1.
The rational structure and the weight filtration

For each
k\geq 0
, let
\tau_{[k]}
denote the canonical filtration of
Rj_{\ast}\mathbb{Q}_{X^{o}_{[k]}}
, defined by
\tau_{[k],i}:=\tau^{\leq i}(Rj_{\ast}\mathbb{Q}_{X^{o}_{[k]}})
. These filtrations induce an increasing filtration (the rational weight filtration)
\tau_{\bullet}
on
\mathbb{D}_{X}(Rj_{!}\mathbb{Q}_{X^{o}})
, via (
2.4
), by defining

(2.6)

\displaystyle\tau_{m}

\displaystyle:={\rm Tot}\left(\cdots\to i_{[2]\ast}\tau^{\leq m-1}(Rj_{\ast}\mathbb{Q}_{X^{o}_{[2]}}(-1)[-2])\to i_{[1]\ast}\tau^{\leq m}(Rj_{\ast}\mathbb{Q}_{X^{o}_{[1]}})\right)

2.5.2.
The Hodge filtration

Let
\emptyset\neq K\subset J\subset I
such that
|K|=|J|-1
, and let
D_{K}:=X_{K}\backslash X^{o}_{K}
. Note that
X_{J}
is a smooth divisor on
X_{K}
, and both
D_{K}
and
X_{J}+D_{K}
are simple normal crossing divisors on
X_{K}
. Consider the Gysin map

\displaystyle\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{J}}(\log X_{J}\cap D_{K})(-1)[-2]\to\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{K}}(\log D_{K})

in
DFW(X)
, constructed in §
2.3
. Denote

D_{[k]}:=X_{[k]}\backslash X^{o}_{[k]}=\coprod_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\subset I,\\
|J|=k\end{subarray}}D_{J},

a simple normal crossing divisor on
X_{[k]}
.
The alternative sum of the Gysin maps provides a complex

\displaystyle\Omega^{\bullet}_{(X,D),{\rm BM}}:={\rm Tot}\left(\cdots\to i_{[2]\ast}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{[2]}}(\log D_{[2]})[-2]\to i_{[1]\ast}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{[1]}}(\log D_{[1]})\right).

Define the weight filtration
W_{\bullet}
by

\displaystyle W_{m}

\displaystyle:={\rm Tot}\left(\cdots\to i_{[2]\ast}W_{m-1}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{[2]}}(\log D_{[2]})(-1)[-2])\to i_{[1]\ast}W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{[1]}}(\log D_{[1]})\right)

\displaystyle={\rm Tot}\left(\cdots\to i_{[2]\ast}W_{m-1}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{[2]}}(\log D_{[2]})[-2]\to i_{[1]\ast}W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{[1]}}(\log D_{[1]})\right).

Define its Hodge filtration
F^{\bullet}
by

(2.7)

\displaystyle F^{p}

\displaystyle:={\rm Tot}\left(\cdots\to i_{[2]\ast}F^{p}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{[2]}}(\log D_{[2]})(-1)[-2])\to i_{[1]\ast}F^{p}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{[1]}}(\log D_{[1]}))\right)

\displaystyle={\rm Tot}\left(\cdots\to i_{[2]\ast}\Omega^{\geq p-1}_{X_{[2]}}(\log D_{[2]})[-2]\to i_{[1]\ast}\Omega^{\geq p}_{X_{[1]}}(\log D_{[1]})\right).

Let
D_{[k]}=\bigcup_{i\in I_{k}}D_{[k],i}
be the irreducible decomposition. For every non-empty subset
J\subset I
and integer
l>0
, denote

D_{[k],J}=\bigcap_{i\in J}D_{[k],i}\quad\textrm{and}\quad D_{[k],[l]}=\coprod_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\subset I,\\
|J|=l\end{subarray}}D_{[k],J}.

We also set
D_{[k],[0]}:=X_{[k]}
.
According to Lemma
2.5
, one observes that

(2.8)

\displaystyle{\rm Gr}^{W}_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{(X,D),{\rm BM}}

\displaystyle\simeq_{\rm qis}\bigoplus_{i\geq 0}{\rm Gr}^{W}_{m-i}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{[i+1]}}(\log D_{[i+1]})(-i)[-2i])[i]

\displaystyle\simeq_{\rm qis}\bigoplus_{i\geq 0}{\rm Gr}^{W}_{m-i}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{[i+1]}}(\log D_{[i+1]}))(-i)[-i]

\displaystyle\simeq_{\rm qis}\bigoplus_{i\geq 0}\Omega^{\bullet}_{D_{[i+1],[m-i]}}(-m)[-m].

This is a Hodge complex of sheaves with weight
m
, whose induced Hodge filtration is given by

(2.9)

\displaystyle F^{p}({\rm Gr}^{W}_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{(X,D),{\rm BM}})

\displaystyle\simeq_{\rm qis}\bigoplus_{i\geq 0}F^{p}{\rm Gr}^{W}_{m-i}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{[i+1]}}(\log D_{[i+1]})(-i)[-2i])[i]

\displaystyle\simeq_{\rm qis}\bigoplus_{i\geq 0}F^{p}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{D_{[i+1],[m-i]}}(-m)[-m])

\displaystyle\simeq_{\rm qis}\bigoplus_{i\geq 0}\Omega^{\geq p-m}_{D_{[i+1],[m-i]}}[-m].

Therefore

\displaystyle{\rm Gr}^{p}_{F}{\rm Gr}^{W}_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{(X,D),{\rm BM}}\simeq_{\rm qis}\bigoplus_{i\geq 0}\Omega^{p-m}_{D_{[i+1],[m-i]}}[-p].

Lemma 2.7
.

\left((\mathbb{D}_{X}(Rj_{!}\mathbb{Q}_{X^{o}}),\tau_{\bullet}),(\Omega^{\bullet}_{(X,D),{\rm BM}},W_{\bullet},F^{\bullet})\right)
is a mixed Hodge complex of sheaves.

Proof.

According to Proposition
2.4
,
(Rj_{\ast}\mathbb{Q}_{X^{o}_{[k]}},\tau_{[k],\bullet})\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{C}
is canonically filtered quasi-isomorphic to
(\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{[k]}}(\log D_{[k]}),W_{\bullet}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{[k]}}(\log D_{[k]}))
. Consequently,

(\mathbb{D}_{X}(Rj_{!}\mathbb{Q}_{X^{o}}),\tau_{\bullet})\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{C}

is filtered quasi-isomorphic to

(\Omega^{\bullet}_{(X,D),{\rm BM}},W_{\bullet}).

By (
2.8
) and (
2.9
),

({\rm Gr}^{W}_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{(X,D),{\rm BM}},F^{\bullet}({\rm Gr}^{W}_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{(X,D),{\rm BM}}))

is a Hodge complex of sheaves of weight
m
for any
m\in\mathbb{Z}
. It remains to show that the spectral sequence

(2.10)

\displaystyle E^{p,q}_{1}:=\mathbb{H}^{p+q}(X,{\rm Gr}^{p}_{F}{\rm Gr}^{W}_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{(X,D),{\rm BM}})\Rightarrow\mathbb{H}^{p,q}(X,{\rm Gr}^{W}_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{(X,D),{\rm BM}})

degenerates at the
E_{1}
-page for every
m
.

By (
2.8
) and (
2.9
), the spectral sequence (
2.10
) decomposes into a direct sum of spectral sequences

E_{1}^{p,q}:=H^{q-p}(X,\Omega^{p-m}_{D_{[i+1],[m-i]}})\Rightarrow H^{q-m}(X,\Omega^{\bullet}_{D_{[i+1],[m-i]}})

for
0\leq i\leq m
. Each of these spectral sequences degenerates at the
E_{1}
-page because each
D_{[i+1],[m-i]}
is a smooth projective variety. Therefore, the spectral sequence (
2.10
) degenerates at the
E_{1}
-page, completing the proof of the lemma.
∎

2.5.3.
Top Hodge piece

According to the construction of the Hodge filtration (
2.7
), the top Hodge piece is given by

(2.11)

\displaystyle F^{n}_{(X,D),{\rm BM}}:=\cdots\to i_{[2]\ast}\omega_{X_{[2]}}(D_{[2]})[-1-n]\to i_{[1]\ast}\omega_{X_{[1]}}(D_{[1]})[-n],

Lemma 2.8
.

F^{n}_{(X,D),{\rm BM}}\simeq_{\rm qis}\mathscr{O}_{X}(K_{X}+D)[-n]
.

Proof.

Denote by
\mathbb{D}^{\rm coh}_{X}(-)=Rhom_{\mathscr{O}_{X}}(-,\omega^{\bullet}_{X})
the duality operator on the derived category of coherent sheaves on
X
, where
\omega^{\bullet}_{X}\simeq_{\rm qis}\mathscr{O}_{X}(K_{X})[n]
represents the dualizing complex of
X
. Then the Grothendieck-Serre duality theorem implies that

\displaystyle F^{n}_{(X,D),{\rm BM}}\simeq_{\rm qis}

\displaystyle\cdots\to i_{[2]\ast}\omega_{X_{[2]}}(D_{[2]})[-1-n]\to i_{[1]\ast}\omega_{X_{[1]}}(D_{[1]})[-n]

\displaystyle\simeq_{\rm qis}

\displaystyle{\rm Tot}\left(\cdots\to i_{[2]\ast}\mathbb{D}^{\rm coh}_{X_{[2]}}(\mathscr{O}_{X_{[2]}}(-D_{[2]}))[-2n]\to i_{[1]\ast}\mathbb{D}^{\rm coh}_{X_{[1]}}(\mathscr{O}_{X_{[1]}}(-D_{[1]}))[-2n]\right)

\displaystyle\simeq_{\rm qis}

\displaystyle{\rm Tot}\left(\cdots\to\mathbb{D}^{\rm coh}_{X}(i_{[2]\ast}\mathscr{O}_{X_{[2]}}(-D_{[2]}))\to\mathbb{D}^{\rm coh}_{X}(i_{[1]\ast}\mathscr{O}_{X_{[1]}}(-D_{[1]}))\right)[-2n]

\displaystyle\simeq_{\rm qis}

\displaystyle\mathbb{D}^{\rm coh}_{X}\left(i_{[1]\ast}\mathscr{O}_{X_{[1]}}(-D_{[1]})\to i_{[2]\ast}\mathscr{O}_{X_{[2]}}(-D_{[2]})\to\cdots\right)[-2n]

\displaystyle\simeq_{\rm qis}

\displaystyle\mathbb{D}^{\rm coh}_{X}\left(\mathscr{O}_{X}(-D)\otimes^{L}_{\mathscr{O}_{X}}(i_{[1]\ast}\mathscr{O}_{X_{[1]}}\to i_{[2]\ast}\mathscr{O}_{X_{[2]}}\to\cdots)\right)[-2n]

\displaystyle\simeq_{\rm qis}

\displaystyle\mathbb{D}^{\rm coh}_{X}\left(\mathscr{O}_{X}(-D)\otimes^{L}_{\mathscr{O}_{X}}\mathscr{O}_{X}\right)[-2n]

\displaystyle\simeq_{\rm qis}

\displaystyle RHom_{\mathscr{O}_{X}}(\mathscr{O}_{X}(-D),\omega^{\bullet}_{X}[-n])[-n]

\displaystyle\simeq_{\rm qis}

\displaystyle\mathscr{O}_{X}(K_{X}+D)[-n].

∎

This relation is the primary motivation for considering Borel-Moore cohomology in this paper. It generalizes Fujino’s observations
[
15
,
17
]
to the sheaf-theoretic level.

2.6.
Variation of Borel-Moore cohomology

Let
f:(X,D)\to S
be a projective simple normal crossing family over a smooth base variety
S
. Suppose
\dim S=d
and
\dim X=n+d
. In this section, we establish that the local system determined by the Borel-Moore cohomology groups
H^{i}_{\rm BM}(X^{o}_{y},\mathbb{Q})
, where
X^{o}_{y}:=f^{-1}(\{y\})\cap X^{o}
, carries a variation of mixed Hodge structures. The detailed constructions will be presented in the subsequent subsections.

2.6.1.
Underlying complex of constructible sheaves

For simplicity, we denote

\mathbb{V}^{\rm BM}_{(X,D)}:=\mathbb{D}_{X}(Rj_{!}\mathbb{Q}_{X^{o}}[2n+2d])\quad\textrm{and}\quad\mathbb{V}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}}:=R^{k}f_{\ast}(\mathbb{V}^{\rm BM}_{(X,D)}).

According to (
2.4
), one obtains

(2.12)

\displaystyle\mathbb{V}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}}

\displaystyle\simeq_{\rm qis}R^{k}f_{\ast}\left({\rm Tot}\left(\cdots\to i_{[2]\ast}Rj_{\ast}\mathbb{Q}_{X^{o}_{[2]}}(-1)[-2]\to i_{[1]\ast}Rj_{\ast}\mathbb{Q}_{X^{o}_{[1]}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\simeq_{\rm qis}h^{k}{\rm Tot}\left(\cdots\to Rf_{\ast}Rj_{\ast}\mathbb{Q}_{X^{o}_{[2]}}(-1)[-2]\to Rf_{\ast}Rj_{\ast}\mathbb{Q}_{X^{o}_{[1]}}\right)

where
h^{k}
denotes the
k
-th cohomology sheaf.

Lemma 2.9
.

The sheaf
\mathbb{V}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}}
is a local system for every
k
.

Proof.

Define the stupid filtration
L^{\bullet}
of

{\rm Tot}\left(\cdots\to i_{[2]\ast}Rj_{\ast}\mathbb{Q}_{X^{o}_{[2]}}(-1)[-2]\to i_{[1]\ast}Rj_{\ast}\mathbb{Q}_{X^{o}_{[1]}}\right)

by

L^{p}={\rm Tot}\left(\cdots\to i_{[p+2]\ast}Rj_{\ast}\mathbb{Q}_{X^{o}_{[p+2]}}(-p-1)[-2p-2]\to i_{[p+1]\ast}Rj_{\ast}\mathbb{Q}_{X^{o}_{[p+1]}}(-p)[-2p]\to 0\to\cdots\to 0\right).

It gives rise to a spectral sequence

(2.13)

\displaystyle E_{1}^{p,q}=R^{p+q}f_{\ast}({\rm Gr}^{p}_{L}(\mathbb{V}^{\rm BM}_{(X,D)}))=R^{q}f_{\ast}Rj_{\ast}\mathbb{Q}_{X^{o}_{[p+1]}}\Rightarrow\mathbb{V}^{p+q}_{f,{\rm BM}}.

Since
(X_{[k]},D_{[k]})
is log smooth over
S
, the sheaf
R^{q}f_{\ast}Rj_{\ast}\mathbb{Q}_{X^{o}_{[k]}}
is a local system for every
k
and
q
. Consequently, by employing (
2.13
), we obtain the lemma.
∎

Given
k\geq 0
, the local system
\mathbb{V}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}}
induces a flat connection
(\mathcal{V}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}},\nabla)
, where
\mathcal{V}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}}:=\mathscr{O}_{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{V}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}}
and
\nabla:=d\otimes{\rm Id}
. Consider the complex
f^{-1}\Omega_{S}^{\bullet}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{V}^{\rm BM}_{(X,D)}
, equipped with a filtration
\{L^{p}\}_{p\geq 0}
, where
L^{p}:=f^{-1}\Omega_{S}^{\geq p}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{V}^{\rm BM}_{(X,D)}
. Notice that

(2.14)

\displaystyle L^{p}/L^{p+1}\simeq f^{-1}\Omega_{S}^{p}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{V}^{\rm BM}_{(X,D)}[-p].

Applying the functor
Rf_{\ast}
to this filtered complex yields a spectral sequence

\displaystyle E^{p,q}_{1}:=\Omega_{S}^{p}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}R^{q}f_{\ast}(\mathbb{V}^{\rm BM}_{(X,D)})\Rightarrow R^{p+q}f_{\ast}(f^{-1}\Omega_{S}^{\bullet}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{V}^{\rm BM}_{(X,D)}).

The morphisms of the
E_{1}
-terms give rise to

\nabla:=d\otimes{\rm Id}:\Omega_{S}^{p}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}R^{q}f_{\ast}(\mathbb{V}^{\rm BM}_{(X,D)})\to\Omega_{S}^{p+1}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}R^{q}f_{\ast}(\mathbb{V}^{\rm BM}_{(X,D)}).

In the special case when
p=0
, the morphism
E^{0,q}\to E^{1,q}
is given by

\nabla:=d\otimes{\rm Id}:\mathscr{O}_{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}R^{q}f_{\ast}(\mathbb{V}^{\rm BM}_{(X,D)})\to\Omega_{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}R^{q}f_{\ast}(\mathbb{V}^{\rm BM}_{(X,D)}),

which is the flat bundle
(\mathcal{V}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}},\nabla)
associated with
\mathbb{V}^{q}_{f,{\rm BM}}=R^{q}f_{\ast}(\mathbb{V}^{\rm BM}_{(X,D)})
via the Riemann-Hilbert correspondence.

2.6.2.
Flat connection

We introduce the complex

\Omega^{\bullet}_{(X,D),{\rm BM}}:={\rm Tot}\left(\cdots\to i_{[2]\ast}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{[2]}}(\log D_{[2]})[-2]\to i_{[1]\ast}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{[1]}}(\log D_{[1]})\right),

and define the Koszul filtration
K^{\bullet}
on it by

K^{p}={\rm Tot}\left(\cdots\to f^{\ast}\Omega^{p}_{S}\wedge i_{[2]\ast}\Omega^{\bullet-p}_{X_{[2]}}(\log D_{[2]})[-2]\to f^{\ast}\Omega^{p}_{S}\wedge i_{[1]\ast}\Omega^{\bullet-p}_{X_{[1]}}(\log D_{[1]})\right).

We define the complex of
f^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}
-modules

\Omega^{\bullet}_{f,{\rm BM}}={\rm Tot}\left(\cdots\to i_{[2]\ast}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{[2]}/S}(\log D_{[2]})(-1)[-2]\to i_{[1]\ast}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{[1]}/S}(\log D_{[1]})\right).

Then, we have

(2.15)

\displaystyle K^{p}/K^{p+1}\simeq f^{-1}\Omega_{S}^{p}\otimes_{f^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{f,{\rm BM}}[-p].

Applying the functor
Rf_{\ast}
to this filtered complex yields the spectral sequence

\displaystyle E^{p,q}_{1}:=\Omega_{S}^{p}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{S}}R^{q}f_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{f,{\rm BM}})\Rightarrow R^{p+q}f_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{(X,D),{\rm BM}}).

The morphisms of the
E_{1}
-terms give rise to the Gauss-Manin connections

\nabla_{\rm GM}:\Omega_{S}^{p}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{S}}R^{q}f_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{f,{\rm BM}})\to\Omega_{S}^{p+1}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{S}}R^{q}f_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{f,{\rm BM}}),

which are defined as the connecting morphism
\delta
in the long exact sequences

\cdots\to R^{p+q}f_{\ast}(K^{p}/K^{p+2})\to\Omega_{S}^{p}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{S}}R^{q}f_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{f,{\rm BM}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\delta}}{{\to}}\Omega_{S}^{p+1}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{S}}R^{q}f_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{f,{\rm BM}})\to\cdots

associated with the distinguished triangle

\displaystyle\small\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.31248pt\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.31248pt\hbox{$\textstyle{f^{-1}\Omega_{S}^{p}\otimes_{f^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{f,{\rm BM}}[-p]}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 276.59938pt\raise-32.5708pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.31248pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

In the special case when
p=0
, the morphism
E^{0,q}\to E^{1,q}
gives us the flat bundle

\nabla_{\rm GM}:R^{q}f_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{f,{\rm BM}})\to\Omega_{S}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{S}}R^{q}f_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{f,{\rm BM}}).

Lemma 2.10
.

There exists a canonical filtered quasi-isomorphism

\left(f^{-1}\Omega_{S}^{\bullet}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{V}^{\rm BM}_{(X,D)},L^{\bullet}\right)\simeq_{\rm qis}\left(\Omega^{\bullet}_{(X,D),{\rm BM}},K^{\bullet}\right).

In particular, there is an isomorphism between the flat connections

(\mathscr{O}_{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{V}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}},d\otimes{\rm Id})\simeq(R^{k}f_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{f,{\rm BM}}),\nabla_{\rm GM})

for every
k
.

Proof.

By (
2.4
) one may replace
\mathbb{V}^{\rm BM}_{(X,D)}
with

{\rm Tot}\left(\cdots\to i_{[2]\ast}Rj_{\ast}\mathbb{Q}_{X^{o}_{[2]}}(-1)[-2]\to i_{[1]\ast}Rj_{\ast}\mathbb{Q}_{X^{o}_{[1]}}\right).

Consequently, there exists a morphism

\mathbb{V}^{\rm BM}_{(X,D)}\to\Omega^{\bullet}_{(X,D),{\rm BM}}

induced by canonical morphisms

Rj_{\ast}\mathbb{Q}_{X^{o}_{[i]}}\to\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{[i]}}(\log D_{[i]})
(
i\geq 0
). This extends to a filtered morphism

\varphi:f^{-1}\Omega_{S}^{\bullet}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{V}^{\rm BM}_{(X,D)}\to\Omega^{\bullet}_{(X,D),{\rm BM}}

such that
\varphi(L^{k})\subset K^{k}
for all
k
.
Since
(X_{[i]},D_{[i]})\to S
is a log smooth family, there exists a quasi-isomorphism

\displaystyle f^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}Rj_{\ast}\mathbb{Q}_{X^{o}_{[i]}}\simeq\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{[i]}/S}(\log D_{[i]})

for each
i
. According to (
2.14
) and (
2.15
), this demonstrates that
\varphi
is a filtered isomorphism. This establishes the first claim of the lemma.

Furthermore, since the left (resp. right) flat connection arises from the morphism
E^{0,k}_{1}\to E^{1,k}_{1}
of the spectral sequence associated with the left (resp. right) filtered complex, the second claim follows.
∎

2.6.3.
Hodge and Weight filtrations

According to Lemma
2.10
, there exists a canonical quasi-isomorphism

(2.20)

\displaystyle f^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{V}^{\rm BM}_{(X,D)}\simeq_{\rm qis}\Omega^{\bullet}_{f,{\rm BM}}

induced by the quasi-isomorphisms

(2.21)

\displaystyle(fi_{[k]})^{-1}(\mathscr{O}_{S})\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}Rj_{\ast}\mathbb{Q}_{X^{o}_{[k]}}\simeq_{\rm qis}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{[k]}/S}(\log D_{[k]})

for all
k\geq 0
.

For every
k\geq 0
and for all
m,p\in\mathbb{Z}
, we define

\displaystyle W_{m}\Omega^{p}_{X_{[k]}/S}(\log D_{[k]})=\begin{cases}0,&m<0\\
\Omega^{p}_{X_{[k]}/S}(\log D_{[k]}),&m\geq p\\
\Omega^{p-m}_{X_{[k]}/S}\wedge\Omega^{m}_{X_{[k]}/S}(\log D_{[k]}),&0\leq m\leq p\end{cases}

and

F^{p}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{[k]}/S}(\log D_{[k]}):=\Omega^{\geq p}_{X_{[k]}/S}(\log D_{[k]}).

We define the weight filtration
W_{\bullet}
of
\Omega_{f,{\rm BM}}^{\bullet}
by

\displaystyle W_{m}\Omega_{f,{\rm BM}}^{\bullet}

\displaystyle:={\rm Tot}\left(\cdots\to i_{[2]\ast}W_{m-1}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{[2]}/S}(\log D_{[2]})(-1)[-2])\to i_{[1]\ast}W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{[1]}/S}(\log D_{[1]})\right)

\displaystyle={\rm Tot}\left(\cdots\to i_{[2]\ast}W_{m-1}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{[2]}/S}(\log D_{[2]})[-2]\to i_{[1]\ast}W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{[1]}/S}(\log D_{[1]})\right)

and the Hodge filtration
F^{\bullet}
by

\displaystyle F^{p}\Omega_{f,{\rm BM}}^{\bullet}

\displaystyle:=\sigma^{\geq p}\Omega_{f,{\rm BM}}^{\bullet}

\displaystyle={\rm Tot}\left(\cdots\to i_{[2]\ast}F^{p}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{[2]}/S}(\log D_{[2]})(-1)[-2])\to i_{[1]\ast}F^{p}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{[1]}/S}(\log D_{[1]}))\right)

\displaystyle={\rm Tot}\left(\cdots\to i_{[2]\ast}\Omega^{\geq p-1}_{X_{[2]}/S}(\log D_{[2]})[-2]\to i_{[1]\ast}\Omega^{\geq p}_{X_{[1]}/S}(\log D_{[1]})\right).

The weight filtration
\tau_{\bullet}
defined in (
2.6
) on
\mathbb{V}^{\rm BM}_{(X,D)}
induces the corresponding weight filtration
f^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\tau_{\bullet}
on
f^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{V}^{\rm BM}_{(X,D)}
.

Lemma 2.11
.

(
2.20
) provides a filtered quasi-isomorphism

\left(f^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{V}^{\rm BM}_{(X,D)},f^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\tau_{\bullet}\right)\simeq_{\rm qis}\left(\Omega^{\bullet}_{f,{\rm BM}},W_{\bullet}\right).

Proof.

The relative version of Lemma
2.7
demonstrates that (
2.21
) is a filtered quasi-isomorphism that preserves the weight filtration
(fi_{[k]})^{-1}(\mathscr{O}_{S})\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\tau_{[k],\bullet}
on the left-hand side and the weight filtration
W_{\bullet}\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{[k]}/S}(\log D_{[k]})
on the right-hand side. Consequently, this leads to the lemma.
∎

As the relative version of (
2.8
), one observes that

(2.22)

\displaystyle{\rm Gr}^{W}_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{f,{\rm BM}}

\displaystyle\simeq_{\rm qis}\bigoplus_{i\geq 0}{\rm Gr}^{W}_{m-i}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{[i+1]}/S}(\log D_{[i+1]})(-i)[-2i])[i]

\displaystyle\simeq_{\rm qis}\bigoplus_{i\geq 0}{\rm Gr}^{W}_{m-i}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{[i+1]}/S}(\log D_{[i+1]}))(-i)[-i]

\displaystyle\simeq_{\rm qis}\bigoplus_{i\geq 0}\Omega^{\bullet}_{D_{[i+1],[m-i]}/S}(-m)[-m],

with the induced Hodge filtration

(2.23)

\displaystyle F^{p}({\rm Gr}^{W}_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{f,{\rm BM}})

\displaystyle\simeq_{\rm qis}\bigoplus_{i\geq 0}F^{p}{\rm Gr}^{W}_{m-i}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{X_{[i+1]}/S}(\log D_{[i+1]})(-i)[-2i])[i]

\displaystyle\simeq_{\rm qis}\bigoplus_{i\geq 0}F^{p}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{D_{[i+1],[m-i]}/S}(-m)[-m])

\displaystyle\simeq_{\rm qis}\bigoplus_{i\geq 0}\Omega^{\geq p-m}_{D_{[i+1],[m-i]}/S}[-m].

Consequently,

\displaystyle{\rm Gr}^{p}_{F}{\rm Gr}^{W}_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{f,{\rm BM}}\simeq_{\rm qis}\bigoplus_{i\geq 0}\Omega^{p-m}_{D_{[i+1],[m-i]}/S}[-p].

According to (
2.22
), the weight filtration induces the spectral sequence

(2.24)

\displaystyle E^{-m,k+m}_{1}(Rf_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{f,{\rm BM}}),W)=R^{k}f_{\ast}({\rm Gr}_{m}^{W}\Omega^{\bullet}_{f,{\rm BM}})=\bigoplus_{i\geq 0}R^{k-m}f_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{D_{[i+1],[m-i]}/S})(-m)\Rightarrow R^{k}f_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{f,{\rm BM}}).

Since each
D_{[i+1],[m-i]}
is smooth and projective over
S
, it follows that each
R^{k-m}f_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{D_{[i+1],[m-i]}/S})(-m)
underlies a polarizable variation of Hodge structure of weight
k+m
, with the Hodge filtration given by
R^{k}f_{\ast}(F^{\bullet}(\operatorname{Gr}^{W}_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{f,\text{BM}}))
. In particular, the morphism
E^{-m,k+m}_{1}\to E^{-m+1,k+m}_{1}
on the
E_{1}
-page is strictly compatible with the Hodge filtrations. Consequently, the limit of this spectral sequence induces a weight filtration on
R^{k}f_{\ast}(\mathbb{V}^{\text{BM}}_{(X,D)})
defined by

\displaystyle W_{m}R^{k}f_{\ast}(\mathbb{V}^{\rm BM}_{(X,D)}):={\rm Im}\left(R^{k}f_{\ast}(\tau_{m-k}(\mathbb{V}^{\rm BM}_{(X,D)}))\to R^{k}f_{\ast}(\mathbb{V}^{\rm BM}_{(X,D)})\right),

and a weight filtration on
R^{k}f_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{f,{\rm BM}})
defined by

\displaystyle W_{m}R^{k}f_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{f,{\rm BM}}):={\rm Im}\left(R^{k}f_{\ast}(W_{m-k}\Omega^{\bullet}_{f,{\rm BM}})\to R^{k}f_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{f,{\rm BM}})\right).

As a consequence of Lemma (
2.11
), we have the following result:

Lemma 2.12
.

For every integer
k
, there exists a filtered quasi-isomorphism

\mathscr{O}_{S}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}(R^{k}f_{\ast}(\mathbb{V}^{{\rm BM}}_{(X,D)}),W_{\bullet}R^{k}f_{\ast}(\mathbb{V}^{{\rm BM}}_{(X,D)}))\simeq_{\rm qis}(R^{k}f_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{f,{\rm BM}}),W_{\bullet}R^{k}f_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{f,{\rm BM}})).

By the lemma on two filtrations (
[
12
]
), we obtain the following theorem.

Theorem 2.13
.

(1)

The spectral sequence (
2.24
) degenerates at the
E_{2}
-page.

(2)

The three filtrations
F_{\text{dir}}
,
F_{\text{dir}}^{\ast}
, and
F_{\text{ind}}
coincide on each page of the spectral sequence (
2.24
).

(3)

The spectral sequence

{{}_{F}}E^{p,q}_{1}=R^{p+q}f_{\ast}({\rm Gr}_{F}^{p}\Omega^{\bullet}_{f,{\rm BM}})\Rightarrow R^{p+q}f_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{f,{\rm BM}})

degenerates at
E_{1}
. In particular, the natual maps

R^{k}f_{\ast}(F^{p}\Omega^{\bullet}_{f,{\rm BM}})\to R^{k}f_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{f,{\rm BM}})

are injective, and their images, denoted by
F^{p}R^{k}f_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{f,{\rm BM}})
, coincide with
F_{\text{dir}}=F_{\text{dir}}^{\ast}=F_{\text{ind}}
. Moreover,

{\rm Gr}_{F}^{p}R^{k}f_{\ast}(F^{p}\Omega^{\bullet}_{f,{\rm BM}})\simeq R^{k}f_{\ast}({\rm Gr}_{F}^{p}\Omega^{\bullet}_{f,{\rm BM}}).

(4)

For every integer
k
, the data

\left((R^{k}f_{\ast}(\mathbb{V}^{\rm BM}_{(X,D)}),W_{\bullet}R^{k}f_{\ast}(\mathbb{V}^{\rm BM}_{(X,D)})),(R^{k}f_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{f,{\rm BM}}),W_{\bullet}R^{k}f_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{f,{\rm BM}}),F^{\bullet}R^{k}f_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{f,{\rm BM}}))\right)

forms a graded polarizable pre-variation of mixed Hodge structures.

(5)

The spectral sequence for the filtered complex

({\rm Gr}_{F}^{p}R^{k}f_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{f,{\rm BM}}),W)
degenerates at
E_{2}
.

Proof.

The proof follows the same argument as in
[
40
, Theorem 3.18]
.
∎

Theorem 2.14
.

For every integer
k
, the data

\left((R^{k}f_{\ast}(\mathbb{V}^{\rm BM}_{(X,D)}),W_{\bullet}R^{k}f_{\ast}(\mathbb{V}^{\rm BM}_{(X,D)})),(R^{k}f_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{f,{\rm BM}}),W_{\bullet}R^{k}f_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{f,{\rm BM}}),F^{\bullet}R^{k}f_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{f,{\rm BM}}))\right)

form a graded polarizable variation of mixed Hodge structures.

Remark 2.15
.

By a slight abuse of notation, the variation of mixed Hodge structures described in Theorem
2.14
is denoted by
\mathbb{V}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}}
. This is referred to as the
Borel-Moore variation of mixed Hodge structures associated with the morphism
f:(X,D)\to S
.

Proof.

According to Theorem
2.13
-(4), it suffices to show the Griffiths transversality

\nabla(F^{p}R^{k}f_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{f,{\rm BM}}))\subset F^{p-1}R^{k}f_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{f,{\rm BM}})\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{S}}\Omega_{S},\quad\forall p.

By Lemma
2.10
, it is equivalent to show

(2.25)

\displaystyle\nabla_{\rm GM}(F^{p}R^{k}f_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{f,{\rm BM}}))\subset F^{p-1}R^{k}f_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{f,{\rm BM}})\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{S}}\Omega_{S},\quad\forall p.

Recall that
\nabla_{\text{GM}}
is the connecting map of the long exact sequence associated with (
2.6.2
). By considering the long exact sequences arising from the commutative diagram of distinguished triangles

\textstyle{f^{-1}\Omega_{S}\otimes_{f^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}F^{p-1}\Omega^{\bullet}_{f,{\rm BM}}[-1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\sigma^{\geq p}(K^{0}/K^{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F^{p}\Omega^{\bullet}_{f,{\rm BM}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{f^{-1}\Omega_{S}\otimes_{f^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{f,{\rm BM}}[-1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K^{0}/K^{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Omega^{\bullet}_{f,{\rm BM}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

we obtain a commutative diagram:

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 38.20192pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\cr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-38.20192pt\raise BM}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 62.20192pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 62.20192pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Omega_{S}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{S}}R^{k}f_{\ast}(F^{p-1}\Omega^{\bullet}_{f,{\rm BM}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 71.7631pt\raise-32.99002pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 71.7631pt\raise-32.99002pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Omega_{S}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{S}}R^{k}f_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{f,{\rm BM}})}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

Combining this with Theorem
2.13
(3), we conclude that (
2.25
) holds.
∎

3.
Logarithmic extension of
\mathbb{V}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}}

In this section, we consider the logarithmic version of the Borel-Moore variation of mixed Hodge structures
\mathbb{V}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}}
for a semistable family of simple normal crossing pairs. This version plays a crucial role in establishing the admissibility of
\mathbb{V}^{k}_{f,\text{BM}}
and in the geometric construction of Deligne’s lower canonical extension of
\mathbb{V}^{k}_{f,\text{BM}}
(see Theorem
3.13
below).

Let
\overline{f}:(\overline{X},\overline{D})\to(\overline{S},D_{\overline{S}})
be a projective semistable morphism (Definition
2.2
) from a simple normal crossing pair
(\overline{X},\overline{D})
to a log smooth pair
(\overline{S},D_{\overline{S}})
. Suppose
\dim S=d
and
\dim X=n+d
. Define
S:=\overline{S}\setminus D_{\overline{S}}
and
(X,D):=(\overline{X},\overline{D})\cap f^{-1}(S)
. Consequently, the restriction
f:=\overline{f}|_{X}:(X,D)\to S
is a simple normal crossing projective family. For every integer
k
, this yields the Borel-Moore variation of mixed Hodge structures
\mathbb{V}^{k}_{f,\text{BM}}
(Remark
2.15
).

3.1.
Local monodromy

Lemma 3.1
.

For each
i\geq 1
and
q\geq 0
, the cohomology sheaf
h^{q}(Rf_{\ast}Rj_{\ast}\mathbb{Z}_{X^{o}_{[i]}})
has quasi-unipotent monodromy around every component of
D_{\overline{S}}
. The underlying local system of
\mathbb{V}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}}
admits a
\mathbb{Z}
-structure, and the local monodromy of
\mathbb{V}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}}
around every component of
D_{\overline{S}}
is quasi-unipotent.

Proof.

According to (
2.12
), the local system
\mathbb{V}^{k}_{f,\text{BM}}
has the integral structure

\mathbb{V}^{k}_{f,\text{BM},\mathbb{Z}}:=h^{k}\operatorname{Tot}\left(\cdots\to Rf_{\ast}Rj_{\ast}\mathbb{Z}_{X^{o}_{[2]}}(-1)[-2]\to Rf_{\ast}Rj_{\ast}\mathbb{Z}_{X^{o}_{[1]}}\right).

The quasi-unipotency of the local monodromies follows from the existence of this
\mathbb{Z}
-structure (cf. Borel’s theorem
[
43
, Lemma 4.5]
).
∎

3.2.
Logarithmic extension of the flat connection

We adopt the notations from §
2.4
. Specifically, we set
X^{o}:=\overline{X}\setminus\overline{D}=X\setminus D
and let
j:X^{o}\to\overline{X}
denote the open immersion. Let
\overline{X}=\bigcup_{i\in I}\overline{X}_{i}
and
\overline{D}=\bigcup_{\lambda\in\Lambda}\overline{D}_{\lambda}
be the irreducible decompositions, where
I
and
\Lambda
are finite index sets. For every non-empty subset
J\subset I
and integer
k>0
, we define

\overline{X}_{J}:=\bigcap_{i\in J}\overline{X}_{i}\quad\text{and}\quad\overline{X}_{[k]}:=\coprod_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\subset I,\\
|J|=k\end{subarray}}\overline{X}_{J}.

Let
i_{[k]}:\overline{X}_{[k]}\to\overline{X}
be the natural map. Define
X^{o}_{J}:=\overline{X}_{J}\setminus\overline{D}
for
\emptyset\neq J\subset I
, and set

X^{o}_{[k]}:=\coprod_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\subset I,\\
|J|=k\end{subarray}}X^{o}_{J},

with the natural morphism denoted by
i^{o}_{[k]}:X^{o}_{[k]}\to X^{o}
. Let
\overline{D}_{[k]}:=\overline{X}_{[k]}\setminus X^{o}
. Then, for each
k
,
(\overline{X}_{[k]},\overline{D}_{[k]})
is a log smooth pair, and the morphism
(\overline{X}_{[k]},\overline{D}_{[k]})\to(\overline{S},D_{\overline{S}})
is semistable with log smooth generic fibers. We denote by

\Omega_{\overline{X}_{[k]}/\overline{S}}(\log\overline{D}_{[k]}):=\Omega_{\overline{X}_{[k]}}(\log\overline{D}_{[k]})/f^{\ast}\Omega_{\overline{S}}(\log D_{\overline{S}})

the relative logarithmic cotangent sheaf and by
\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{X}_{[k]}/\overline{S}}(\log\overline{D}_{[k]})
the associated relative logarithmic de Rham complex.

As in §
2.6.2
,
we introduce the Koszul filtration
K^{\bullet}_{\overline{f}}
on

\Omega^{\bullet}_{(\overline{X},\overline{D}),{\rm BM}}:={\rm Tot}\left(\cdots\to i_{[2]\ast}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{X}_{[2]}}(\log\overline{D}_{[2]})[-2]\to i_{[1]\ast}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{X}_{[1]}}(\log\overline{D}_{[1]})\right),

defined by

K^{p}_{\overline{f}}={\rm Tot}\left(\cdots\to\overline{f}^{\ast}\Omega^{p}_{\overline{S}}(\log D_{\overline{S}})\wedge i_{[2]\ast}\Omega^{\bullet-p}_{\overline{X}_{[2]}}(\log\overline{D}_{[2]})[-2]\to\overline{f}^{\ast}\Omega^{p}_{\overline{S}}(\log D_{\overline{S}})\wedge i_{[1]\ast}\Omega^{\bullet-p}_{\overline{X}_{[1]}}(\log\overline{D}_{[1]})\right).

We define the complex of
\overline{f}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{\overline{S}}
-modules

\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}={\rm Tot}\left(\cdots\to i_{[2]\ast}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{X}_{[2]}/\overline{S}}(\log\overline{D}_{[2]})[-2]\to i_{[i]\ast}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{X}_{[1]}/\overline{S}}(\log\overline{D}_{[1]})\right).

Then we have

\displaystyle K^{p}_{\overline{f}}/K^{p+1}_{\overline{f}}\simeq\overline{f}^{-1}\Omega_{\overline{S}}^{p}(\log D_{\overline{S}})\otimes_{\overline{f}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{\overline{S}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}[-p].

Applying the functor
R\overline{f}_{\ast}
to this filtered complex yields the spectral sequence

\displaystyle E^{p,q}_{1}:=\Omega_{\overline{S}}^{p}(\log D_{\overline{S}})\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\overline{S}}}R^{q}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}})\Rightarrow R^{p+q}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{(\overline{X},\overline{D}),{\rm BM}}).

The morphisms of the
E_{1}
-terms give rise to the logarithmic Gauss-Manin connections

\nabla_{\rm GM}:\Omega_{\overline{S}}^{p}(\log D_{\overline{S}})\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\overline{S}}}R^{q}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}})\to\Omega_{\overline{S}}^{p+1}(\log D_{\overline{S}})\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\overline{S}}}R^{q}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}).

This connection is defined as the connecting morphism
\delta
in the long exact sequence

\cdots\to R^{p+q}\overline{f}_{\ast}(K^{p}_{\overline{f}}/K^{p+2}_{\overline{f}})\to\Omega_{\overline{S}}^{p}(\log D_{\overline{S}})\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\overline{S}}}R^{q}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\delta}}{{\to}}\Omega_{\overline{S}}^{p+1}(\log D_{\overline{S}})\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\overline{S}}}R^{q}f_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}})\to\cdots

which is associated with the distinguished triangle

\displaystyle{\small\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.31248pt\hbox{\kern 77.5522pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&\cr&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-29.10294pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.31248pt\hbox{$\textstyle{K^{p+1}_{\overline{f}}/K^{p+2}_{\overline{f}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-16.2854pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.575pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\simeq}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-23.99998pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 242.2169pt\raise-16.2854pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.575pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\simeq}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.31248pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-77.5522pt\raise-32.5708pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.31248pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\overline{f}^{-1}\Omega_{\overline{S}}^{p+1}(\log D_{\overline{S}})\otimes_{\overline{f}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{\overline{S}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}[-p-1]}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 123.26057pt\raise-32.5708pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.31248pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 174.96895pt\raise-32.5708pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.31248pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\overline{f}^{-1}\Omega_{\overline{S}}^{p}(\log D_{\overline{S}})\otimes_{\overline{f}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{\overline{S}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}[-p]}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 333.46486pt\raise-32.5708pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.31248pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces}.

In the special case when
p=0
, the morphism
E^{0,q}\to E^{1,q}
yields the logarithmic flat connection

\nabla_{\rm GM}:R^{q}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}})\to\Omega_{\overline{S}}(\log D_{\overline{S}})\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\overline{S}}}R^{q}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}).

We will demonstrate in Proposition
3.2
that
R^{q}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},\text{BM}})
is locally free for every
q
.

3.3.
Logarithmic Hodge and Weight filtrations

As in §
2.6.3
, we formally define the logarithmic weight filtration and Hodge filtration on
\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}
. Let
E:=\overline{f}^{-1}(D_{\overline{S}})
denote the preimage of
D_{\overline{S}}
under the map
\overline{f}
. Then
E_{[k]}:=i_{[k]}^{-1}(E)
is a simple normal crossing divisor on
\overline{X}_{[k]}
. For every non-negative integer
k
and integers
m,p\in\mathbb{Z}
, we establish the following definitions:

\displaystyle W_{m}\Omega^{p}_{\overline{X}_{[k]}/\overline{S}}(\log\overline{D}_{[k]})=\begin{cases}0,&m<0\\
\Omega^{p}_{\overline{X}_{[k]}/\overline{S}}(\log E_{[k]}),&m\geq p\\
\Omega_{\overline{X}_{[k]}/\overline{S}}^{p-m}(\log E_{[k]})\wedge\Omega^{m}_{\overline{X}_{[k]}/\overline{S}}(\log\overline{D}_{[k]}),&0\leq m\leq p\end{cases}

and set

F^{p}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{X}_{[k]}/\overline{S}}(\log\overline{D}_{[k]}):=\Omega^{\geq p}_{\overline{X}_{[k]}/\overline{S}}(\log\overline{D}_{[k]}).

We define the weight filtration
W_{\bullet}
of
\Omega_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}^{\bullet}
as follows:

(3.1)

\displaystyle W_{m}\Omega_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}^{\bullet}

\displaystyle:={\rm Tot}\left(\cdots\to W_{m-1}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{X}_{[2]}/\overline{S}}(\log\overline{D}_{[2]})[-2]\to W_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{X}_{[1]}/\overline{S}}(\log\overline{D}_{[1]})\right).

Additionally, we define its logarithmic Hodge filtration
F^{\bullet}
by

(3.2)

\displaystyle F^{p}\Omega_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}^{\bullet}

\displaystyle:=\sigma^{\geq p}\Omega_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}^{\bullet}={\rm Tot}\left(\cdots\to\Omega^{\geq p-1}_{\overline{X}_{[2]}/\overline{S}}(\log\overline{D}_{[2]})[-2]\to\Omega^{\geq p}_{\overline{X}_{[1]}/\overline{S}}(\log\overline{D}_{[1]})\right).

In particular, the top Hodge complex is given by

(3.3)

\displaystyle F^{n}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}

\displaystyle\simeq{\rm Tot}\left(\cdots\to\omega_{\overline{X}_{[2]}/\overline{S}}(\log\overline{D}_{[2]})[-1-n]\to\omega_{\overline{X}_{[1]}/\overline{S}}(\log\overline{D}_{[1]})[-n]\right)

\displaystyle\simeq F^{n+d}\Omega^{\bullet}_{(\overline{X},\overline{D}),{\rm BM}}[d]\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\overline{X}}}(\overline{f}^{\ast}\omega_{\overline{S}}(D_{\overline{S}}))^{-1}\quad(\ref{align_top_Hodge_ab})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathscr{O}_{\overline{X}}(K_{\overline{X}}+\overline{D})[-n]\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\overline{X}}}(\overline{f}^{\ast}\omega_{\overline{S}}(D_{\overline{S}}))^{-1}\quad\textrm{(Lemma \ref{lem_top_Hodge_BM})}

\displaystyle\simeq\mathscr{O}_{\overline{X}}(K_{\overline{X}/\overline{S}}+\overline{D}-\overline{f}^{\ast}(D_{\overline{S}}))[-n].

These subcomplexes induce the (logarithmic) Hodge filtration

\displaystyle F^{p}R^{k}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}^{\bullet}):={\rm Im}\left(R^{k}\overline{f}_{\ast}(F^{p}\Omega_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}^{\bullet})\to R^{k}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}^{\bullet})\right),\quad p\geq 0

and the weight filtration

\displaystyle W_{m}R^{k}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}^{\bullet}):={\rm Im}\left(R^{k}\overline{f}_{\ast}(W_{m-k}\Omega_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}^{\bullet})\to R^{k}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}^{\bullet})\right),\quad m\in\mathbb{Z}.

Let
\overline{D}^{h}_{[k]}
denote the horizontal part of
\overline{D}_{[k]}
, and let
\overline{D}^{h}_{[k]}=\bigcup_{i\in I_{k}}\overline{D}_{[k],i}
be its irreducible decomposition. For every non-empty subset
\emptyset\neq J\subset I_{k}
and positive integer
l>0
, we define

\overline{D}_{[k],J}:=\bigcap_{i\in J}\overline{D}_{[k],i}\quad\text{and}\quad\overline{D}_{[k],[l]}:=\coprod_{\begin{subarray}{c}J\subset I_{k},\\
|J|=l\end{subarray}}\overline{D}_{[k],J}.

Additionally, we set
\overline{D}_{[k],[0]}:=\overline{X}_{[k]}
. Let
E_{[k],[l]}:=\overline{D}_{[k],[l]}\cap E
. Then, each morphism
(\overline{D}_{[k],[l]},E_{[k],[l]})\to(\overline{S},D_{\overline{S}})
forms a semistable family with smooth general fibers.
Similar to (
2.22
) and (
2.23
), we observe that

(3.4)

\displaystyle{\rm Gr}^{W}_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}

\displaystyle\simeq_{\rm qis}\bigoplus_{i\geq 0}{\rm Gr}^{W}_{m-i}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{X}_{[i+1]}/\overline{S}}(\log\overline{D}_{[i+1]})(-i)[-2i])[i]

\displaystyle\simeq_{\rm qis}\bigoplus_{i\geq 0}{\rm Gr}^{W}_{m-i}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{X}_{[i+1]}/\overline{S}}(\log\overline{D}_{[i+1]}))(-i)[-i]

\displaystyle\simeq_{\rm qis}\bigoplus_{i\geq 0}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{D}_{[i+1],[m-i]}/\overline{S}}(\log E_{[i+1],[m-i]})(-m)[-m],

with the induced Hodge filtration given by

\displaystyle F^{p}({\rm Gr}^{W}_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}})

\displaystyle\simeq_{\rm qis}\bigoplus_{i\geq 0}F^{p}{\rm Gr}^{W}_{m-i}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{X}_{[i+1]}/\overline{S}}(\log\overline{D}_{[i+1]})(-i)[-2i])[i]

\displaystyle\simeq_{\rm qis}\bigoplus_{i\geq 0}F^{p}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{D}_{[i+1],[m-i]}/\overline{S}}(\log E_{[i+1],[m-i]})(-m)[-m])

\displaystyle\simeq_{\rm qis}\bigoplus_{i\geq 0}\Omega^{\geq p-m}_{\overline{D}_{[i+1],[m-i]}/\overline{S}}(\log E_{[i+1],[m-i]})[-m].

Therefore

(3.5)

\displaystyle{\rm Gr}^{p}_{F}{\rm Gr}^{W}_{m}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}\simeq_{\rm qis}\bigoplus_{i\geq 0}\Omega^{p-m}_{\overline{D}_{[i+1],[m-i]}/\overline{S}}(\log E_{[i+1],[m-i]})[-p].

3.4.
The logarithmic weight spectral sequence

Recall that for a flat connection
(\mathcal{V},\nabla)
on
S
, the lower canonical extension of
(\mathcal{V},\nabla)
to
\overline{S}
is a logarithmic connection

\nabla:\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\overline{S}}}\Omega_{\overline{S}}(\log D_{\overline{S}})

that extends
(\mathcal{V},\nabla)
such that the real parts of the eigenvalues of the residue maps along
D_{\overline{S}}
lie in the interval
[0,1)
. According to
[
9
, Proposition 5.4]
, the lower canonical extension always exists and is unique up to isomorphism.

According to (
3.4
), the weight filtration
W_{\bullet}
(
3.1
) on
\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}
induces a spectral sequence

(3.6)

\displaystyle E^{-m,k+m}_{1}(R\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}),W):=\bigoplus_{i\geq 0}R^{k-m}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{D}_{[i+1],[m-i]}/\overline{S}}(\log E_{[i+1],[m-i]}))(-m)\Rightarrow R^{k}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}).

Proposition 3.2
.

The Gauss-Manin connection

\nabla_{\rm GM}:R^{q}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}})\to\Omega_{\overline{S}}(\log D_{\overline{S}})\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\overline{S}}}R^{q}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}).

is isomorphic to Deligne’s lower canonical extension of

\nabla_{\rm GM}:R^{q}f_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{f,{\rm BM}})\to\Omega_{S}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{S}}R^{q}f_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{f,{\rm BM}}).

In particular, each
R^{q}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}})
is locally free. Moreover, the weight spectral sequence (
3.6
) degenerates at the
E_{2}
-page. Consequently, one has

E^{-m,k+m}_{2}(R\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}),W)\simeq{\rm Gr}_{k+m}^{W}R^{k}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}})

and
{\rm Gr}_{m}^{W}R^{k}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}})
,
W_{m}R^{k}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}})
are locally free for every
k
and
m
.

Proof.

For every
k>0
and
l\geq 0
, note that since
(\overline{D}_{[k],[l]},E_{[k],[l]})\to(\overline{S},D_{\overline{S}})
is a semistable family extending the smooth family
D_{[k],[l]}\to S
, it follows from
[
46
, Proposition 2.20]
that

(R^{q}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{D}_{[k],[l]}/\overline{S}}(\log E_{[k],[l]})),\nabla_{\text{GM}})

is the lower canonical extension of

(R^{q}f_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{D_{[k],[l]}/S}),\nabla_{\text{GM}})

for every
q
.
Consequently, in the spectral sequence (
3.6
),

E^{-m,k+m}_{1}(R\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}),W):=\bigoplus_{i\geq 0}R^{k-m}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{D}_{[i+1],[m-i]}/\overline{S}}(\log E_{[i+1],[m-i]}))

is the lower canonical extension of

E^{-m,k+m}_{1}(Rf_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{f,{\rm BM}}),W):=\bigoplus_{i\geq 0}R^{k-m}f_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{D_{[i+1],[m-i]}/S}).

Since taking the lower canonical extension is an exact functor
[
9
, Proposition 5.4(ii)]
, it follows that

E_{2}^{-m,k+m}(R\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}),W)

is the lower canonical extension of

E^{-m,k+m}_{2}(Rf_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{f,{\rm BM}}),W)

for every
k
and
m
.
In particular, this implies that
E_{2}^{-m,k+m}(R\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}),W)
is locally free for every
k
and
m
. Once the local freeness is established, the spectral sequence (
3.6
) degenerates at the
E_{2}
-page because its restriction to
S
degenerates at the
E_{2}
-page (Theorem
2.13
(1)). Consequently,

R^{k}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}})\simeq\bigoplus_{m}E_{2}^{-m,k+m}(R\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}),W)

is locally free and is the lower canonical extension of

R^{k}f_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{f,{\rm BM}})\simeq\bigoplus_{m}E_{2}^{-m,k+m}(Rf_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{f,{\rm BM}}),W)

for every
k
. The local freeness of
{\rm Gr}_{m}^{W}R^{k}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}})
follows from the
E_{2}
-degeneration and the local freeness of
R^{k}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}})
.

Thus, the proposition is proved.
∎

3.5.
The logarithmic Hodge spectral sequence

The logarithmic Hodge filtration
F^{\bullet}
(
3.2
) on
\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}
induces a spectral sequence

(3.7)

\displaystyle E^{p,q}_{1}:=R^{p+q}\overline{f}_{\ast}({\rm Gr}_{F}^{p}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}})\Rightarrow R^{p+q}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}).

The main result of this section is the
E_{1}
-degeneration of this spectral sequence (Proposition
3.5
). Prior to presenting the proof of this main result, we shall review the theory of the degeneration of variations of Hodge structures in the geometric context, which was developed by Steenbrink
[
46
,
44
]
and further extended by Qianyu Chen
[
7
]
.

Let
Y\to\Delta
be a projective morphism from a complex manifold
Y
to the unit disc
\Delta
, with
0
as its unique critical value. Suppose that the schematic fiber
Y_{0}:=f^{-1}\{0\}
is a (possibly non-reduced) simple normal crossing divisor on
Y
. Denote
Y^{\ast}=Y\setminus Y_{0}
. Then,
R^{k}f_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{Y^{\ast}/\Delta^{\ast}})
underlies a variation of Hodge structure of weight
k
. Furthermore,
(R^{k}f_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{Y/\Delta}(\log Y_{0})),\nabla_{\rm GM})
is isomorphic to the lower canonical extension of
(R^{k}f_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{Y^{\ast}/\Delta^{\ast}}),\nabla_{\rm GM})
. The logarithmic Hodge spectral sequence

\displaystyle E_{1}^{p,q}:=R^{q}f_{\ast}(\Omega^{p}_{Y/\Delta}(\log Y_{0}))\Rightarrow R^{p+q}f_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{Y/\Delta}(\log Y_{0}))

degenerates at
E_{1}
. Moreover,
R^{q}f_{\ast}(\Omega^{p}_{Y/\Delta}(\log Y_{0}))
is locally free for all
p
and
q
. The canonical morphism

F^{p}:=R^{k}f_{\ast}(\Omega^{\geq p}_{Y/\Delta}(\log Y_{0}))\to R^{k}f_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{Y/\Delta}(\log Y_{0}))

is injective for every
k
, thereby defining a filtration
F^{\bullet}
on
R^{k}f_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{Y/\Delta}(\log Y_{0}))
consisting of subbundles with locally free subquotients.

Let
\mathcal{V}:=R^{k}f_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{Y/\Delta}(\log Y_{0}))
for simplicity. Denote by
R:\mathcal{V}(0)\to\mathcal{V}(0)
the residue operator associated with
\nabla_{\rm GM}
. Let
R=P+N
be the Jordan decomposition, where
P
is a semisimple operator and
N
is nilpotent. Since the monodromy of
\nabla_{\rm GM}
is quasi-unipotent, the eigenvalues of
P
lie in
[0,1)\cap\mathbb{Q}
. The nilpotent operator
N
defines the monodromy weight filtration
W_{\bullet}
on
\mathcal{V}(0)
, which satisfies the following two properties:

•

N(W_{i})\subset W_{i-2}
for every
i
;

•

N^{l}:{\rm Gr}^{W}_{k+l}\mathcal{V}(0)\to{\rm Gr}^{W}_{k-l}\mathcal{V}(0)
is an isomorphism for every
l
.

Let
F^{\bullet}(0)
denote the filtration on
\mathcal{V}(0)
induced by
F^{\bullet}
. When the monodromy operator of
\nabla_{\rm GM}
is unipotent (i.e.,
P=0
),
(\mathcal{V}(0),W_{\bullet},F^{\bullet}(0))
forms a mixed Hodge structure (
[
46
]
). However, in the general case (i.e.,
P\neq 0
), the situation becomes more intricate.

Let
0\leq\lambda_{1}<\lambda_{2}<\cdots<\lambda_{m}<1
be the eigenvalues of
P
, which induce a filtration of logarithmic sub-connections:

0=\mathcal{V}_{0}\subset\mathcal{V}_{1}\subset\cdots\subset\mathcal{V}_{m}=\mathcal{V}.

Here,
\mathcal{V}_{i}
is the submodule of
\mathcal{V}
spanned by the generalized eigenspaces
\ker(R-\lambda)^{\infty}
for
\lambda\geq\lambda_{i}
. Let
W_{\bullet,i}
and
F^{\bullet}_{i}
denote the induced filtrations on
\mathcal{V}_{i}(0)/\mathcal{V}_{i-1}(0)
, defined as follows:

W_{m,i}=\frac{W_{m}\cap\mathcal{V}_{i}(0)+\mathcal{V}_{i-1}(0)}{\mathcal{V}_{i-1}(0)},\quad\forall m,

F^{p}_{i}=\frac{F^{p}\cap\mathcal{V}_{i}(0)+\mathcal{V}_{i-1}(0)}{\mathcal{V}_{i-1}(0)},\quad\forall p.

Theorem 3.3
.

[
7
, Theorem A]

For every
i=1,\dots,m
,
(\mathcal{V}_{i}(0)/\mathcal{V}_{i-1}(0),W_{\bullet,i},F^{\bullet}_{i})
forms a mixed Hodge structure.

The following lemma will be used in proving Proposition
3.5
.

Lemma 3.4
.

Let
(V,V_{\bullet},F^{\bullet}_{V})
and
(W,W_{\bullet},F^{\bullet}_{W})
be two bi-filtered vector spaces, where
V_{\bullet}
and
W_{\bullet}
are regular increasing filtrations on
V
and
W
, respectively, and
F^{\bullet}_{V}
and
F^{\bullet}_{W}
are regular decreasing filtrations on
V
and
W
, respectively. Let
\varphi:V\to W
be a linear operator that preserves both filtrations. For each
i
, let
F^{\bullet}(V_{i}/V_{i-1})
denote the filtration induced on
V_{i}/V_{i-1}
, defined as

F^{p}(V_{i}/V_{i-1})=\frac{F^{p}_{V}\cap V_{i}+V_{i-1}}{V_{i-1}},\quad\forall p.

Similarly, define
F^{\bullet}(W_{i}/W_{i-1})
for the filtration induced on
W_{i}/W_{i-1}
. Assume that the induced operator

\varphi_{i}:=\varphi\!\!\!\!\mod V_{i-1}:V_{i}/V_{i-1}\to W_{i}/W_{i-1}

is strictly compatible with
F^{\bullet}(V_{i}/V_{i-1})
and
F^{\bullet}(W_{i}/W_{i-1})
for all
i=1,\dots,m
. Then,
\varphi
is strictly compatible with
F^{\bullet}_{V}
and
F^{\bullet}_{W}
.

Proof.

Our goal is to demonstrate that
F^{p}_{W}\cap\mathrm{Im}(\varphi)\subset\varphi(F^{p}_{V})
for every
p
. Let
a<b
be integers such that
V_{b}=V
,
V_{a}=0
,
W_{b}=W
, and
W_{a}=0
. By assumption, we have

F^{p}(W_{i}/W_{i-1})\cap\mathrm{Im}(\varphi_{i})\subset\varphi(F^{p}(V_{i}/V_{i-1}))

for every
i
. This implies that

(3.8)

\displaystyle F^{p}_{W}\cap\mathrm{Im}(\varphi)\cap W_{i}\subset\varphi(F^{p}_{V})+W_{i-1},\quad\forall i=a,\dots,b.

Let
x\in V
such that
\varphi(x)\in F^{p}_{W}
. We claim that for each
a\leq m\leq b
, there exists
x_{m}\in F^{p}_{V}
such that
\varphi(x-x_{m})\in W_{m-1}
. We prove this claim by induction. The base case
m=b
follows from (
3.8
) by taking
i=b
. Now suppose that there exists
x_{m+1}\in F^{p}_{V}
such that
\varphi(x-x_{m+1})\in W_{m}
. Then
\varphi(x-x_{m+1})\in F^{p}_{W}\cap\mathrm{Im}(\varphi)\cap W_{m}
. According to (
3.8
) (taking
i=m
), there exists
y\in F^{p}_{V}
such that
\varphi(x-x_{m+1})-\varphi(y)\in W_{m-1}
. By letting
x_{m}=x_{m+1}+y
, the claim follows.

As a consequence, there exists
x_{a+1}\in F^{p}_{V}
such that
\varphi(x)-\varphi(x_{a+1})\in W_{a}=0
. Therefore,
\varphi(x)\in\varphi(F^{p}_{V})
. This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Proposition 3.5
.

The spectral sequence (
3.7
) degenerates at the
E_{1}
-page. Moreover, for every
p
,
m
, and
k
, both

{\rm Gr}^{k}_{F}{\rm Gr}^{W}_{-m}R^{k}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}})

and

R^{k}\overline{f}_{\ast}(F^{p}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}})\simeq F^{p}R^{k}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}})

are locally free.

Proof.

Consider the morphism of the
E_{1}
-page of the weight spectral sequence (
3.6
):

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 65.08235pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-60.01984pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{E^{-m,k+m}_{1}(R\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}),W)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-16.49501pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.93056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{d_{1}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 78.37402pt\raise 4.72855pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.72855pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\simeq}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 108.97823pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 108.97823pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bigoplus_{i\geq 0}R^{k-m}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{D}_{[i+1],[m-i]}/\overline{S}}(\log
