70.95735pt\raise-28.26147pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.72855pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\simeq}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 89.08235pt\raise-32.99002pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 89.08235pt\raise-32.99002pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\bigoplus_{i\geq 0}R^{k-m+2}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{D}_{[i+1],[m-i-1]}/\overline{S}}(\log E_{[i+1],[m-i-1]}))(-m+1)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

By the lemma on two filtrations (
[
11
]
, see also
[
40
, Theorem 3.12]
),
d_{1}
preserves the filtration
F
, as
F_{\rm rec}=F_{d}=F_{d^{\ast}}
on the
E_{1}
-terms in general, and
F_{\rm rec}
on
E^{-m,k+m}_{1}(R\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}),W)
coincides with the logarithmic Hodge filtrations on

\bigoplus_{i\geq 0}R^{k-m}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{D}_{[i+1],[m-i]}/\overline{S}}(\log E_{[i+1],[m-i]}))(-m).

We aim to demonstrate that the restriction of (
3.5
) to each point
s\in\overline{S}
,

(3.13)

\displaystyle d_{1}(s):

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{i\geq 0}R^{k-m}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{D}_{[i+1],[m-i]}/\overline{S}}(\log E_{[i+1],[m-i]}))\otimes\mathbb{C}(s)

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{i\geq 0}R^{k-m+2}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{D}_{[i+1],[m-i-1]}/\overline{S}}(\log E_{[i+1],[m-i-1]}))\otimes\mathbb{C}(s),

is strictly compatible with the logarithmic Hodge filtrations.
Since
d_{1}|_{S}
is a morphism between variations of Hodge structures, it follows that
d_{1}(s)
is strictly compatible with the logarithmic Hodge filtration for any
s\in S
.

Now, let us consider a point
s\in\overline{S}\setminus S
. Consider those
k
and
l
such that
s\in\overline{f}(E_{[k],[l]})
. Then
(R^{q}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{D}_{[k],[l]}/\overline{S}}(\log E_{[k],[l]})),\nabla_{\rm GM})
is a logarithmic connection whose eigenvalues of the residue map
R_{s}
at
s
lie in
[0,1)\cap\mathbb{Q}
. Denote by

\mathcal{V}^{q}_{[k],[l]}:=R^{q}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{D}_{[k],[l]}/\overline{S}}(\log E_{[k],[l]}))

and
\mathcal{V}^{q}_{[k],[l]}(s)=\mathcal{V}^{q}_{[k],[l]}\otimes\mathbb{C}(s)
. Let

\{F^{p}:=R^{q}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\geq p}_{\overline{D}_{[k],[l]}/\overline{S}}(\log E_{[k],[l]}))\}

be the logarithmic Hodge filtration on
\mathcal{V}^{q}_{[k],[l]}
, which induces a filtration
\{F^{p}(s):=F^{p}\otimes\mathbb{C}(s)\}
on
\mathcal{V}^{q}_{[k],[l]}(s)
. Let
W_{\bullet}
be the monodromy weight filtration on
\mathcal{V}^{q}_{[k],[l]}(s)
associated with the nilpotent part of
R_{s}
.

Let

0\leq\lambda_{1}<\lambda_{2}<\cdots<\lambda_{m}<1

be the eigenvalues of
R_{s}:\mathcal{V}^{q}_{[k],[l]}(s)\to\mathcal{V}^{q}_{[k],[l]}(s)
. They induce a filtration of logarithmic sub-connections:

0=\mathcal{V}_{0}\subset\mathcal{V}_{1}\subset\cdots\subset\mathcal{V}_{m}=\mathcal{V}^{q}_{[k],[l]}(s).

Here,
\mathcal{V}_{i}
is the linear subspace of
\mathcal{V}^{q}_{[k],[l]}(s)
spanned by the generalized eigenspaces
\ker(R_{s}-\lambda)^{\infty}
for
\lambda\geq\lambda_{i}
. Let
W_{\bullet}(\mathcal{V}_{i}/\mathcal{V}_{i-1})
and
F^{\bullet}(\mathcal{V}_{i}/\mathcal{V}_{i-1})
denote the induced filtrations on
\mathcal{V}_{i}/\mathcal{V}_{i-1}
, defined as follows:

W_{m}(\mathcal{V}_{i}/\mathcal{V}_{i-1})=\frac{W_{m}\cap\mathcal{V}_{i}+\mathcal{V}_{i-1}}{\mathcal{V}_{i-1}},\quad\forall m,

F^{p}(\mathcal{V}_{i}/\mathcal{V}_{i-1})=\frac{F^{p}(s)\cap\mathcal{V}_{i}+\mathcal{V}_{i-1}}{\mathcal{V}_{i-1}},\quad\forall p.

According to Theorem
3.3
, the triple

\left(\mathcal{V}_{i}/\mathcal{V}_{i-1},W_{\bullet}(\mathcal{V}_{i}/\mathcal{V}_{i-1}),F^{\bullet}(\mathcal{V}_{i}/\mathcal{V}_{i-1})\right)

forms a mixed Hodge structure for every
i=1,\dots,m
.

Now let us analyze (
3.13
). Since
d_{1}
admits a rational structure:

\displaystyle d_{1}:\bigoplus_{i\geq 0}R^{k-m}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\psi_{[i+1],[m-i]})\to\bigoplus_{i\geq 0}R^{k-m+2}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\psi_{[i+1],[m-i-1]}),

where
\psi_{[i+1],[m-i]}
denotes the nearby cycle complex associated with the semistable morphism
(\overline{D}_{[i+1],[m-i]},E_{[i+1],[m-i]})\to(\overline{S},D_{\overline{S}})
at
s
, it follows that
d_{1}
commutes with the monodromy operators. Consequently,
d_{1}(s)
preserves both the generalized eigenspaces of the residue maps at
s
and the monodromy weight filtrations. Let

0\leq\lambda_{1}<\lambda_{2}<\cdots<\lambda_{m}<1

be the union of eigenvalues of the residue maps (on the source and target of
d_{1}(s)
). Define

0=\mathcal{V}_{0}\subset\mathcal{V}_{1}\subset\cdots\subset\mathcal{V}_{m}=\bigoplus_{i\geq 0}R^{k-m}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{D}_{[i+1],[m-i]}/\overline{S}}(\log E_{[i+1],[m-i]}))\otimes\mathbb{C}(s),

where
\mathcal{V}_{i}
is the submodule spanned by the generalized eigenspaces
\ker(R-\lambda)^{\infty}
for
\lambda\geq\lambda_{i}
, where
R
is the direct sum of residue maps. Similarly, one obtains the filtration

0=\mathcal{V}^{\prime}_{0}\subset\mathcal{V}^{\prime}_{1}\subset\cdots\subset\mathcal{V}^{\prime}_{m}=\bigoplus_{i\geq 0}R^{k-m+2}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{D}_{[i+1],[m-i-1]}/\overline{S}}(\log E_{[i+1],[m-i-1]}))\otimes\mathbb{C}(s),

where
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}_{i}
is the submodule spanned by the generalized eigenspaces
\ker(R-\lambda)^{\infty}
for
\lambda\geq\lambda_{i}
. Then

d_{1}(s)(\mathcal{V}_{i})\subset\mathcal{V}^{\prime}_{i},\quad i=1,\dots,m,

and

d_{1}(s)\!\!\!\!\mod\mathcal{V}_{i-1}:\frac{\mathcal{V}_{i}}{\mathcal{V}_{i-1}}\to\frac{\mathcal{V}^{\prime}_{i}}{\mathcal{V}^{\prime}_{i-1}}

preserves the monodromy weight filtrations and the logarithmic Hodge filtrations. Hence,
d_{1}(s)\!\!\!\mod\mathcal{V}_{i-1}
defines a morphism between mixed Hodge structures. Therefore,
d_{1}(s)\!\!\!\mod\mathcal{V}_{i-1}
is strictly compatible with the logarithmic Hodge filtrations for every
i=1,\dots,m
. By Lemma
3.4
, we conclude that
d_{1}(s)
is strictly compatible with the logarithmic Hodge filtrations.

This demonstrates that
d_{1}(s)
is strictly compatible with the logarithmic Hodge filtration for every
s\in\overline{S}
. By applying
[
15
, Lemma 3.4(iv)]
to the filtered complex

\left(E^{\bullet,k+m}_{1}(R\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}),W),F_{\rm rec}\right),

it follows that

{\rm Gr}^{p}_{F_{\rm rec}}E_{2}^{-m,k+m}(R\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}),W)

is locally free for all
p
,
k
, and
m
, and that
F_{\rm rec}=F_{d}=F_{d^{\ast}}
holds on
E_{2}^{-m,k+m}(R\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}),W)
(see
[
40
, Theorem 3.12]
). Furthermore, the morphism (
3.5
) is strictly compatible with
F_{\rm rec}
(see
[
15
, Lemma 3.4(iv)]
).
Therefore, by Proposition
3.2
, it follows that

{\rm Gr}^{p}_{F_{\rm rec}}E_{2}^{-k,k+m}(R\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}),W)\simeq{\rm Gr}^{p}_{F_{\rm rec}}{\rm Gr}^{W}_{k+m}R^{k}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}})

is locally free for all
p
,
q
, and
k
.

Since the spectral sequence (
3.6
) degenerates at the
E_{2}
-page (Proposition
3.2
) and the morphism of the
E_{1}
-page is strictly compatible with
F_{\rm rec}
, it follows from the lemma on two filtrations that the spectral sequence (
3.7
) degenerates at the
E_{1}
-page. Consequently,

R^{k}\overline{f}_{\ast}(F^{p}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}})\simeq F^{p}R^{k}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}})

is locally free for all
p
and
k
. Furthermore,
{\rm Gr}^{p}_{F}R^{k}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}})
is locally free for all
p
and
k
, as a result of the
E_{1}
-degeneration and the local freeness of
R^{k}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}})
(Proposition
3.2
).
∎

Lemma 3.6
.

The logarithmic Hodge filtration
F^{\bullet}R^{k}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},\rm BM})
satisfies the Griffiths tranversality:

\nabla_{\rm GM}(F^{p}R^{k}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},\rm BM}))\subset F^{p-1}R^{k}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},\rm BM})\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\overline{S}}}\Omega_{\overline{S}}(\log D_{\overline{S}}),\quad\forall p.

Proof.

By considering the long exact sequences arising from the commutative diagram

\textstyle{\overline{f}^{-1}\Omega_{\overline{S}}(\log D_{\overline{S}})\otimes_{\overline{f}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{\overline{S}}}F^{p-1}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}[-1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\sigma^{\geq p}(K^{0}_{\overline{f}}/K^{2}_{\overline{f}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F^{p}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\overline{f}^{-1}\Omega_{\overline{S}}(\log D_{\overline{S}})\otimes_{\overline{f}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{\overline{S}}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}[-1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K^{0}_{\overline{f}}/K^{2}_{\overline{f}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

we obtain a commutative diagram:

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 38.08905pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\cr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-38.08905pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{R^{k}\overline{f}_{\ast}(F^{p}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 62.08905pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Omega_{\overline{S}}(\log D_{\overline{S}})\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\overline{S}}}R^{k}\overline{f}_{\ast}(F^{p-1}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm By Proposition
3.5
, we establish the assertion of the lemma.
∎

3.6.
Relative monodromy weight filtration

In this section, we assume that
\overline{S}=\Delta
is the unit disc with a coordinate
t
, and
D_{\overline{S}}=\{t=0\}
. By Lemma
3.1
, one can always achieve unipotent monodromy by performing a base change via a finite map
\Delta\to\Delta
,
z\mapsto z^{m}
for some
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
. Specifically, the following condition holds:

(UM):
For each
k\geq 1
,
l\geq 0
and
q\geq 0
, the local system
h^{q}(Rf_{\ast}Rj_{\ast}\mathbb{Z}_{D_{[k],[l]}})
has unipotent monodromy around
0
.

Henceforth, we assume that condition
(UM)
holds throughout this section. The main result of this section is the existence of the relative monodromy weight filtration (Theorem
3.12
).

3.6.1.
The residue map

Recall that
E:=\overline{f}^{-1}(0)
. The canonical morphism

\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}\to\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\overline{X}}}\mathscr{O}_{E_{\rm red}}

induces a morphism:

\displaystyle\tau_{0}:R\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}})\otimes\mathbb{C}(0)\to R\Gamma(E_{\rm red},\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\overline{X}}}\mathscr{O}_{E_{\rm red}}).

Lemma 3.7
.

\tau_{0}
is a quasi-isomorphism.

Proof.

The weight filtration
W_{\bullet}
(
3.1
) on
\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}
induces filtrations (still denoted by
W_{\bullet}
) on
\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\overline{X}}}\mathscr{O}_{E}
and
\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\overline{X}}}\mathscr{O}_{E_{\rm red}}
, respectively. The restriction gives a filtered morphism:

(3.14)

\displaystyle\tau:(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\overline{X}}}\mathscr{O}_{E},W_{\bullet})\to(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\overline{X}}}\mathscr{O}_{E_{\rm red}},W_{\bullet}).

By (
3.4
), we have

\displaystyle{\rm Gr}^{W}_{m}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\overline{X}}}\mathscr{O}_{E})=\bigoplus_{i\geq 0}R^{k-m}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{D}_{[i+1],[m-i]}/\Delta}(\log E_{[i+1],[m-i]}))\otimes\mathscr{O}_{E_{[i+1],[m-i]}},

and

\displaystyle{\rm Gr}^{W}_{m}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\overline{X}}}\mathscr{O}_{E_{\rm red}})=\bigoplus_{i\geq 0}R^{k-m}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{D}_{[i+1],[m-i]}/\Delta}(\log E_{[i+1],[m-i]}))\otimes\mathscr{O}_{E_{[i+1],[m-i],{\rm red}}}.

According to
[
46
, §4.14]
and the assumption
(UM)
, the induced morphism

\displaystyle{\rm Gr}^{W}_{m}(\tau):{\rm Gr}^{W}_{m}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\overline{X}}}\mathscr{O}_{E})\to{\rm Gr}^{W}_{m}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\overline{X}}}\mathscr{O}_{E_{\rm red}})

is a quasi-isomorphism for every
m
. Consequently,
\tau
is a quasi-isomorphism. As a result, the morphism

(3.15)

\displaystyle R\Gamma(E,\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\overline{X}}}\mathscr{O}_{E})\to R\Gamma(E_{\rm red},\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\overline{X}}}\mathscr{O}_{E_{\rm red}})

is also a quasi-isomorphism. By Theorem
3.2
,
R^{k}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}})
is locally free and commutes with arbitrary base changes for every
k
. Therefore, there exists a canonical isomorphism

(3.16)

\displaystyle R\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}})\otimes\mathbb{C}(0)\simeq R\Gamma(E,\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\overline{X}}}\mathscr{O}_{E}).

Combining (
3.15
) and (
3.16
), we conclude that
\tau_{0}
is a quasi-isomorphism.
∎

The residue sequences

\displaystyle 0\to

\displaystyle\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{X}_{[k]}/\Delta}(\log\overline{D}_{[k]})\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\overline{X}_{[k]}}}\mathscr{O}_{E_{[k],\rm red}}[-1]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\wedge\frac{dt}{t}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{X}_{[k]}}(\log\overline{D}_{[k]})\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\overline{X}_{[k]}}}\mathscr{O}_{E_{[k],\rm red}}

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{X}_{[k]}/\Delta}(\log\overline{D}_{[k]})\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\overline{X}_{[k]}}}\mathscr{O}_{E_{[k],\rm red}}\to 0

for all
k\geq 0
induce the residue distinguished triangle:

(3.17)

\displaystyle\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\overline{X}}}\mathscr{O}_{E_{\rm red}}[-1]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\wedge\frac{dt}{t}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{(\overline{X},\overline{D}),{\rm BM}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\overline{X}}}\mathscr{O}_{E_{\rm red}}\to\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\overline{X}}}\mathscr{O}_{E_{\rm red}}\to.

The connecting map of this triangle yields a natural morphism

\displaystyle{\rm Res}^{q}_{0}:\mathbb{H}^{q}(E_{\rm red},\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\overline{X}}}\mathscr{O}_{E_{\rm red}})\to\mathbb{H}^{q}(E_{\rm red},\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\overline{X}}}\mathscr{O}_{E_{\rm red}})

for every
q
.

Lemma 3.8
.

The map
{\rm Res}^{q}_{0}
is the residue map associated with

\nabla_{\rm GM}:R^{q}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}})\to\Omega_{\Delta}(\log 0)\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\Delta}}R^{q}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}})

via the identification of fibers given by Lemma
3.7
:

\tau^{q}_{0}:R^{q}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}})\otimes\mathbb{C}(0)\simeq\mathbb{H}^{q}(E_{\rm red},\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\overline{X}}}\mathscr{O}_{E_{\rm red}}).

Proof.

Consider the commutative diagram of distinguished triangles:

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 66.60783pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&\cr&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-66.60783pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\overline{f}^{\ast}\Omega_{\Delta}(\log 0)\otimes_{\overline{f}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{\Delta}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}[-1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 112.83989pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-16.0pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.55057pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\overline{f}^{-1}({\rm Res}_{0})}$}}}\kern where
{\rm Res}_{0}:\Omega_{\Delta}(\log 0)\to\mathbb{C}(0)
is the residue map.
The connecting morphisms yields a commutative diagram

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 60.9646pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-31.61623pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 86.85245pt\raise-32.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbb{H}^{q}(E_{\rm red},\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm Therefore, the lemma is established.
∎

3.6.2.
Bi-complex resolution

Motivated by
[
46
]
, we aim to introduce a bi-complex resolution of
\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\overline{X}}}\mathscr{O}_{E_{\rm red}}
, where the operator
{\rm Res}_{0}(\nabla_{\rm GM})
is defined at the level of complexes.

Let
k\geq 1
be given. Observe that
(\overline{X}_{[k]},\overline{D}_{[k]})
forms a log smooth pair, and the morphism
(\overline{X}_{[k]},\overline{D}_{[k]})\to(\Delta,0)
is semistable with log smooth general fibers. Define
E_{[k]}:=i^{-1}_{[k]}(E)
as the pullback of
E
via the natural morphism
\overline{X}_{[k]}\to\overline{X}
, and let
\overline{D}^{h}_{[k]}
denote the horizontal component of
\overline{D}_{[k]}
.

Let
W^{E_{[k]}}_{\bullet}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{X}_{[k]}}(\log\overline{D}_{[k]})
represent the weight filtration on
\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{X}_{[k]}}(\log\overline{D}_{[k]})
with respect to the divisor
E_{[k]}
, which is explicitly defined as follows:

\displaystyle W^{E_{[k]}}_{m}\Omega^{p}_{\overline{X}_{[k]}}(\log\overline{D}_{[k]})=\begin{cases}0,&m<0\\
\Omega^{p}_{\overline{X}_{[k]}}(\log\overline{D}_{[k]}),&m\geq p\\
\Omega^{p-m}_{\overline{X}_{[k]}}(\log\overline{D}^{h}_{[k]})\wedge\Omega^{m}_{X}(\log\overline{D}_{[k]}),&0\leq m\leq p\end{cases}.

Let
A^{\bullet,\bullet}_{(\overline{X}_{[k]},\overline{D}_{[k]})/\Delta}
denote a bi-complex of sheaves on
E_{[k]}
, where

A^{p,q}_{(\overline{X}_{[k]},\overline{D}_{[k]})/\Delta}=\Omega^{p+q+1}_{\overline{X}_{[k]}}(\log\overline{D}_{[k]})/W^{E_{[k]}}_{p}\Omega^{p+q+1}_{\overline{X}_{[k]}}(\log\overline{D}_{[k]}).

The differentials are defined as follows:

d^{\prime}=\frac{dt}{t}\wedge:A^{p,q}_{(\overline{X}_{[k]},\overline{D}_{[k]})/\Delta}\to A^{p+1,q}_{(\overline{X}_{[k]},\overline{D}_{[k]})/\Delta},

and

d^{\prime\prime}=d:A^{p,q}_{(\overline{X}_{[k]},\overline{D}_{[k]})/\Delta}\to A^{p,q+1}_{(\overline{X}_{[k]},\overline{D}_{[k]})/\Delta}.

The bi-complex
A^{\bullet,\bullet}_{\overline{X}_{[k]}/\Delta}
is equipped with a weight filtration given by

W^{\overline{f}}_{m}(A^{p,q}_{(\overline{X}_{[k]},\overline{D}_{[k]})/\Delta}):=W_{m}^{\overline{D}^{h}_{[k]}}\Omega^{p+q+1}_{\overline{X}_{[k]}}(\log\overline{D}_{[k]})/W^{E_{[k]}}_{p}\Omega^{p+q+1}_{\overline{X}_{[k]}}(\log\overline{D}_{[k]}),

where
W^{\overline{D}^{h}_{[k]}}_{\bullet}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{X}_{[k]}}(\log\overline{D}_{[k]})
denotes the weight filtration on
\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{X}_{[k]}}(\log\overline{D}_{[k]})
with respect to the divisor
\overline{D}^{h}_{[k]}
. Explicitly,

W^{\overline{D}^{h}_{[k]}}_{m}\Omega^{p}_{\overline{X}_{[k]}}(\log\overline{D}_{[k]})=\begin{cases}0,&m<0\\
\Omega^{p}_{\overline{X}_{[k]}}(\log\overline{D}_{[k]}),&m\geq p\\
\Omega^{p-m}_{\overline{X}_{[k]}}(\log E_{[k]})\wedge\Omega^{m}_{X}(\log\overline{D}_{[k]}),&0\leq m\leq p.\end{cases}

Additionally,
A^{\bullet,\bullet}_{(\overline{X}_{[k]},\overline{D}_{[k]})/\Delta}
is endowed with a monodromy weight filtration defined as

W_{m}(A^{p,q}_{(\overline{X}_{[k]},\overline{D}_{[k]})/\Delta}):=W^{\overline{D}_{[k]}}_{m+2p+1}\Omega^{p+q+1}_{\overline{X}_{[k]}}(\log\overline{D}_{[k]})/W^{E_{[k]}}_{p}\Omega^{p+q+1}_{\overline{X}_{[k]}}(\log\overline{D}_{[k]}),\quad m\in\mathbb{Z}.

We define the Hodge filtration as:

F^{r}(A^{\bullet,\bullet}_{(\overline{X}_{[k]},\overline{D}_{[k]})/\Delta})=\bigoplus_{p}\bigoplus_{q\geq r}A^{p,q}_{(\overline{X}_{[k]},\overline{D}_{[k]})/\Delta},\quad r\in\mathbb{Z}.

Let
A^{\bullet}_{(\overline{X}_{[k]},\overline{D}_{[k]})/\Delta}
denote the total complex of
A^{\bullet,\bullet}_{(\overline{X}_{[k]},\overline{D}_{[k]})/\Delta}
. The induced monodromy weight filtration, weight filtration, and Hodge filtration on
A^{\bullet}_{(\overline{X}_{[k]},\overline{D}_{[k]})/\Delta}
are denoted by
W_{\bullet}(A^{\bullet}_{(\overline{X}_{[k]},\overline{D}_{[k]})/\Delta})
,
W^{\overline{f}}_{[k],\bullet}(A^{\bullet}_{(\overline{X}_{[k]},\overline{D}_{[k]})/\Delta})
, and
F^{\bullet}(A^{\bullet}_{(\overline{X}_{[k]},\overline{D}_{[k]})/\Delta})
, respectively.

The morphism

\Omega^{q}_{\overline{X}_{[k]}}(\log\overline{D}_{[k]})\to A^{0,q}_{(\overline{X}_{[k]},\overline{D}_{[k]})/\Delta},\quad\omega\mapsto(-1)^{q}\frac{dt}{t}\wedge\omega\!\!\!\!\mod W^{E_{[k]}}_{0}

induces a morphism

(3.18)

\displaystyle\theta_{[k]}:\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{X}_{[k]}/\Delta}(\log\overline{D}_{[k]})\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\overline{X}_{[k]}}}\mathscr{O}_{E_{\rm red}}\to A^{\bullet}_{(\overline{X}_{[k]},\overline{D}_{[k]})/\Delta}.

On the other hand, the projection map

A^{p,q}_{(\overline{X}_{[k]},\overline{D}_{[k]})/\Delta}\to A^{p+1,q-1}_{(\overline{X}_{[k]},\overline{D}_{[k]})/\Delta},\quad\omega\mapsto\omega\!\!\!\!\mod W^{E_{[k]}}_{p+1}

commutes with
d^{\prime}
and
d^{\prime\prime}
. Consequently, it induces an endomorphism

(3.19)

\displaystyle\nu_{[k]}:A^{\bullet}_{(\overline{X}_{[k]},\overline{D}_{[k]})/\Delta}\to A^{\bullet}_{(\overline{X}_{[k]},\overline{D}_{[k]})/\Delta}.

Let

\displaystyle A^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}:={\rm Tot}\left(\cdots\to A^{\bullet}_{(\overline{X}_{[2]},\overline{D}_{[2]})/\Delta}[-2]\to A^{\bullet}_{(\overline{X}_{[1]},\overline{D}_{[1]})/\Delta}\right).

The complex
A^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}
is endowed with a weight filtration

\displaystyle W^{\overline{f}}_{m}:={\rm Tot}\left(\cdots\to W^{\overline{f}}_{m-1}A^{\bullet}_{(\overline{X}_{[2]},\overline{D}_{[2]})/\Delta}[-2]\to W^{\overline{f}}_{m}A^{\bullet}_{(\overline{X}_{[1]},\overline{D}_{[1]})/\Delta}\right),\quad m\in\mathbb{Z},

and a monodromy weight filtration

\displaystyle W_{m}:={\rm Tot}\left(\cdots\to W_{m-1}A^{\bullet}_{(\overline{X}_{[2]},\overline{D}_{[2]})/\Delta}[-2]\to W_{m}A^{\bullet}_{(\overline{X}_{[1]},\overline{D}_{[1]})/\Delta}\right),\quad m\in\mathbb{Z}.

We define the Hodge filtration as

\displaystyle F^{p}:={\rm Tot}\left(\cdots\to F^{p-1}A^{\bullet}_{(\overline{X}_{[2]},\overline{D}_{[2]})/\Delta}[-2]\to F^{p}A^{\bullet}_{(\overline{X}_{[1]},\overline{D}_{[1]})/\Delta}\right),\quad p\in\mathbb{Z}.

Observe that the weight filtration
W_{\bullet}
and the Hodge filtration
F^{\bullet}
on
\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}
naturally induce corresponding filtrations on
\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\overline{X}}}\mathscr{O}_{E_{\rm red}}
, which are still denoted by
W_{\bullet}
and
F^{\bullet}
.
The maps (
3.18
) induce a morphism

\displaystyle\theta:\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\overline{X}}}\mathscr{O}_{E_{\rm red}}\to A^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}},

which preserves both the weight filtration and the Hodge filtration on both sides.

Lemma 3.9
.

The map

\theta:(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\overline{X}}}\mathscr{O}_{E_{\rm red}},W_{\bullet},F^{\bullet})\to(A^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}},W^{\overline{f}}_{\bullet},F^{\bullet})

is a bi-filtered quasi-isomorphism. In particular,
\theta
induces an isomorphism

\mathbb{H}^{q}(E_{\rm red},\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\overline{X}}}\mathscr{O}_{E_{\rm red}})\to\mathbb{H}^{q}(E_{\rm red},A^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}})

for every
q
.

Proof.

According to (
3.5
), one has

\displaystyle\text{Gr}^{p}_{F}\text{Gr}^{W}_{m}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},\text{BM}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\overline{X}}}\mathscr{O}_{E_{\text{red}}})\simeq_{\text{qis}}\bigoplus_{i\geq 0}\Omega^{p-m}_{\overline{D}_{[i+1],[m-i]}/\Delta}(\log E_{[i+1],[m-i]})\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\overline{D}_{[i+1],[m-i]}}}\mathscr{O}_{E_{[i+1],[m-i],\text{red}}}[-p].

On the other hand, one has

(3.20)

\displaystyle\text{Gr}^{W^{\overline{f}}}_{m}A^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},\text{BM}}

\displaystyle\simeq_{\text{qis}}\bigoplus_{i\geq 0}\text{Gr}^{W^{\overline{f}}}_{m-i}(A^{\bullet}_{(\overline{X}_{[i+1]},\overline{D}_{[i+1]})/\Delta})[-2i][i]

\displaystyle\simeq_{\text{qis}}\bigoplus_{i\geq 0}A^{\bullet}_{(\overline{D}_{[i+1],[m-i]},E_{[i+1],[m-i]})/\Delta}[-m],

with the induced Hodge filtration

\displaystyle F^{p}(\text{Gr}^{W^{\overline{f}}}_{m}A^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},\text{BM}})

\displaystyle\simeq_{\text{qis}}\bigoplus_{i\geq 0}F^{p}\text{Gr}^{W^{\overline{f}}}_{m-i}(A^{\bullet}_{(\overline{X}_{[i+1]},\overline{D}_{[i+1]})/\Delta}(-i)[-2i])[i]

\displaystyle\simeq_{\text{qis}}\bigoplus_{i\geq 0}F^{p}(A^{\bullet}_{(\overline{D}_{[i+1],[m-i]},E_{[i+1],[m-i]})/\Delta}(-m)[-m])

\displaystyle\simeq_{\text{qis}}\bigoplus_{i\geq 0}F^{p-m}A^{\bullet}_{(\overline{D}_{[i+1],[m-i]},E_{[i+1],[m-i]})/\Delta}[-m].

Therefore,

\displaystyle\text{Gr}^{p}_{F}\text{Gr}^{W}_{m}A^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},\text{BM}}\simeq_{\text{qis}}\bigoplus_{i\geq 0}\text{Gr}^{p-m}_{F}A^{\bullet}_{(\overline{D}_{[i+1],[m-i]},E_{[i+1],[m-i]})/\Delta}[-m].

Since each
(\overline{D}_{[k],[l]},E_{[k],[l]})\to(\Delta,0)
is a semistable family with smooth general fibers, by condition
(UM)
and
[
46
, Lemma 4.15]
, one has

\Omega^{p-m}_{\overline{D}_{[i+1],[m-i]}/\Delta}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\overline{D}_{[i+1],[m-i]}}}\mathscr{O}_{E_{[i+1],[m-i],\text{red}}}\simeq\text{Gr}^{p-m}_{F}A^{\bullet}_{(\overline{D}_{[i+1],[m-i]},E_{[i+1],[m-i]})/\Delta}[p-m]

for every
p
and
m
. Thus,
\theta
induces an isomorphism

\displaystyle\text{Gr}^{p}_{F}\text{Gr}^{W}_{m}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},\text{BM}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\overline{X}}}\mathscr{O}_{E_{\text{red}}})\simeq_{\text{qis}}\text{Gr}^{p}_{F}\text{Gr}^{W^{\overline{f}}}_{m}(A^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},\text{BM}})

for every
p
and
m
.
The lemma is proved.
∎

3.6.3.

The endomorphisms (
3.19
) induce an endomorphism

\displaystyle\nu:A^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}\to A^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}

which satisfies the following conditions:

\displaystyle\nu(W_{m})\subset W_{m-2},\quad\nu(W^{\overline{f}}_{m})\subset W^{\overline{f}}_{m},\quad\text{and}\quad\nu(F^{p})\subset F^{p-1},\quad\forall m,p.

Lemma 3.10
.

The following diagram is commutative:

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 60.9646pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-60.9646pt\raise BM}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\overline{X}}}\mathscr{O}_{E_{\rm red}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 68.0601pt\raise 5.43056pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.43056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 84.9646pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-15.97223pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.0853pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{{\rm Res}_{0}^{q}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 84.9646pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbb{H}^{q}(E_{\rm red},A^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 123.83603pt\raise-15.97223pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{-\nu}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 123.83603pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-60.9646pt\raise-31.94446pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbb{H}^{q}(E_{\rm red},\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\overline{X}}}\mathscr{O}_{E_{\rm red}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 68.0601pt\raise-26.5139pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.43056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 84.9646pt\raise-31.94446pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 84.9646pt\raise-31.94446pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbb{H}^{q}(E_{\rm red},A^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}})}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

Proof.

Let

B^{\bullet}:=\text{cone}(\nu:A^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},\text{BM}}\to A^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},\text{BM}})[-1].

Then
B^{\bullet}
is the total complex of a double complex
B^{\bullet,\bullet}
, where
B^{p,q}=A^{p,q}_{\overline{f},\text{BM}}\oplus A^{p,q-1}_{\overline{f},\text{BM}}
. Define maps

\mu_{[k]}:\Omega^{q}_{\overline{X}_{[k]}}(\log\overline{D}_{[k]})\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\overline{X}_{[k]}}}\mathscr{O}_{E_{[k],\text{red}}}\to A^{0,q}_{(\overline{X}_{[k]},\overline{D}_{[k]})/\Delta}\oplus A^{0,q-1}_{(\overline{X}_{[k]},\overline{D}_{[k]})/\Delta},

given explicitly by

\mu_{[k]}(\omega)=\left(\omega\wedge\frac{dt}{t}\!\!\!\!\mod W_{0}^{E_{[k]}},(-1)^{q-1}\omega\!\!\!\!\mod W_{0}^{E_{[k]}}\right).

These maps induce a morphism

\mu:\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},\text{BM}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\overline{X}}}\mathscr{O}_{E_{\text{red}}}\to B^{\bullet}.

We obtain the following commutative diagram with exact columns, in which the left column is the residue distinguished triangle (
3.17
), and the horizontal maps are quasi-isomorphisms (Lemma
3.9
):

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 47.17307pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-47.17307pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},\text{BM}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\overline{X}}}\mathscr{O}_{E_{\text{red}}}[-1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-15.84647pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.94446pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\wedge\frac{dt}{t}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.30707pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 48.66437pt\raise 6.5pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\theta[-1]}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 71.17307pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 71.17307pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Omega^{\bullet}_{(\overline{X},\overline{D}),\text{BM}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\overline{X}}}\mathscr{O}_{E_{\text{red}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 60.96156pt\raise-26.50543pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mu}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 86.91095pt\raise-31.69293pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 96.25296pt\raise-55.69293pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\raise-89.89673pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 54.26857pt\raise-57.64824pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.43056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 79.2286pt\raise-63.0788pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 79.2286pt\raise-63.0788pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 96.25296pt\raise-89.89673pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-91.64673pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 93.25296pt\raise-91.64673pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

The lemma follows from Lemma
3.8
and the observation that the connecting homomorphism in the long exact hypercohomology sequence associated with the right-hand column is induced by
-\nu
.
∎

Proposition 3.11
.

(A^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}},W^{\overline{f}}_{\bullet},W_{\bullet},F^{\bullet})
is a cohomological filtered mixed Hodge complex in the sense of Zein
[
14
]
, i.e., the following conditions are satisfied:

(1)

For every
a<b
,
(W^{\overline{f}}_{b}/W^{\overline{f}}_{a},W_{\bullet},F^{\bullet})
forms a mixed Hodge complex of sheaves;

(2)

The spectral sequence associated with the hypercohomology of
(A^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}},W^{\overline{f}}_{\bullet})
degenerates at the
E_{2}
-page.

Proof.

Let
a<b
. According to
[
13
, (3.3.2)]
there are quasi-isomorphisms

\displaystyle\text{Gr}^{W}_{m}(W^{\overline{f}}_{b}/W^{\overline{f}}_{a})\simeq

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{i\geq 0}\text{Gr}^{W}_{m-i}(W^{\overline{f}}_{b-i}/W^{\overline{f}}_{a-i}(A^{\bullet}_{(\overline{X}_{[i+1]},\overline{D}_{[i+1]})/\Delta}))(-i)[-i]

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\geq 0,p\geq 0,j\leq m+p-i,\\
j\in[a-i,b-i]\end{subarray}}\Omega^{\bullet}_{E_{[i+1],[j],[m+2p-i-j+1]}}(-m-p)[-m-2p].

with the induced Hodge filtration given by

\displaystyle F^{\bullet}(\text{Gr}^{W}_{m}(W^{\overline{f}}_{b}/W^{\overline{f}}_{a}))

\displaystyle\simeq_{\rm qis}\bigoplus_{i\geq 0}F^{\bullet}\text{Gr}^{W}_{m-i}(W^{\overline{f}}_{b-i}/W^{\overline{f}}_{a-i}(A^{\bullet}_{(\overline{X}_{[i+1]},\overline{D}_{[i+1]})/\Delta})(-i)[-2i])[i]

\displaystyle\simeq\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\geq 0,p\geq 0,j\leq m+p-i,\\
j\in[a-i,b-i]\end{subarray}}F^{\bullet}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{E_{[i+1],[j],[m+2p-i-j+1]}}(-m-p)[-m-2p]).

Here
E_{[i],[j],[k]}
denotes the union of strata of codimension
k
of
E_{[i],[j]}
.
This shows that

\left(\text{Gr}^{W}_{m}(W^{\overline{f}}_{b}/W^{\overline{f}}_{a}),F^{\bullet}(\text{Gr}^{W}_{m}(W^{\overline{f}}_{b}/W^{\overline{f}}_{a}))\right)

is a Hodge complex of sheaves of weight
m
. This proves claim (1). Statement (2) follows from Theorem
3.2
, Lemma
3.7
and Lemma
3.9
.
∎

Theorem 3.12
.

For every
k
, the induced filtration
W_{\bullet}
on
\mathbb{H}^{k}(E_{\rm red},A^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}})
coincides with the relative monodromy weight filtration of
(\mathbb{H}^{k}(E_{\rm red},A^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}),W^{\overline{f}}_{\bullet},\nu)
. Consequently, the triple

\left(R^{k}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}})\otimes\mathbb{C}(0),W_{\bullet}R^{k}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}})\otimes\mathbb{C}(0),{\rm Res}^{k}_{0}\right)

admits a relative monodromy weight filtration.

Proof.

Consider the hypercohomology spectral sequence of
(A^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},\text{BM}},W^{\overline{f}}_{\bullet})
. According to (
3.20
), one has

(3.21)

\displaystyle E_{1}^{-m,k+m}(A^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},\text{BM}},W^{\overline{f}}_{\bullet})\simeq_{\text{qis}}\bigoplus_{i\geq 0}\mathbb{H}^{k-m}(E_{[i+1],[m-i]},A^{\bullet}_{(\overline{D}_{[i+1],[m-i]},E_{[i+1],[m-i]})/\Delta}).

The morphism and filtration on

\mathbb{H}^{k-m}(E_{[i+1],[m-i]},A^{\bullet}_{(\overline{D}_{[i+1],[m-i]},E_{[i+1],[m-i]})/\Delta})

induced by
\nu
and
W_{\bullet}[-m]
coincide with the residue map and the monodromy weight filtration on the limit mixed Hodge structure of the semistable family

(\overline{D}_{[i+1],[m-i]},E_{[i+1],[m-i]})\to(\Delta,0)

(
[
46
, §4.17]
). Therefore, by
[
46
, Proposition 4.23]
, it follows that the induced filtration
W_{\bullet}[-m]
on

E_{1}^{-m,k+m}(A^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},\text{BM}},W^{\overline{f}}_{\bullet})

is the relative monodromy weight filtration of the endomorphism
H^{k}(\text{Gr}^{W^{\overline{f}}}_{m}\nu)
with respect to the filtration
W^{\overline{f}}
. Since
(A^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}},W^{\overline{f}}_{\bullet},W_{\bullet},F^{\bullet})
forms a cohomological filtered mixed Hodge complex and the spectral sequence
E_{r}^{-m,k+m}(A^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},\text{BM}},W^{\overline{f}}_{\bullet})
degenerates at the
E_{2}
-page (Proposition
3.11
), the first assertion follows directly from
[
15
, Lemma 3.17]
. The second assertion is then a consequence of the first assertion, combined with Lemma
3.9
and Lemma
3.10
.
∎

3.7.
Admissibility

Let
f:(X,D)\to S
be a projective simple normal crossing family with relative dimension
\dim X-\dim S=n
. Let
\widetilde{S}
be a smooth variety containing
S
as a Zariski open subset such that
D_{\widetilde{S}}:=\widetilde{S}\setminus S
is a simple normal crossing divisor. Denote
\mathcal{V}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}}:=R^{k}f_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{f,{\rm BM}})
. Then, as discussed in §
2.6
, there exists a variation of mixed Hodge structures

\mathbb{V}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}}=(\mathcal{V}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}},\nabla_{\rm GM},W_{\bullet}(\mathcal{V}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}}),F^{\bullet}(\mathcal{V}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}}))

for every
k\geq 0
.

Theorem 3.13
.

For every
k
,
\mathbb{V}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}}
is a graded polarizable variation of mixed Hodge structures which is admissible with respect to
\widetilde{S}
.

Remark 3.14
.

The admissibility condition for variations of mixed Hodge structures was first introduced by Steenbrink-Zucker
[
45
]
over one-dimensional bases and later generalized by Kashiwara
[
21
]
to general bases. In this paper, we adopt Kashiwara’s definition.

Proof.

By
[
21
, Lemma 1.9.1]
and Lemma
3.1
, it suffices to assume that
S
is a punctured disc and that condition
(UM)
holds for the family. The extension of the Hodge filtration then follows from Proposition
3.5
, while the existence of the relative monodromy weight filtration is guaranteed by Theorem
3.12
.
∎

Denote by
\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}}
the lower canonical extension of
\mathcal{V}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}}
, and let
W_{m}(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}})
be the lower canonical extension of
W_{m}(\mathcal{V}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}})
.

Corollary 3.15
.

(Kashiwara
[
21
, Proposition 1.11.3]
)

The Hodge filtration
\{F^{p}(\mathcal{V}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}})\}
admits an extension
\{F^{p}(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}})\}
on
\widetilde{S}
such that:

(1)

Each
F^{p}(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}})
is a subbundle of
\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}}
.

(2)

Each
{\rm Gr}_{F}^{p}{\rm Gr}_{m}^{W}(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}})
is locally free.

Next we give a geometric discription of Kashiwara’s extension of Hodge filtrations.
As mentioned in Remark
2.3
, by employing a semi-log resolution, the simple normal crossing family
f:(X,D)\to S
can be extended to a projective morphism
\widetilde{f}:(\widetilde{X},\widetilde{D})\to(\widetilde{S},D_{\widetilde{S}})
, where
(\widetilde{X},\widetilde{D})
is a simple normal crossing pair and
(\widetilde{S},D_{\widetilde{S}})
is a log smooth pair. Furthermore, it is possible to assume the existence of a closed algebraic subset
Z\subset\widetilde{S}
of codimension
\geq 2
, such that

(\overline{S},D_{\overline{S}}):=(\widetilde{S},D_{\widetilde{S}})\setminus Z

is a log smooth pair with a smooth boundary divisor
D_{\overline{S}}
, and

(\overline{X},\overline{D}):=(\widetilde{X},\widetilde{D})\cap\widetilde{f}^{-1}(\overline{S})

is a simple normal crossing pair. Additionally, the restricted morphism

\overline{f}:=\widetilde{f}|_{\overline{X}}:(\overline{X},\overline{D})\to(\overline{S},D_{\overline{S}})

forms a semistable family.

Theorem 3.16
.

There is a natural bi-filtered isomorphism between

\left(R^{k}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}),W_{\bullet}(R^{k}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}})),F^{\bullet}(R^{k}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}))\right)

and

\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}},W_{\bullet}(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}}),F^{\bullet}(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}})\right)|_{\overline{S}}.

Proof.

By
Proposition
3.2
there is a filtered isomorphism

\tau:\left(R^{k}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}),W_{\bullet}(R^{k}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}))\right)\to\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}},W_{\bullet}(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}})\right)|_{\overline{S}}.

By Proposition
3.5
,
\{\tau(R^{k}\overline{f}_{\ast}(F^{p}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}}))\}_{p\geq 0}
is an extension of of the Hodge filtrations
\{F^{p}(\mathcal{V}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}})\}_{p\geq 0}
with locally free subquotients. According to the uniqueness of such extensions (
[
15
, Corollary 5.2]
) one concludes that
\tau
gives an isomorphism

\displaystyle\tau:F^{p}R^{k}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}})\simeq R^{k}\overline{f}_{\ast}(F^{p}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}})\to\widetilde{F}^{p}(\mathcal{V}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}})|_{\overline{S}}.

This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

3.8.
The associated logarithmic Higgs bundle

We adopt the notations introduced in §
3.7
. Let
j:\overline{S}\to\widetilde{S}
denote the open immersion. According to Theorem
3.16
, it follows that

F^{p}(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}})\simeq j_{\ast}\left(R^{k}\overline{f}_{\ast}(F^{p}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}})\right)

is the reflexive hull of
R^{k}\overline{f}_{\ast}(F^{p}\Omega^{\bullet}_{\overline{f},{\rm BM}})
for every
p
. Consequently, by Lemma
3.6
,
F^{p}(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}})
satisfies Griffiths transversality:

(3.22)

\displaystyle\nabla_{\rm GM}(F^{p}(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}}))\subset F^{p}(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}})\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\widetilde{S}}}\Omega_{\widetilde{S}}(\log D_{\widetilde{S}}).

We observe that
F^{n+1}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}}=0
.
Let

\widetilde{H}^{p,k-p}_{f,{\rm BM}}:={\rm Gr}_{F}^{p}(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}}),\quad p=0,\dots,n.

Then (
3.22
) implies that
\nabla_{\rm GM}
induces a morphism

(3.23)

\displaystyle\theta:\widetilde{H}^{p,k-p}_{f,{\rm BM}}\to\widetilde{H}^{p-1,k-p+1}_{f,{\rm BM}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\widetilde{S}}}\Omega_{\widetilde{S}}(\log D_{\widetilde{S}}),\quad p=0,\dots,n.

Define
\widetilde{H}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}}:=\bigoplus_{p=0}^{n}\widetilde{H}^{p,k-p}_{f,{\rm BM}}
, and let

\theta:\widetilde{H}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}}\to\widetilde{H}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\widetilde{S}}}\Omega_{\widetilde{S}}(\log D_{\widetilde{S}})

denote the induced map. The flatness of
\nabla_{\rm GM}
(i.e.,
\nabla^{2}_{\rm GM}=0
) ensures that
\theta^{2}=0
. Consequently,
(\widetilde{H}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}},\theta)
defines a logarithmic Higgs bundle.

Definition 3.17
.

We refer to
(\widetilde{H}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}},\theta)
as the lower canonical logarithmic Higgs bundle associated with the admissible variation of mixed Hodge structures
\mathbb{V}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}}
.

This logarithmic Higgs bundle is equipped with a weight filtration of Higgs subbundles. For each
k
and
p
, let

\{W_{m}(\widetilde{H}^{p,k-p}_{f,{\rm BM}})=W_{m}({\rm Gr}_{F}^{p}(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}}))\}_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}

denote the induced weight filtration, and define

W_{m}(\widetilde{H}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}}):=\bigoplus_{p=0}^{n}W_{m}(\widetilde{H}^{p,k-p}_{f,{\rm BM}}).

Then
(W_{m}(\widetilde{H}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}}),\theta)
forms a logarithmic Higgs subbundle, i.e.,

\theta(W_{m}(\widetilde{H}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}}))\subset W_{m}(\widetilde{H}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}})\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\widetilde{S}}}\Omega_{\widetilde{S}}(\log D_{\widetilde{S}}).

This construction yields the sub-quotient logarithmic Higgs bundles

(3.24)

\displaystyle\theta:{\rm Gr}^{W}_{m}(\widetilde{H}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}})\to{\rm Gr}^{W}_{m}(\widetilde{H}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}})\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\widetilde{S}}}\Omega_{\widetilde{S}}(\log D_{\widetilde{S}}).

This defines the lower canonical logarithmic Higgs bundle associated with the variation of pure Hodge structure
{\rm Gr}^{W}_{m}(\mathbb{V}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}})
.

Proposition 3.18
.

Suppose that
\widetilde{S}
is projective. Let
K\subset\widetilde{H}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}}
be a coherent subsheaf on
\widetilde{S}
satisfying
\theta(K)=0
. Then, the class
-c_{1}(K)
is pseudo-effective.

Proof.

Define

{\rm Gr}^{W}_{m}(K):=\frac{K\cap W_{m}(\widetilde{H}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}})}{K\cap W_{m-1}(\widetilde{H}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}})}.

Then, we have

(3.25)

\displaystyle-c_{1}(K)=\sum_{m}-c_{1}({\rm Gr}^{W}_{m}(K)).

Note that
\mathbb{V}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}}
is a graded polarized variation of mixed Hodge structures (Theorem
2.14
). Consequently,

({\rm Gr}_{m}^{W}(\mathcal{V}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}}),F^{\bullet}{\rm Gr}_{m}^{W}(\mathcal{V}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}}))

is a polarized variation of Hodge structure on
S
, and

\theta:{\rm Gr}^{W}_{m}(\widetilde{H}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}})\to{\rm Gr}^{W}_{m}(\widetilde{H}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}})\otimes\Omega_{\widetilde{S}}(\log D_{\widetilde{S}})

represents the associated lower canonical logarithmic Higgs bundle. Moreover,
{\rm Gr}^{W}_{m}(K)
is a coherent subsheaf of
{\rm Gr}^{W}_{m}(\widetilde{H}^{k}_{f,{\rm BM}})
satisfying
\theta({\rm Gr}^{W}_{m}(K))=0
. By
[
5
, Theorem 1.4]
(see also
[
54
, Proposition 1.1]
for the unipotent version),
-c_{1}({\rm Gr}^{W}_{m}(K))
is pseudo-effective for every
m
. Therefore,
-c_{1}(K)
is pseudo-effective as well, thanks to (
3.25
).
∎

3.8.1.
Top Hodge bundle

Recall that
n=\dim X-\dim S
. Regarding the extension of the top-indexed Hodge bundle, there exists the following inclusion.

Lemma 3.19
.

There exists an inclusion

R^{q}\widetilde{f}_{\ast}(\mathscr{O}_{\widetilde{X}}(K_{\widetilde{X}/\widetilde{S}}+\widetilde{D}^{h}))\subset\widetilde{H}^{n,q}_{f,{\rm BM}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\widetilde{S}}}\mathscr{O}_{\widetilde{S}}(D_{\widetilde{S}})

where
\widetilde{D}^{h}
denotes the horizontal part of
\widetilde{D}
.

Proof.

According to equation (
3.3
) and Theorem
3.16
, we derive the following:

\displaystyle R^{q}\widetilde{f}_{\ast}(\mathscr{O}_{\widetilde{X}}(K_{\widetilde{X}/\widetilde{S}}+\widetilde{D}^{h}))|_{\overline{S}}

\displaystyle\simeq R^{q}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\mathscr{O}_{\overline{X}}(K_{\overline{X}/\overline{S}}+\overline{D}^{h}))

\displaystyle\subset R^{q}\overline{f}_{\ast}(\mathscr{O}_{\overline{X}}(K_{\overline{X}/\overline{S}}+\overline{D}-\overline{f}^{\ast}D_{\overline{S}}))\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\overline{S}}}\mathscr{O}_{\overline{S}}(D_{\overline{S}})

\displaystyle\simeq F^{n}(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}^{n+q}_{f,{\rm BM}})|_{\overline{S}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\overline{S}}}\mathscr{O}_{\overline{S}}(D_{\overline{S}})

\displaystyle\simeq\widetilde{H}^{n,q}_{f,{\rm BM}}|_{\overline{S}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{\overline{S}}}\mathscr{O}_{\overline{S}}(D_{\overline{S}}).

Given that
R^{q}\widetilde{f}_{\ast}(\mathscr{O}_{\widetilde{X}}(K_{\widetilde{X}/\widetilde{S}}+\widetilde{D}^{h}))
is locally free as stated in
[
15
, Theorem 7.3,(b)]
, taking reflexive hulls on both sides completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

4.
The Viehweg-Zuo Higgs sheaves

4.1.
Setting

Throughout this section, we fix a projective morphism
f:X\to S
from a simple normal crossing variety to a smooth variety, with relative dimension
n=\dim X-\dim S
. Let
\Delta
be a
\mathbb{Q}
-divisor on
X
whose coefficients lie in
[0,1]
, such that
(X,\Delta)
is a simple normal crossing pair. Assume that there exists a simple normal crossing divisor
D_{f}\subset S
such that the restricted morphism
f^{o}:=f|_{X^{o}}:(X^{o},\Delta^{o})\to S^{o}
is a simple normal crossing family, where
S^{o}:=S\setminus D_{f}
,
X^{o}:=f^{-1}(S^{o})
, and
\Delta^{o}:=\Delta\cap X^{o}
. Furthermore, assume that
{\rm supp}(\Delta)
does not contain any component of
f^{-1}(D_{f})
.

We fix a line bundle
L
on
S
, and a nonzero morphism

(4.1)

\displaystyle s_{L}:L^{\otimes k}\to f_{\ast}\left(\mathscr{O}_{X}(kK_{X/S}+k\Delta)\right)

for some
k\geq 1
such that
k\Delta
is integral.

4.2.
The main result: Viehweg-Zuo Higgs sheaves

Define

B=\omega_{X/S}(\lceil\Delta\rceil)\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X}}f^{\ast}(L)^{-1},

a line bundle on
X
. The map
s_{L}
determines a non-zero section

s\in H^{0}(X,B^{\otimes k})
. Let
\varpi:X_{k}\to X
be the
k:1
cyclic covering map branched along
\{s=0\}
. Let
\mu:Z\to X_{k}
be a resolution of singularities such that
Z
is smooth (not necessarily connected) and
\Delta_{Z}:=(\varpi\mu)^{-1}(\Delta\cup X_{\rm sing})
is a simple normal crossing divisor. Define
g:=f\varpi\mu:Z\to S
, and let
D_{g}\subset\widetilde{X}
be a reduced closed algebraic subset containing
D_{f}
, such that
g:(Z,\Delta_{Z})\to S
is a log smooth family over
U:=S\setminus D_{g}
. Let
Z^{o}:=g^{-1}(U)
, and let
g^{o}:=g|_{Z^{o}}:(Z^{o},Z^{o}\cap\Delta_{Z})\to U
denote the restricted log smooth family.

Let
V\subset S
be a Zariski open subset such that
{\rm codim}_{S}(S\setminus V)\geq 2
and
V\cap D_{g}
is a simple normal crossing divisor on
V
. Then
R^{n}g^{o}_{\ast}(\mathbb{V}_{(Z^{o},Z^{o}\cap\Delta_{Z}),{\rm BM}})
underlies a graded polarized variation of mixed Hodge structures
\mathbb{V}^{n}_{g^{o},{\rm BM}}=(\mathcal{V}^{n}_{g^{o},{\rm BM}},\nabla,W_{\bullet}(\mathcal{V}^{n}_{g^{o},{\rm BM}}),F^{\bullet}_{{\rm BM}}(\mathcal{V}^{n}_{g^{o},{\rm BM}}))
on
S^{\prime o}
(Theorem
2.14
). By Theorem
3.13
,
\mathbb{V}^{n}_{g^{o},{\rm BM}}
is admissible on
V
. Therefore, according to §
3.8
, one has the lower canonical logarithmic Higgs bundle

\left(\widetilde{H}^{k}_{g^{o},{\rm BM}}=\bigoplus_{p=0}^{n}\widetilde{H}^{p,k-p}_{g^{o},{\rm BM}},\theta\right)

on
V
, associated with
\mathbb{V}^{n}_{g^{o},{\rm BM}}
(Definition
3.17
).

Theorem 4.1
.

Notations and assumptions as in §
4.1
and §
4.2
. Then the following statements hold:

(1)

There exists a natural inclusion
L\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{S}}\mathscr{O}_{S}(-D_{f})|_{V}\subset\widetilde{H}^{n,0}_{g^{o},{\rm BM}}
.

(2)

Let

(\bigoplus_{p=0}^{n}L^{p},\theta)\subset(\widetilde{H}^{n}_{g^{o},{\rm BM}},\theta)

be the meromorphic Higgs subsheaf generated by
L^{0}:=L\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{S}}\mathscr{O}_{S}(-D_{f})|_{V}
, where
L^{p}\subset\widetilde{H}^{n-p,p}_{g^{o},{\rm BM}}
. Then the Higgs field

\theta:L^{p}\to L^{p+1}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{V}}\Omega_{V}(\log(D_{g}\cap V))

is holomorphic over
V\setminus D_{f}
and has at most logarithmic poles along
D_{f}
for each
0\leq p<n
, i.e.,

\theta(L^{p})\subset L^{p+1}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{V}}\Omega_{V}(\log(D_{f}\cap V)),\quad 0\leq p<n.

The proof will occupy the remainder of this section. It will be completed by constructing a logarithmic Higgs subsheaf
\bigoplus_{p+q=n}G^{p,q}
of
\widetilde{H}^{n}_{g^{o},{\rm BM}}
that contains
L\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{S}}\mathscr{O}_{S}(-D_{f})|_{V}
, such that the Higgs field is holomorphic on
V\setminus D_{f}
.

4.3.
Construction:

4.3.1.
Preliminary

We recall some facts and make some assumptions that will be used in the construction of the Viehweg-Zuo Higgs sheaf.

(1)

By taking a suitable semi-log resolution of
(X,\Delta+f^{\ast}(D_{f}))
and
(Z,\Delta_{Z}+g^{\ast}(D_{g}))
, one can assume that
(X,\Delta+f^{\ast}(D_{f}))
and
(Z,\Delta_{Z}+g^{\ast}(D_{g}))
are semistable in codimension 1 over
S
. More precisely, there exists a Zariski open subset
V^{\prime}\subset V
such that
{\rm codim}_{S}(S\setminus V^{\prime})\geq 2
,
D^{\prime}_{g}:=V^{\prime}\cap D_{g}
and
D^{\prime}_{f}:=V^{\prime}\cap D_{f}
are (not necessarily connected) smooth divisors on
V^{\prime}
, and the restricted families

f_{1}=f|_{X_{1}}:(X_{1}:=f^{-1}(V^{\prime}),\Delta_{1}:=\lceil\Delta+f^{\ast}(D_{f})\rceil\cap X_{1})\to(V^{\prime},D^{\prime}_{f})

and

g_{1}=g|_{Z_{1}}:(Z_{1}:=g^{-1}(V^{\prime}),\Delta_{Z_{1}}:=\lceil\Delta_{Z}+g^{\ast}(D_{g})\rceil\cap Z_{1})\to(V^{\prime},D^{\prime}_{g})

are semistable.

(2)

According to §
3
, we have a logarithmic connection

\nabla_{\rm GM}:R^{n}g_{1\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{g_{1},{\rm BM}})\to R^{n}g_{1\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{g_{1},{\rm BM}})\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{V^{\prime}}}\Omega_{V^{\prime}}(\log D^{\prime}_{g})

and a filtration of subbundles
\{F^{p}R^{n}g_{1\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{g_{1},{\rm BM}})\}_{p=0,\dots,n}
satisfying the Griffiths transversality condition:

\nabla_{\rm GM}(F^{p}R^{n}g_{1\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{g_{1},{\rm BM}}))\subset F^{p-1}R^{n}g_{1\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{g_{1},{\rm BM}})\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{V^{\prime}}}\Omega_{V^{\prime}}(\log D^{\prime}_{g}),\quad p=0,\dots,n.

Moreover, one has isomorphisms

{\rm Gr}^{p}_{F}R^{n}g_{1\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{g_{1},{\rm BM}})\simeq R^{n}g_{1\ast}({\rm Gr}^{p}_{F}\Omega^{\bullet}_{g_{1},{\rm BM}}),\quad p=0,\dots,n.

Let

H^{p,n-p}_{g_{1},{\rm BM}}:={\rm Gr}^{p}_{F}R^{n}g_{1\ast}(\Omega^{\bullet}_{g_{1},{\rm BM}}),\quad p=0,\dots,n,

let

H^{n}_{g_{1},{\rm BM}}:=\bigoplus_{p=0}^{n}H^{p,n-p}_{g_{1},{\rm BM}},

and let

\theta_{g_{1}}:={\rm Gr}_{F}(\nabla):H^{n}_{g_{1},{\rm BM}}\to H^{n}_{g_{1},{\rm BM}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{V^{\prime}}}\Omega_{V^{\prime}}(\log D^{\prime}_{g}).

Then we obtain a logarithmic Higgs bundles
(H^{n}_{g_{1},{\rm BM}},\theta)
satisfying

\theta_{g_{1}}(H^{p,n-p}_{g_{1},{\rm BM}})\subset H^{p-1,n-p+1}_{g_{1},{\rm BM}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{V^{\prime}}}\Omega_{V^{\prime}}(\log D^{\prime}_{g}),\quad p=0,\dots,n.

According to Theorem
3.16
, there exists an isomorphism of logarithmic Higgs bundles

(4.2)

\displaystyle\eta:(H^{k}_{g_{1},{\rm BM}},\theta_{g_{1}})\to(\widetilde{H}^{k}_{g^{o},{\rm BM}},\theta)|_{V^{\prime}}.

which sends
H^{p,n-p}_{g_{1},{\rm BM}}
to
\widetilde{H}^{p,n-p}_{g^{o},{\rm BM}}|_{V^{\prime}}
for every
p=0,\dots,n
.

4.3.2.
Construction

Let
\varphi_{1}:=\varpi\mu|_{Z_{1}}:Z_{1}\to X_{1}
. Since
X_{k}
is embedded into the total space of the line bundle
B
, the pullback
\varpi^{\ast}(B)
admits a tautological section induced by
s
. This implies the existence of a non-zero morphism

(4.3)

\displaystyle\times\varphi_{1}^{\ast}(s^{\frac{1}{k}}):\varphi_{1}^{\ast}(B^{-1})\to\mathscr{O}_{Z_{1}}.

Recall that both families

f_{1}:(X_{1},\Delta_{1})\to(V^{\prime},D^{\prime}_{f})\quad\text{and}\quad g_{1}:(Z_{1},\Delta_{Z_{1}})\to(V^{\prime},D^{\prime}_{g})

are semistable. By (
3.2
), one has the Hodge filtrations (of complexes)
F^{p}\Omega^{\bullet}_{f_{1},{\rm BM}}
and
F^{p}\Omega^{\bullet}_{g_{1},{\rm BM}}
, for
0\leq p\leq n
, together with the natural morphisms

\varphi_{1}^{\ast}(F^{p}\Omega^{\bullet}_{f_{1},{\rm BM}})\to F^{p}\Omega^{\bullet}_{g_{1},{\rm BM}},\quad 0\leq p\leq n.

Combining these morphisms with (
4.3
), we obtain natural morphisms

\varphi_{1}^{\ast}(F^{p}\Omega^{\bullet}_{f_{1},{\rm BM}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X_{1}}}B^{-1})\to F^{p}\Omega^{\bullet}_{g_{1},{\rm BM}},\quad 0\leq p\leq n.

These induce natural morphisms

(4.4)

\displaystyle F^{p}\Omega^{\bullet}_{f_{1},{\rm BM}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X_{1}}}B^{-1}\to R\varphi_{1\ast}(F^{p}\Omega^{\bullet}_{g_{1},{\rm BM}}),\quad 0\leq p\leq n.

This further induces a morphism

\iota_{V^{\prime}}:R^{p+q}f_{1\ast}({\rm Gr}_{F}^{p}\Omega^{\bullet}_{f_{1},{\rm BM}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X_{1}}}B^{-1})\to R^{p+q}g_{1\ast}({\rm Gr}_{F}^{p}\Omega^{\bullet}_{g_{1},{\rm BM}})

for every
p,q
.

As in §
3.2
, we have the Koszul filtrations
\{K^{p}_{f_{1}}\}_{p\geq 0}
and
\{K^{p}_{g_{1}}\}_{p\geq 0}
of
\Omega^{\bullet}_{(X_{1},\Delta_{1}),{\rm BM}}
and
\Omega^{\bullet}_{(Z_{1},\Delta_{Z_{1}}),{\rm BM}}
, respectively.
The morphisms (
4.4
) induce a morphism between distinguished triangles:

\textstyle{f_{1}^{-1}\Omega_{V^{\prime}}(\log D^{\prime}_{f})\otimes_{f_{1}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{V^{\prime}}}{\rm Gr}_{F}^{p-1}\Omega^{\bullet}_{f_{1},{\rm BM}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X_{1}}}B^{-1}[-1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{R\varphi_{1\ast}\left(g_{1}^{-1}\Omega_{V^{\prime}}(\log D^{\prime}_{g})\otimes_{g_{1}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{V^{\prime}}}{\rm Gr}_{F}^{p-1}\Omega^{\bullet}_{g_{1},{\rm BM}}[-1]\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\frac{\sigma^{\geq p-1}(K^{0}_{f_{1}}/K^{2}_{f_{1}})}{\sigma^{\geq p+1}(K^{0}_{f_{1}}/K^{2}_{f_{1}})}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X_{1}}}B^{-1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{R\varphi_{1\ast}\left(\frac{\sigma^{\geq p-1}(K^{0}_{g_{1}}/K^{2}_{g_{1}})}{\sigma^{\geq p+1}(K^{0}_{g_{1}}/K^{2}_{g_{1}})}\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\rm Gr}_{F}^{p}\Omega^{\bullet}_{f_{1},{\rm BM}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X_{1}}}B^{-1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{R\varphi_{1\ast}({\rm Gr}_{F}^{p}\Omega^{\bullet}_{g_{1},{\rm BM}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

Taking the higher direct images, one obtains a commutative diagram:

\textstyle{R^{p+q}f_{1\ast}({\rm Gr}_{F}^{p}\Omega^{\bullet}_{f_{1},{\rm BM}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X_{1}}}B^{-1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\vartheta}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{V^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{R^{p+q}f_{1\ast}({\rm Gr}_{F}^{p-1}\Omega^{\bullet}_{f_{1},{\rm BM}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X_{1}}}B^{-1})\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{V^{\prime}}}\Omega_{V^{\prime}}(\log D^{\prime}_{f})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{V^{\prime}}\otimes{\rm Id}}
\textstyle{R^{p+q}g_{1\ast}({\rm Gr}_{F}^{p}\Omega^{\bullet}_{g_{1},{\rm BM}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\theta_{g_{1}}}
\textstyle{R^{p+q}g_{1\ast}({\rm Gr}_{F}^{p-1}\Omega^{\bullet}_{g_{1},{\rm BM}})\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{V^{\prime}}}\Omega_{V^{\prime}}(\log D^{\prime}_{g}),}

where
\vartheta
is the coboundary map.
Combining this commutative diagram with the isomorphism (
4.2
), we obtain a commutative diagram:

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 70.24289pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\cr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-70.24289pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise BM}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X_{1}}}B^{-1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 76.8284pt\raise 5.43056pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.43056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\vartheta}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 94.24289pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-16.32pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.6014pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\eta\iota_{V^{\prime}}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.91779pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 94.24289pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{R^{p+q}f_{1\ast}({\rm Gr}_{F}^{p-1}\Omega^{\bullet}_{f_{1},{\rm D^{\prime}_{f})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 205.0832pt\raise-16.32pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.525pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\eta\iota_{V^{\prime}}\otimes{\rm Id}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 205.0832pt\raise-23.99998pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise
