3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\widetilde{H}^{p,q}_{g^{o},{\rm BM}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 73.27982pt\raise-27.20946pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.43056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\theta}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 138.7333pt\raise-32.64001pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 138.7333pt\raise-32.64001pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\widetilde{H}^{p-1,q+1}_{g^{o},{\rm BM}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{V^{\prime}}}\Omega_{V^{\prime}}(\log D_{g}\cap V^{\prime})}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

For every
p,q
, we define

\displaystyle G^{p,q}:={\rm Im}\left(\eta\iota_{V^{\prime}}:R^{p+q}f_{1\ast}({\rm Gr}_{F}^{p}\Omega^{\bullet}_{f_{1},{\rm BM}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X_{1}}}B^{-1})\to\widetilde{H}^{p,q}_{g^{o},{\rm BM}}\right).

4.3.3.
Proof of Theorem
4.1

Theorem
4.1
is the consequence of the following two lemmas.

Lemma 4.2
.

(4.9)

\displaystyle\theta(G^{p,q})\subset G^{p-1,q+1}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{V^{\prime}}}\Omega_{V^{\prime}}(\log D^{\prime}_{f}).

Proof.

The inclusion (
4.9
) holds over
V^{\prime}\backslash D^{\prime}_{f}
due to the commutative diagram (
4.3.2
). Let
x\in D^{\prime}_{f}
, and let
z_{1},\dots,z_{d}
denote holomorphic local coordinates at
x
such that
D^{\prime}_{f}=\{z_{1}=0\}
. Define

\displaystyle\xi_{i}:=\begin{cases}z_{i}\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{i}},&i=1,\\
\frac{\partial}{\partial z_{i}},&i=2,\dots,d.\end{cases}

Let
v\in R^{p+q}f_{1\ast}({\rm Gr}_{F}^{p}\Omega^{\bullet}_{f_{1},{\rm BM}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X_{1}}}B^{-1})
. From (
4.3.2
), it follows that

\displaystyle\theta(\xi_{i})(\eta\iota_{V^{\prime}}(v))=\eta\iota_{V^{\prime}}\left(\vartheta(\xi_{i})(v)\right)\in{\rm Im}(\eta\iota_{V^{\prime}}),\quad\forall i=1,\dots,d.

This demonstrates that

\displaystyle\theta(\xi_{i})(G^{p,q})\subset G^{p-1,q+1},\quad\forall i=1,\dots,d.

Hence, the lemma is proved.
∎

Lemma 4.3
.

There is a natural inclusion
L\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{S}}\mathscr{O}_{S}(-D_{f})|_{V^{\prime}}\subset G^{n,0}
.

Proof.

According to (
3.3
), we have

\omega_{X_{1}/V^{\prime}}(\lceil\Delta|_{X_{1}}\rceil-f_{1}^{\ast}(D^{\prime}_{f}))\subset\omega_{X_{1}/V^{\prime}}(\lceil\Delta_{1}-f_{1}^{\ast}(D^{\prime}_{f})\rceil)\simeq F^{n}\Omega^{\bullet}_{f_{1},{\rm BM}}[n].

Consider the natural maps:

\displaystyle\beta:L\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{S}}\mathscr{O}_{S}(-D_{f})|_{V^{\prime}}\to f_{1\ast}(f_{1}^{\ast}(L|_{V^{\prime}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{V^{\prime}}}\mathscr{O}_{V^{\prime}}(-D^{\prime}_{f})))

\displaystyle\simeq f_{1\ast}(B^{-1}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X_{1}}}\omega_{X_{1}/V^{\prime}}(\lceil\Delta|_{X_{1}}\rceil-f_{1}^{\ast}(D^{\prime}_{f})))

\displaystyle\subset R^{n}f_{1\ast}(B^{-1}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X_{1}}}F^{n}\Omega^{\bullet}_{f_{1},{\rm BM}}),

and

\displaystyle\alpha:L\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{S}}\mathscr{O}_{S}(-D_{f})|_{V^{\prime}}\to f_{1\ast}(f_{1}^{\ast}(L|_{V^{\prime}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{V^{\prime}}}\mathscr{O}_{V^{\prime}}(-D^{\prime}_{f})))

\displaystyle\subset f_{1\ast}(B^{-1}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X_{1}}}\omega_{X_{1}/V^{\prime}}(\lceil\Delta|_{X_{1}}\rceil))

\displaystyle\to g_{1\ast}(\omega_{Z_{1}/V^{\prime}}(\lceil\Delta_{Z}|_{Z_{1}}\rceil))\subset\widetilde{H}^{n,0}_{g^{o},{\rm BM}}|_{V^{\prime}},

where the last inclusion follows from Lemma
3.19
, along with the fact that
\Delta_{Z}
is
g
-horizontal. By construction, it holds that
\iota_{V^{\prime}}\circ\beta=\alpha
. Consequently,
{\rm Im}(\alpha)\subset G^{n,0}
.

Now consider the composition of maps:

\displaystyle\alpha|_{S^{o}}:L|_{S^{o}}\to f^{o}_{\ast}(f^{o\ast}L|_{S^{o}})\simeq f^{o}_{\ast}(B^{-1}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X^{o}}}\omega_{X^{o}/S^{o}}(\lceil\Delta|_{X^{o}}\rceil))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\iota_{V^{\prime}}|_{S^{o}}}}{{\to}}H^{n,0}_{g^{o},{\rm BM}}.

Observe that
\alpha
is the multiplication by
s^{\frac{1}{k}}
, combined with the pullback of differential forms
\omega_{X/S}\to(\varpi\mu)_{\ast}(\omega_{Z/X})
. For each local generator
e
of
L
,
\alpha(e)
must be nonzero on some component of
Z
. Consequently,
\alpha|_{S^{o}}
is a nonzero map.
Since
\widetilde{H}^{n,0}_{g^{o},{\rm BM}}
is locally free, it follows that
\alpha
is injective. Therefore, we obtain the claimed injective morphism
L\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{S}}\mathscr{O}_{S}(-D_{f})|_{V^{\prime}}\subset G^{n,0}
as stated in the lemma. ∎

Now we proceed to finalize the proof of Theorem
4.1
. To summarize, we have constructed an injective morphism

\alpha:L\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{S}}\mathscr{O}_{S}(-D_{f})|_{V^{\prime}}\to\widetilde{H}^{n,0}_{g^{o},{\rm BM}}|_{V^{\prime}}

such that the image
{\rm Im}(\alpha)
is contained in a Higgs subsheaf
(\oplus_{p=0}^{n}G^{p,n-p},\theta)
of
(\widetilde{H}^{n}_{g^{o},{\rm BM}},\theta)|_{V^{\prime}}
, satisfying

\displaystyle\theta(G^{p,n-p})\subset G^{p-1,n-p+1}\otimes\Omega_{V^{\prime}}(\log D^{\prime}_{f})

for every
p=0,\cdots,n
.
Since
\widetilde{H}^{n}_{g^{o},{\rm BM}}
is locally free and
{\rm codim}_{V}(V\setminus V^{\prime})\geq 2
, the morphism
\alpha
extends to an injective morphism

\alpha:L\otimes\mathscr{O}_{S}(-D_{f})|_{V}\to\widetilde{H}^{n,0}_{g^{o},{\rm BM}}

that satisfies the assertion of the theorem.

5.
Admissible families of stable minimal models

5.1.
Stable minimal models and their moduli

We now review the main results from
[
4
]
that will be utilized in the subsequent sections. For the purposes of this article, all schemes are defined over
{\rm Spec}(\mathbb{C})
. A
stable minimal model
is a triple
(X,B),A
, where
X
is a reduced, connected, projective scheme of finite type over
{\rm Spec}(\mathbb{C})
, and
A,B\geq 0
are
\mathbb{Q}
-divisors satisfying the following conditions:

•

(X,B)
is a projective, connected slc (semi-log-canonical) pair,

•

K_{X}+B
is semi-ample,

•

K_{X}+B+tA
is ample for some
t>0
, and

•

(X,B+tA)
is slc for some
t>0
.

Let

d\in\mathbb{N},\,c\in\mathbb{Q}^{\geq 0},\,\Gamma\subset\mathbb{Q}^{>0}\text{ a finite set, and }\sigma\in\mathbb{Q}[t].

A
(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)
-stable minimal model is a stable minimal model
(X,B),A
satisfying the following conditions:

•

\dim X=d
,

•

the coefficients of
A
and
B
belong to
c\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0}
,

•

{\rm vol}(A|_{F})\in\Gamma
, where
F
is any general fiber of the fibration
f:X\to Z
determined by
K_{X}+B
, and

•

{\rm vol}(K_{X}+B+tA)=\sigma(t)
for
0\leq t\ll 1
.

Let
S
be a reduced scheme over
{\rm Spec}(\mathbb{C})
. A family of
(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)
-stable minimal models over
S
consists of a projective morphism
X\to S
of schemes and
\mathbb{Q}
-divisors
A
and
B
on
X
, satisfying the following conditions:

•

(X,B+tA)\to S
is a locally stable family (i.e.,
K_{X/S}+B+tA
is
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier) for every sufficiently small rational number
t\geq 0
,

•

A=cN
,
B=cD
, where
N,D\geq 0
are relative Mumford divisors, and

•

(X_{s},B_{s}),A_{s}
is a
(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)
-stable minimal model for each point
s\in S
.

Let
{\rm Sch}_{\mathbb{C}}^{\rm red}
denote the category of reduced schemes defined over
{\rm Spec}(\mathbb{C})
. Define

\mathscr{M}^{\rm red}_{\rm slc}(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma):S\mapsto\{\text{families of }(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)\text{-stable minimal models over }S\},

a functor of groupoids over
{\rm Sch}_{\mathbb{C}}^{\rm red}
.

Theorem 5.1

(Birkar
[
4
]
)
.

There exists a proper Deligne-Mumford stack
\mathscr{M}_{\rm slc}(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)
over
{\rm Spec}(\mathbb{C})
such that the following properties hold:

•

\mathscr{M}_{\rm slc}(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)|_{{\rm Sch}_{\mathbb{C}}^{\rm red}}=\mathscr{M}^{\rm red}_{\rm slc}(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)
as functors of groupoids.

•

\mathscr{M}_{\rm slc}(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)
admits a projective good coarse moduli space
M_{\rm slc}(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)
.

Proof.

See the proof of
[
4
, Theorem 1.14]
. Using the notations in
[
4
, §10.7]
, we have

\mathscr{M}_{\rm slc}(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)=\left[M_{\rm slc}^{e}(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma,a,r,\mathbb{P}^{n})/{\rm PGL}_{n+1}(\mathbb{C})\right],

where the right-hand side denotes the stacky quotient.
∎

5.2.
Polarization on
M_{\rm slc}(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)

In this section, we consider certain natural ample
\mathbb{Q}
-line bundles on
M_{\rm slc}(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)
. Their constructions are implicitly described in the proof of
[
4
, Theorem 1.14]
, relying on Kollár’s ampleness criterion
[
28
]
.

Fix the data
d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma
. Since
\mathscr{M}_{\rm slc}(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)
is of finite type, there exist constants

(a,r,j)\in\mathbb{Q}^{\geq 0}\times(\mathbb{Z}^{>0})^{2},

depending only on
d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma
, such that every
(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)
-stable minimal model
(X,B),A
satisfies the following conditions (cf.
[
4
, Lemma 10.2]
):

•

(X,B+aA)
is an slc pair,

•

r(K_{X}+B+aA)
is a very ample integral Cartier divisor with

H^{i}(X,\mathscr{O}_{X}(kr(K_{X}+B+aA)))=0,\quad\forall i>0,\forall k>0,

•

the embedding
X\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}(H^{0}(X,r(K_{X}+B+aA)))
is defined by equations of degree
\leq j
, and

•

the multiplication map

S^{j}(H^{0}(X,\mathscr{O}_{X}(r(K_{X}+B+aA))))\to H^{0}(X,\mathscr{O}_{X}(jr(K_{X}+B+aA)))

is surjective.

Definition 5.2
.

A tuple
(a,r,j)\in\mathbb{Q}^{\geq 0}\times(\mathbb{Z}^{>0})^{2}
that satisfies the conditions above is referred to as a
(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)
-polarization datum
.

Let
(a,r,j)\in\mathbb{Q}^{\geq 0}\times(\mathbb{Z}^{>0})^{2}
be a
(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)
-polarization datum. Let
(X,B),A\to S
be a family of
(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)
-stable minimal models. Then
f_{\ast}(r(K_{X/S}+B+aA))
is locally free and commutes with arbitrary base changes. Therefore, the assignment

f:(X,B),A\to S\in\mathscr{M}_{\rm slc}(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)(S)\mapsto f_{\ast}(r(K_{X/S}+B+aA))

defines a locally free coherent sheaf on the stack
\mathscr{M}_{\rm slc}(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)
, denoted by
\Lambda_{a,r}
. Let
\lambda_{a,r}:=\det(\Lambda_{a,r})
. Since
\mathscr{M}_{\rm slc}(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)
is Deligne-Mumford, some power
\lambda_{a,r}^{\otimes k}
descends to a line bundle on
M_{\rm slc}(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)
. For this reason, we regard
\lambda_{a,r}
as a
\mathbb{Q}
-line bundle on
M_{\rm slc}(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)
.

Proposition 5.3
.

Let
(a,r,j)\in\mathbb{Q}^{\geq 0}\times(\mathbb{Z}^{>0})^{2}
be a
(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)
-polarization datum. Then
\lambda_{a,r}
is ample on
M_{\rm slc}(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)
.

Proof.

By the same arguments as in
[
28
, §2.9]
. It suffices to show that
f_{\ast}(r(K_{X/S}+B+aA))
is nef when
S
is a smooth projective curve. This was established by Fujino
[
17
]
and Kovács-Patakfalvi
[
31
]
.
∎

5.3.
Fiber product of stable families

Following Kollár
[
30
]
, a flat family of slc pairs
(X,\Delta)\to S
over a smooth base
S
is referred to as a
locally stable family
if
K_{X/S}+\Delta
is
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier.

Let
S
be a smooth variety, and let
f:(X,\Delta)\to S
be a locally stable family of slc pairs. Denote by
X^{[r]}_{S}
the
r
-fold fiber product
X\times_{S}X\times_{S}\cdots\times_{S}X
, and let
f^{[r]}:X^{[r]}_{S}\to S
be the projection map. Define

\Delta^{[r]}_{S}:=\sum_{i=1}^{r}p_{i}^{\ast}(\Delta),

where
p_{i}:X^{[r]}_{S}\to X
is the projection onto the
i
-th component. Then
f^{[r]}:(X^{[r]}_{S},\Delta^{[r]}_{S})\to S
is also a locally stable family of slc pairs (
[
52
, Corollary 4.3]
). According to
[
30
, Theorem 4.54]
,
(X^{[r]}_{S},\Delta^{[r]}_{S})
is an slc pair.

Let
\tau:(X^{(r)}_{S},\Delta^{(r)}_{S})\to(X^{[r]}_{S},\Delta^{[r]}_{S})
be a semi-log resolution, where
\Delta^{(r)}_{S}
is a simple normal crossing divisor determined by

(5.1)

\displaystyle\tau^{\ast}(K_{X^{[r]}_{S}}+\Delta^{[r]}_{S})=K_{X^{(r)}_{S}}+\Delta^{(r)}_{S}-E,

with
E
being an effective
\tau
-exceptional divisor that shares no common components with
\Delta^{(r)}_{S}
.

Lemma 5.4
.

Let
k\geq 1
such that
kK_{X/S}+k\Delta
is integral. Then the following statements hold:

(1)

\tau_{\ast}\left(\mathscr{O}_{X^{(r)}_{S}}(kK_{X^{(r)}_{S}/S}+k\Delta^{(r)}_{S})\right)\simeq\mathscr{O}_{X^{[r]}_{S}}(kK_{X^{[r]}_{S}/S}+k\Delta^{[r]}_{S})
.

(2)

f^{[r]}_{\ast}\left(\mathscr{O}_{X^{[r]}_{S}}(kK_{X^{[r]}_{S}/S}+k\Delta^{[r]}_{S})\right)
is a reflexive sheaf.

(3)

f^{[r]}_{\ast}\left(\mathscr{O}_{X^{[r]}_{S}}(kK_{X^{[r]}_{S}/S}+k\Delta^{[r]}_{S})\right)\simeq(f_{\ast}\left(\mathscr{O}_{X}(kK_{X/S}+k\Delta)\right)^{\otimes r})^{\vee\vee}
.

Proof.

Observe that
\tau_{\ast}(\mathscr{O}_{X^{(r)}_{S}}(kE))\simeq\mathscr{O}_{X^{[r]}_{S}}
, as
X^{[r]}_{S}
satisfies the
S_{2}
condition and
E
is
\tau
-exceptional with
\mathrm{codim}_{X^{[r]}_{S}}(\tau(E))\geq 2
. Consequently, the first assertion follows from (
5.1
) and the projection formula.

For the second assertion, it suffices to show that any section of
f^{[r]}_{\ast}\left(\mathscr{O}_{X^{[r]}_{S}}(kK_{X^{[r]}_{S}/S}+k\Delta^{[r]}_{S})\right)
extends across an arbitrary locus of codimension
\geq 2
. Let
U\subset S
be an open subset, and let
Z\subset U
be a Zariski closed subset of codimension
\geq 2
. Let

s\in\Gamma\left(U\setminus Z,f^{[r]}_{\ast}\left(\mathscr{O}_{X^{[r]}_{S}}(kK_{X^{[r]}_{S}/S}+k\Delta^{[r]}_{S})\right)\right)=\Gamma\left((f^{[r]})^{-1}(U\setminus Z),\mathscr{O}_{X^{[r]}_{S}}(kK_{X^{[r]}_{S}/S}+k\Delta^{[r]}_{S})\right).

Since
f
is flat, so is
f^{[r]}
. Hence
(f^{[r]})^{-1}(Z)
has codimension
\geq 2
in
(f^{[r]})^{-1}(U)
. Since
X^{[r]}_{S}
satisfies the
S_{2}
condition and
\mathscr{O}_{X^{[r]}_{S}}(kK_{X^{[r]}_{S}/S}+k\Delta^{[r]}_{S})
is invertible, there exists

\widetilde{s}\in\Gamma\left(U,f^{[r]}_{\ast}\left(\mathscr{O}_{X^{[r]}_{S}}(kK_{X^{[r]}_{S}/S}+k\Delta^{[r]}_{S})\right)\right)=\Gamma\left((f^{[r]})^{-1}(U),\mathscr{O}_{X^{[r]}_{S}}(kK_{X^{[r]}_{S}/S}+k\Delta^{[r]}_{S})\right)

that extends
s
. This proves Assertion (2).

Finally, we prove the last assertion. Observe that

\mathscr{O}_{X^{[r]}_{S}}(kK_{X^{[r]}_{S}/S}+k\Delta^{[r]}_{S})\simeq\bigotimes_{i=1}^{r}p_{i}^{\ast}\mathscr{O}_{X}(kK_{X/S}+k\Delta),

where
p_{i}:X^{[r]}_{S}\to X
denotes the projection onto the
i
-th component. Let
U\subset S
be the largest open subset such that both
f^{[r]}_{\ast}\left(\mathscr{O}_{X^{[r]}_{S}}(kK_{X^{[r]}_{S}/S}+k\Delta^{[r]}_{S})\right)
and
f_{\ast}\left(\mathscr{O}_{X}(kK_{X/S}+k\Delta)\right)
are locally free on
U
. Since the relevant sheaves are torsion-free, the complement
S\setminus U
has codimension
\geq 2
. By the flat base change theorem, we obtain

f^{[r]}_{\ast}\left(\mathscr{O}_{X^{[r]}_{S}}(kK_{X^{[r]}_{S}/S}+k\Delta^{[r]}_{S})\right)\big|_{U}\simeq f_{\ast}\left(\mathscr{O}_{X}(kK_{X/S}+k\Delta)\right)^{\otimes r}\big|_{U}.

Since
f^{[r]}_{\ast}\left(\mathscr{O}_{X^{[r]}_{S}}(kK_{X^{[r]}_{S}/S}+k\Delta^{[r]}_{S})\right)
and
\left(f_{\ast}\left(\mathscr{O}_{X}(kK_{X/S}+k\Delta)\right)^{\otimes r}\right)^{\vee\vee}
are reflexive, this completes the proof of Assertion (3).
∎

We will also need the following lemma (originated to Viehweg
[
51
]
) in the sequel.

Lemma 5.5
.

Let
g:Y\to S
be a projective surjective morphism from a simple normal crossing variety
Y
to a smooth variety
S
. Let
\tau:S^{\prime}\to S
be a flat projective surjective morphism from a smooth variety
S^{\prime}
. Let
\Delta\geq 0
be an integral divisor on
Y
. Consider the following commutative diagram:

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 7.01389pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-7.01389pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-15.98114pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{g}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.68451pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 31.01389pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{Y^{\prime\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 13.86475pt\raise 6.57835pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.21725pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rho^{\prime}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 7.0139pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 40.58333pt\raise-15.98114pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.21725pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{g^{\prime\prime}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 40.58333pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 74.15277pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{Y^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 56.50189pt\raise 5.1875pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\rho}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 50.15279pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 60.04456pt\raise-22.55948pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.21725pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{g^{\prime}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 48.34027pt\raise-26.05368pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern-6.35416pt\raise-31.96228pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{S}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 32.8264pt\raise-31.96228pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{S^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 15.44774pt\raise-27.45534pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tau}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 6.35416pt\raise-31.96228pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 79.56944pt\raise-31.96228pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces,

where
g^{\prime\prime}:Y^{\prime\prime}\to S^{\prime}
is the base change of
g
, and
\rho:Y^{\prime}\to Y^{\prime\prime}
is a semi-log resolution of singularities. Then, for every
r\geq 1
, there exists a natural inclusion

g^{\prime}_{\ast}\left(\mathscr{O}_{Y^{\prime}}(rK_{Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}+(\rho^{\prime}\rho)^{\ast}\Delta)\right)\subset\tau^{\ast}g_{\ast}\left(\mathscr{O}_{Y}(rK_{Y/S}+\Delta)\right).

Proof.

Case I:
Y
is irreducible.

Since
g
is a Gorenstein morphism, we have
\rho^{\prime\ast}(K_{Y/S})\simeq K_{Y^{\prime\prime}/S^{\prime}}
, where
K_{Y^{\prime\prime}/S^{\prime}}
denotes the Cartier divisor associated with the invertible relative dualizing sheaf of
Y^{\prime\prime}/S^{\prime}
. Consequently, it follows that
\rho^{\prime\ast}(rK_{Y/S}+\Delta)\simeq rK_{Y^{\prime\prime}/S^{\prime}}+\rho^{\prime\ast}(\Delta)
. By invoking the flat base change theorem, we deduce the following isomorphism:

(5.6)

\displaystyle\tau^{\ast}g_{\ast}\left(\mathscr{O}_{Y}(rK_{Y/S}+\Delta)\right)\simeq g^{\prime\prime}_{\ast}\left(\mathscr{O}_{Y^{\prime\prime}}(rK_{Y^{\prime\prime}/S^{\prime}}+\rho^{\prime\ast}(\Delta))\right).

According to
[
18
, Lemma 3.1.19]
, the trace map induces an injective morphism:

\rho_{\ast}\left(\mathscr{O}_{Y^{\prime}}(rK_{Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}})\right)\to\mathscr{O}_{Y^{\prime\prime}}(rK_{Y^{\prime\prime}/S^{\prime}}).

This further implies an injective morphism:

\rho_{\ast}\left(\mathscr{O}_{Y^{\prime}}(rK_{Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}+(\rho^{\prime}\rho)^{\ast}(\Delta))\right)\to\mathscr{O}_{Y^{\prime\prime}}(rK_{Y^{\prime\prime}/S^{\prime}}+\rho^{\prime\ast}(\Delta)).

Consequently, there exists a natural injective morphism:

\displaystyle g^{\prime}_{\ast}\left(\mathscr{O}_{Y^{\prime}}(rK_{Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}+(\rho^{\prime}\rho)^{\ast}(\Delta))\right)\to g^{\prime\prime}_{\ast}\left(\mathscr{O}_{Y^{\prime\prime}}(rK_{Y^{\prime\prime}/S^{\prime}}+\rho^{\prime\ast}(\Delta))\right).

Combining this result with (
5.6
), we obtain the desired inclusion.

Case II:
Y
is reducible.

Let
\varrho:Y^{v}\to Y
denote the normalization, and let
D^{v}\subset Y^{v}
represent the conductor divisor. Then
Y^{v}
is smooth (possibly with many components),
D^{v}
is a simple normal crossing divisor on
Y^{v}
, and there exists an inclusion:

\displaystyle g_{\ast}\left(\mathscr{O}_{Y}(rK_{Y/S}+\Delta)\right)\subseteq(g\varrho)_{\ast}\left(\mathscr{O}_{Y^{v}}(rK_{Y^{v}/S}+rD^{v}+\varrho^{*}(\Delta))\right).

Let
\eta:\widetilde{D}\to D^{v}
denote the normalization of
D^{v}
, and let

\displaystyle{\rm Res}_{\widetilde{D}}:\mathscr{O}_{Y^{v}}(rK_{Y^{v}/S}+rD^{v}+\varrho^{*}(\Delta))\to\mathscr{O}_{\widetilde{D}}(rK_{\widetilde{D}/S}+\eta^{\ast}(\varrho^{*}(\Delta)|_{D^{v}}))

represent the residue map (cf.
[
29
, §4.1]
). Let
\alpha:\widetilde{D}\to\widetilde{D}
denote the involution over
D^{v}
(cf.
[
29
, §5.2]
). Consequently, one obtains a morphism:

\displaystyle(g\varrho)_{\ast}({\rm Res}_{\widetilde{D}}-(-1)^{r}\alpha^{\ast}{\rm Res}_{\widetilde{D}}):

\displaystyle(g\varrho)_{\ast}\left(\mathscr{O}_{Y^{v}}(rK_{Y^{v}/S}+rD^{v}+\varrho^{*}(\Delta))\right)

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle(g\varrho\eta)_{\ast}\left(\mathscr{O}_{\widetilde{D}}(rK_{\widetilde{D}/S}+\eta^{\ast}(\varrho^{*}(\Delta)|_{D^{v}}))\right).

According to
[
29
, Proposition 5.8]
, it follows that:

(5.7)

\displaystyle g_{\ast}\left(\mathscr{O}_{Y}(rK_{Y/S}+\Delta)\right)={\rm Ker}\left((g\varrho)_{\ast}({\rm Res}_{\widetilde{D}}-(-1)^{r}\alpha^{\ast}{\rm Res}_{\widetilde{D}})\right).

Similarly, let
\varrho^{\prime}:Y^{\prime v}\to Y^{\prime}
denote the normalization, and let
D^{\prime v}\subset Y^{\prime v}
represent the conductor divisor. Let
\eta^{\prime}:\widetilde{D^{\prime}}\to D^{\prime v}
denote the normalization of
D^{\prime v}
. Let
\alpha^{\prime}:\widetilde{D^{\prime}}\to\widetilde{D^{\prime}}
denote the involution over
D^{\prime v}
. Consequently, one obtains a morphism:

\displaystyle(g^{\prime}\varrho^{\prime})_{\ast}({\rm Res}_{\widetilde{D^{\prime}}}-(-1)^{r}\alpha^{\prime\ast}{\rm Res}_{\widetilde{D^{\prime}}}):

\displaystyle(g^{\prime}\varrho^{\prime})_{\ast}\left(\mathscr{O}_{Y^{\prime v}}(rK_{Y^{\prime v}/S^{\prime}}+rD^{\prime v}+(\rho^{\prime}\rho\varrho^{\prime})^{*}(\Delta))\right)

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle(g^{\prime}\varrho^{\prime}\eta^{\prime})_{\ast}\left(\mathscr{O}_{\widetilde{D^{\prime}}}(rK_{\widetilde{D^{\prime}}/S^{\prime}}+\eta^{\prime\ast}((\rho^{\prime}\rho\varrho^{\prime})^{*}(\Delta)|_{D^{\prime v}}))\right).

It follows that:

(5.8)

\displaystyle g^{\prime}_{\ast}\left(\mathscr{O}_{Y^{\prime}}(rK_{Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}+(\rho^{\prime}\rho)^{\ast}\Delta)\right)={\rm Ker}\left((g^{\prime}\varrho^{\prime})_{\ast}({\rm Res}_{\widetilde{D^{\prime}}}-(-1)^{r}\alpha^{\prime\ast}{\rm Res}_{\widetilde{D^{\prime}}})\right).

By applying Step 1 to each irreducible component of
Y
and
\widetilde{D}
, we obtain the following commutative diagram:

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 104.80087pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-100.22371pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(g^{\prime}\varrho^{\prime})_{\ast}\left(\mathscr{O}_{Y^{\prime v}}(rK_{Y^{\prime v}/S^{\prime}}+rD^{\prime v}+(\rho^{\prime}\rho\varrho^{\prime})^{*}(\Delta))\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-104.80087pt\raise-16.00891pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.49225pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{(g^{\prime}\varrho^{\prime})_{\ast}({\rm Res}_{\widetilde{D^{\prime}}}-(-1)^{r}\alpha^{\prime\ast}{\rm Res}_{\widetilde{D^{\prime}}})}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 124.96063pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 124.96063pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\tau^{\ast}(g\varrho)_{\ast}\left(\mathscr{O}_{Y^{v}}(rK_{Y^{v}/S}+rD^{v}+\varrho^{*}(\Delta))\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 105.23378pt\raise-16.00891pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.26654pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\tau^{\ast}(g\varrho)_{\ast}({\rm Res}_{\widetilde{D}}-(-1)^{r}\alpha^{\ast}{\rm Res}_{\widetilde{D}})}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 210.28847pt\raise-24.01782pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-100.74234pt\raise-32.01782pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(g^{\prime}\varrho^{\prime}\eta^{\prime})_{\ast}\left(\mathscr{O}_{\widetilde{D^{\prime}}}(rK_{\widetilde{D^{\prime}}/S^{\prime}}+\eta^{\prime\ast}((\rho^{\prime}\rho\varrho^{\prime})^{*}(\Delta)|_{D^{\prime v}}))\right)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 124.74234pt\raise-32.01782pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 124.74234pt\raise-32.01782pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\tau^{\ast}(g\varrho\eta)_{\ast}\left(\mathscr{O}_{\widetilde{D}}(rK_{\widetilde{D}/S}+\eta^{\ast}(\varrho^{*}(\Delta)|_{D^{v}}))\right)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

Since
\tau
is flat, by taking the kernels of the vertical morphisms, one obtains a morphism:

\displaystyle{\rm Ker}\left((g^{\prime}\varrho^{\prime})_{\ast}({\rm Res}_{\widetilde{D^{\prime}}}-(-1)^{r}\alpha^{\prime\ast}{\rm Res}_{\widetilde{D^{\prime}}})\right)\to

\displaystyle{\rm Ker}\left(\tau^{\ast}(g\varrho)_{\ast}({\rm Res}_{\widetilde{D}}-(-1)^{r}\alpha^{\ast}{\rm Res}_{\widetilde{D}})\right)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\tau^{\ast}{\rm Ker}\left((g\varrho)_{\ast}({\rm Res}_{\widetilde{D}}-(-1)^{r}\alpha^{\ast}{\rm Res}_{\widetilde{D}})\right).

Combining this result with (
5.7
) and (
5.8
), we obtain the desired morphism:

\beta:g^{\prime}_{\ast}\left(\mathscr{O}_{Y^{\prime}}(rK_{Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}+(\rho^{\prime}\rho)^{\ast}\Delta)\right)\to\tau^{\ast}g_{\ast}\left(\mathscr{O}_{Y}(rK_{Y/S}+\Delta)\right).

By construction,
\beta
is an isomorphism over
\tau^{-1}(S^{o})
, where
S^{o}\subset S
is the dense Zariski open subset over which
\tau
is smooth and
g:(Y,\Delta)\to S
is a simple normal crossing family. Consequently,
\beta
is an injective morphism since
g^{\prime}_{\ast}\left(\mathscr{O}_{Y^{\prime}}(rK_{Y^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}+(\rho^{\prime}\rho)^{\ast}\Delta)\right)
is torsion-free.
∎

5.4.
Higgs sheaves associated to an admissible stable family

The objective of this section is to define the notion of an admissible family of stable minimal models and to construct the Viehweg-Zuo Higgs sheaf associated with such families (Theorem
5.10
). Intuitively, the admissibility condition implies that the family admits an equisingular birational model.

Definition 5.6
.

A
semi-pair

(X,\Delta)
consists of a reduced scheme of finite type over
{\rm Spec}(\mathbb{C})
, of pure dimension, together with a
\mathbb{Q}
-divisor
\Delta\geq 0
on
X
, satisfying the following conditions:

(1)

X
is an
S_{2}
-scheme with nodal singularities in codimension one,

(2)

no component of
{\rm Supp}(\Delta)
is contained in the singular locus of
X
,

(3)

K_{X}+\Delta
is
\mathbb{Q}
-Cartier.

A
projective semi-pair
is a semi-pair
(X,\Delta)
where
X
is a projective scheme.

Definition 5.7
.

Let
(X,\Delta)
be a projective semi-pair. A morphism between projective semi-pairs
\pi:(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})\to(X,\Delta)
is said to be a
semi-log resolution
if
\pi:X^{\prime}\to X
is a semi-log resolution of singularities (
[
3
]
or
[
29
, §10.4]
) such that there exists a
\pi
-exceptional
\mathbb{Q}
-divisor
E\geq 0
on
X^{\prime}
satisfying the relation
K_{X^{\prime}}+\Delta^{\prime}=\pi^{\ast}(K_{X}+\Delta)+E
.

To provide a precise definition of an admissible family, we extend Fujino’s concept of B-birational maps
[
16
, Definition 1.5]
, which naturally generalizes birational maps to the context of log pairs.

Definition 5.8
.

Let
(X_{1},\Delta_{1})
and
(X_{2},\Delta_{2})
be two projective semi-pairs over a variety
S
. A rational map
f:(X_{1},\Delta_{1})\dasharrow(X_{2},\Delta_{2})
over
S
is said to be a
log birational map over
S
if there exists a common semi-log resolution
\pi_{i}:(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})\to(X_{i},\Delta_{i})
,
i=1,2
, over
S
such that
f=\pi_{2}\circ\pi_{1}^{-1}
. We say that a morphism
f:(X,\Delta)\to S
admits a
simple normal crossing log birational model
if there exists a simple normal crossing family
f^{\prime}:(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})\to S
(Definition
2.1
) and a log birational map
(X,\Delta)\dasharrow(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
over
S
.

Definition 5.9
.

Let
f:(X,B),A\to S
be a family of stable minimal models over a variety
S
. The morphism
f
is said to be
birationally admissible
if
(X,B+A)\to S
admits a log smooth log birational model.

A typical example of an admissible family of stable minimal models is a family
f:(X,B),A\to S
such that
(X,B+A)\to S
admits a simultaneous resolution. More precisely, there exists a semi-log resolution
(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})\to(X,B+A)
satisfying the following conditions:
(X^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})\to S
is a simple normal crossing family, and for each
s\in S
, the induced morphism
(X^{\prime}_{s},\Delta^{\prime}_{s})\to(X_{s},B_{s}+A_{s})
is a semi-log resolution.

Theorem 5.10
.

Let
f^{o}:(X^{o},B^{o}),A^{o}\to S^{o}
be a birationally admissible family of
(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)
-stable minimal models over a smooth quasi-projective variety
S^{o}
, defining a generically finite morphism
\xi^{o}:S^{o}\to M_{\rm slc}(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)
. Let
S
be a smooth projective variety containing
S^{o}
as a Zariski open subset such that
D:=S\setminus S^{o}
is a reduced simple normal crossing divisor, and
\xi^{o}
extends to a morphism
\xi:S\to M_{\rm slc}(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)
. Let
\mathscr{L}
be a line bundle on
S
. Then there exist the following data:

(1)

An algebraic subset
Z\subset S
of codimension
\geq 2
such that
(U:=S\setminus Z,U\cap D)
is a log smooth pair, and a simple normal crossing divisor
E\subset U
containing
U\cap D
.

(2)

A log smooth family
h_{0}:(Y,\Delta_{Y})\to U\setminus E
induces a graded
\mathbb{Q}
-polarized admissible variation of mixed Hodge structures
\mathbb{V}^{w}_{h_{0},{\rm BM}}
on
U\setminus E
, which in turn gives rise to the lower canonical logarithmic Higgs bundle
(\widetilde{H}=\bigoplus_{p=0}^{w}\widetilde{H}^{p,w-p},\theta)
on
U
(Definition
3.17
).

These data satisfy the following conditions:

(1)

There exists a natural inclusion
\mathscr{L}|_{U}\subset\widetilde{H}^{w,0}
.

(2)

Let
(\bigoplus_{p=0}^{w}L^{p},\theta)
be the logarithmic Higgs subsheaf generated by
L^{0}:=\mathscr{L}|_{U}
, where
L^{p}\subset\widetilde{H}^{w-p,p}
. Then the logarithmic Higgs field

\theta:L^{p}\to L^{p+1}\otimes\Omega_{U}(\log E)

is holomorphic over
U\setminus D
for each
0\leq p<w
, i.e.,

\theta(L^{p})\subset L^{p+1}\otimes\Omega_{U}(\log D\cap U),\quad p=0,\dots,w-1.

Proof.

Step 1: Compactify the family.

By the properness of
\mathscr{M}_{\rm slc}(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)
, we obtain the following constructions:

•

A generically finite, proper, and surjective morphism
\sigma:\widetilde{S}\to S
from a smooth projective variety
\widetilde{S}
such that
\sigma^{-1}(D)
is a simple normal crossing divisor. The morphism
\sigma
is constructed as a combination of smooth blow-ups, with centers lying over
S\setminus S^{o}
, and a finite flat morphism.

•

Let
\widetilde{S}^{o}:=\sigma^{-1}(S^{o})
and
\widetilde{X}^{o}:=\widetilde{S}^{o}\times_{S^{o}}X^{o}
. Denote by
\widetilde{A}^{o}
and
\widetilde{B}^{o}
the divisorial pullbacks of
A^{o}
and
B^{o}
on
\widetilde{X}^{o}
, respectively. There exists a completion
\widetilde{f}:(\widetilde{X},\widetilde{B}),\widetilde{A}\to\widetilde{S}
of the base change family
(\widetilde{X}^{o},\widetilde{B}^{o}),\widetilde{A}^{o}\to\widetilde{S}^{o}
such that
\widetilde{f}\in\mathscr{M}_{\rm slc}(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)(\widetilde{S})
.

Step 2: Take the log smooth birational models.
Let
(a,r,j)\in\mathbb{Q}^{\geq 0}\times(\mathbb{Z}^{>0})^{2}
be a
(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)
-polarization datum. In this case,
(X^{o},B^{o}+aA^{o})\to S^{o}
is a locally stable family. We denote
\Delta^{o}:=B^{o}+aA^{o}
.

Since
f^{o}
is birationally admissible, we may assume that
a
is sufficiently small such that the following diagrams exist:

where

and

are semi-log resolutions over

, and

is a simple normal crossing family over

. The diagram on the right is the base change of the diagram on the left.

Step 3: Viehweg’s fiber product trick.
Now we consider the fiber products of (
5.4
). Let
N>0
be an integer. Denote by
X_{1S^{o}}^{o[N]}
the main components of the
N
-fold fiber product
X_{1}^{o}\times_{S^{o}}\cdots\times_{S^{o}}X_{1}^{o}
. Let
p_{i}:X_{1S^{o}}^{o[N]}\to X_{1}^{o}
be the projection map onto the
i
-th component. We define
\Delta_{1}^{o[N]}:=\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}^{\ast}(\Delta_{1}^{o})
and similarly define
X^{o[N]}_{2S^{o}}
,
\Delta^{o[N]}_{2}
, etc.

Let
\varrho:X_{1S^{o}}^{o(N)}\to X_{1S^{o}}^{o[N]}
be a semi-log resolution of singularities such that
{\rm supp}(\varrho^{\ast}(\Delta_{1}^{o[N]}))\cup{\rm Exc}(\varrho)
is a simple normal crossing divisor. Let
E^{+},E^{-}\geq 0
be the simple normal crossing
\mathbb{Q}
-divisors on
X_{1S^{o}}^{o(N)}
determined by

\varrho^{\ast}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}p_{i}^{\ast}(K_{X^{o}_{1}/S^{o}}+\Delta_{1}^{o})\right)=K_{X_{1S^{o}}^{o(N)}/S^{o}}+E^{+}-E^{-},

where
E^{+}
and
E^{-}
have no common components and
E^{-}
is
\varrho
-exceptional. Then the composition morphisms

(X_{1S^{o}}^{o(N)},E^{+})\to(X^{o[N]}_{S^{o}},\Delta^{o[N]}),\quad(X_{1S^{o}}^{o(N)},E^{+})\to(X^{o[N]}_{2S^{o}},\Delta^{o[N]}_{2})

are log birational morphisms. Based on the discussions above, we obtain the following commutative diagram:

79.1876pt\raise-65.92947pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 79.1876pt\raise-65.92947pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{S^{o}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces,

where
\widetilde{X}^{o(N)}_{\widetilde{S}^{o}}
is a semi-log resolution of the main components of
X_{1S^{o}}^{o(N)}\times_{S^{o}}\widetilde{S}^{o}
, such that
\widetilde{E}^{+}:=h^{o\ast}(E^{+})
is a simple normal crossing divisor. Consequently,

(5.23)

\displaystyle\pi^{o\ast}(K_{\widetilde{X}^{o[N]}_{\widetilde{S}^{o}}/\widetilde{S}^{o}}+\widetilde{\Delta}^{o[N]})

\displaystyle=(h^{\prime}\pi^{o})^{\ast}(K_{X^{o[N]}_{S^{o}}/S^{o}}+\Delta^{o[N]})

\displaystyle=(\alpha h^{o})^{\ast}(K_{X^{o[N]}_{S^{o}}/S^{o}}+\Delta^{o[N]})

\displaystyle=h^{o\ast}(K_{X^{o(N)}_{1S^{o}}/S^{o}}+E^{+}-E^{\prime})\quad(E^{\prime}\textrm{ is }\alpha-\textrm{exceptional})

\displaystyle=K_{\widetilde{X}^{o(N)}_{\widetilde{S}^{o}}/\widetilde{S}^{o}}+\widetilde{E}^{+}-F^{o}

for some divisor
F^{o}\geq 0
.
Taking a completion of (
5.4
), we obtain the following diagram:

\textstyle{(\widetilde{Y}^{(N)},\widetilde{\Delta}^{(N)})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\scriptstyle{h}
\textstyle{(Y^{(N)}_{1},\Delta^{(N)}_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g_{1}}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{(Y^{(N)}_{2},\Delta^{(N)}_{2})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g_{2}}
\textstyle{(\widetilde{X}^{[N]}_{\widetilde{S}},\widetilde{\Delta}^{[N]})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{f}^{[N]}}
\textstyle{\widetilde{S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma}
\textstyle{S}

where the following conditions are satisfied:

•

Y^{(N)}_{2}
is a simple normal crossing projective variety such that
Y^{(N)}_{2}\to S
is a completion of
X^{o[N]}_{2S^{o}}\to S^{o}
, and
\Delta^{(N)}_{2}
, the closure of
\Delta^{o[N]}_{2}
in
Y^{(N)}_{2}
, is a simple normal crossing divisor;

•

Y^{(N)}_{1}
is a simple normal crossing projective variety such that
Y^{(N)}_{1}\to S
is a completion of
X^{o(N)}_{1S^{o}}\to S^{o}
. The divisor
\Delta^{(N)}_{1}\geq 0
contains
E^{+}
and is chosen such that
g
is a semi-log resolution;

•

\widetilde{Y}^{(N)}
is a simple normal crossing projective variety such that
\widetilde{Y}^{(N)}\to\widetilde{S}
is a completion of
\widetilde{X}^{o(N)}_{\widetilde{S}^{o}}\to\widetilde{S}^{o}
, and
(\widetilde{f}^{[N]}\pi)^{\ast}(D)\cup\overline{\widetilde{E}^{+}}
is a simple normal crossing divisor. The divisor
\widetilde{\Delta}^{(N)}\geq 0
is determined by

(5.30)

\displaystyle\pi^{\ast}(K_{\widetilde{X}_{\widetilde{S}}^{[N]}}+\widetilde{\Delta}^{[N]})=K_{\widetilde{Y}^{(N)}}+\widetilde{\Delta}^{(N)}-F,

where
F\geq 0
is a
\pi
-exceptional divisor that shares no common components with
\widetilde{\Delta}^{(N)}
.

Observe that

\widetilde{\Delta}^{(N)}|_{\widetilde{X}^{o(N)}_{\widetilde{S}^{o}}}\leq\widetilde{E}^{+}=h^{\ast}(\Delta_{1}^{(N)})|_{\widetilde{X}^{o(N)}_{\widetilde{S}^{o}}}

according to (
5.23
). Combining this with the fact that
(\widetilde{X}^{[N]}_{S},\widetilde{\Delta}^{[N]})
is an slc pair, we conclude that

(5.31)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Delta}^{(N)}\leq h^{\ast}(\Delta_{1}^{(N)})+(\sigma\widetilde{f}^{[N]}\pi)^{\ast}(D).

According to (
5.30
), we have

(5.32)

\displaystyle\widetilde{f}^{[N]}_{\ast}\left(\mathscr{O}_{\widetilde{X}^{[N]}_{\widetilde{S}}}(rK_{\widetilde{X}^{[N]}_{\widetilde{S}}/\widetilde{S}}+r\widetilde{\Delta}^{[N]})\right)\simeq(\widetilde{f}^{[N]}\pi)_{\ast}\left(\mathscr{O}_{\widetilde{Y}^{(N)}}(rK_{\widetilde{Y}^{(N)}/\widetilde{S}}+r\widetilde{\Delta}^{(N)})\right).

Since
g
is a semi-log resolution, there exists an isomorphism

(5.33)

\displaystyle g_{1\ast}\left(\mathscr{O}_{Y^{(N)}_{1}}(rK_{Y^{(N)}_{1}/S}+r\Delta^{(N)}_{1})\right)\simeq g_{2\ast}\left(\mathscr{O}_{Y^{(N)}_{2}}(rK_{Y^{(N)}_{2}/S}+r\Delta^{(N)}_{2})\right).

By Lemma
5.5
and (
5.31
), we obtain an inclusion

(5.34)

\displaystyle(\widetilde{f}^{[N]}\pi)_{\ast}\left(\mathscr{O}_{\widetilde{Y}^{(N)}}(rK_{\widetilde{Y}^{(N)}/\widetilde{S}}+r\widetilde{\Delta}^{(N)})\right)\otimes\sigma^{\ast}(I)\subset\sigma^{\ast}g_{1\ast}\left(\mathscr{O}_{Y^{(N)}_{1}}(rK_{Y^{(N)}_{1}/S}+r\Delta^{(N)}_{1}+rg_{1}^{\ast}(D))\right),

where
I
is an ideal sheaf on
S
whose co-support lies in the codimension
\geq 2
loci over which
\sigma
is not flat. Combining (
5.32
)–(
5.34
), we conclude that

(5.35)

\displaystyle\widetilde{f}^{[N]}_{\ast}\left(\mathscr{O}_{\widetilde{X}^{[N]}_{\widetilde{S}}}(rK_{\widetilde{X}^{[N]}_{\widetilde{S}}/\widetilde{S}}+r\widetilde{\Delta}^{[N]})\right)\otimes\sigma^{\ast}(I)\subset\sigma^{\ast}g_{2\ast}\left(\mathscr{O}_{Y^{(N)}_{2}}(rK_{Y^{(N)}_{2}/S}+r\Delta^{(N)}_{2}+rg_{2}^{\ast}(D))\right).

Last step.
We complete the proof by applying Theorem
4.1
. Let

W:=\widetilde{f}_{\ast}\left(\mathscr{O}_{\widetilde{X}}(rK_{\widetilde{X}/\widetilde{S}}+r\widetilde{B}+ra\widetilde{A})\right)\quad\text{and}\quad l:={\rm rank}(W).

By the construction of
\lambda_{a,r}
, we have

(\xi\circ\sigma)^{\ast}\lambda_{a,r}\simeq\det(W).

Since
\lambda_{a,r}
is ample (Proposition
5.3
) and
\xi\circ\sigma
is generically finite, it follows that
\det(W)
is big. Take an ample line bundle
M
on
S
such that there exists an inclusion

\sigma_{\ast}\mathscr{O}_{\widetilde{S}}\otimes M^{-1}\subset\mathscr{O}_{S}^{\oplus N^{\prime}}

for some
N^{\prime}>0
. Since
\det(W)
is big, there exists an inclusion

(5.36)

\displaystyle\sigma^{\ast}(\mathscr{L}^{\otimes r}\otimes\mathscr{O}_{S}(2rD)\otimes M)\subset\det(W)^{\otimes kr}

for some
k>0
.

Now consider the diagram (
5.4
) in the case where
N=klr
. By Lemma
5.4
, there exists a natural inclusion

\displaystyle\det(W)^{\otimes kr}\to\left(\widetilde{f}_{\ast}\left(\mathscr{O}_{\widetilde{X}}(rK_{\widetilde{X}/\widetilde{S}}+r\widetilde{\Delta})\right)^{\otimes klr}\right)^{\vee\vee}\simeq\widetilde{f}^{[klr]}_{\ast}\left(\mathscr{O}_{\widetilde{X}^{[klr]}_{\widetilde{S}}}(rK_{\widetilde{X}^{[klr]}_{\widetilde{S}}/\widetilde{S}}+r\widetilde{\Delta}^{[klr]})\right).

Combining this with (
5.35
) and (
5.36
), we deduce that

\sigma^{\ast}(\mathscr{L}^{\otimes r}\otimes\mathscr{O}_{S}(2rD)\otimes M\otimes I_{Z^{\prime}})\subset\det(W)^{\otimes kr}\otimes\sigma^{\ast}(I_{Z^{\prime}})\to\sigma^{\ast}g_{2\ast}\left(\mathscr{O}_{Y_{2}^{(klr)}}(rK_{Y_{2}^{(klr)}/S}+r\Delta_{2}^{(klr)}+rg_{2}^{\ast}(D))\right),

where
I_{Z^{\prime}}
is an ideal sheaf on
S
whose co-support
Z^{\prime}
lies in the codimension
\geq 2
loci over which
\sigma
is not flat. This induces a non-zero map

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}^{\otimes r}\otimes\mathscr{O}_{S}(rD)\otimes I_{Z^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\to g_{2\ast}\left(\mathscr{O}_{Y_{2}^{(klr)}}(rK_{Y_{2}^{(klr)}/S}+r\Delta_{2}^{(klr)})\right)\otimes\sigma_{\ast}\mathscr{O}_{\widetilde{S}}\otimes M^{-1}

\displaystyle\subset g_{2\ast}\left(\mathscr{O}_{Y_{2}^{(klr)}}(rK_{Y_{2}^{(klr)}/S}+r\Delta_{2}^{(klr)})\right)^{\oplus N^{\prime}}.

Hence, we obtain a non-zero map

(5.37)

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}^{\otimes r}\otimes\mathscr{O}_{S}(rD)\otimes I_{Z^{\prime}}\to g_{2\ast}\left(\mathscr{O}_{Y_{2}^{(klr)}}(rK_{Y_{2}^{(klr)}/S}+r\Delta_{2}^{(klr)})\right).

Applying Theorem
4.1
to the restriction of the family
g_{2}:(Y_{2}^{(klr)},\Delta_{2}^{(klr)})\to S
to
S\backslash Z^{\prime}
(which is a simple normal crossing family over
S^{o}
) and the line bundle
\mathscr{L}\otimes\mathscr{O}_{S}(D)|_{S\backslash Z^{\prime}}
, the theorem have been proven.
∎

6.
Hyperbolicity properties for admissible families of stable minimal models

Based on the constructions presented in the preceding sections (particularly Theorem
5.10
), we proceed to prove the stratified hyperbolicity property of the moduli stack
\mathscr{M}_{\rm slc}(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)
.

Theorem 6.1
.

Let
f^{o}:(X^{o},B^{o}),A^{o}\to S^{o}
be a birationally admissible family of
(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)
-stable minimal models over a smooth quasi-projective variety
S^{o}
, defining a generically finite morphism
\xi^{o}:S^{o}\to M_{\rm slc}(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)
. Let
S
be a smooth projective variety containing
S^{o}
as a Zariski open subset such that
D:=S\setminus S^{o}
is a divisor. Then the line bundle
\omega_{S}(D)
is big.

Proof.

Since the property of
\omega_{S}(D)
being big is independent of the choice of the compactification
S^{o}\subset S
, we may assume without loss of generality that
D
is a simple normal crossing divisor and the morphism
\xi^{o}
extends to a morphism
\xi:S\to M_{\rm slc}(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)
. Let
\mathscr{L}
be a big line bundle on
S
. By Theorem
5.10
, the following data exist:

(1)

An algebraic subset
Z\subset S
of codimension
\geq 2
such that
(U:=S\setminus Z,U\cap D)
is a log smooth pair, and a simple normal crossing divisor
E\subset U
containing
U\cap D
.

(2)

A graded
\mathbb{Q}
-polarized admissible variation of mixed Hodge structures on
U\setminus E
, with
(\widetilde{H}=\bigoplus_{p=0}^{w}\widetilde{H}^{p,w-p},\theta)
as its associated lower canonical logarithmic Higgs bundle on
U
.

These data satisfy the following conditions:

(1)

There exists a natural inclusion
\mathscr{L}|_{U}\subset\widetilde{H}^{w,0}
.

(2)

Let
(\bigoplus_{p=0}^{w}L^{p},\theta)
be the logarithmic Higgs subsheaf generated by
L^{0}:=\mathscr{L}|_{U}
, where
L^{p}\subset\widetilde{H}^{w-p,p}
. Then the logarithmic Higgs field

\theta:L^{p}\to L^{p+1}\otimes\Omega_{U}(\log E)

is holomorphic over
U\setminus D
for each
0\leq p<w
, i.e.,

\theta(L^{p})\subset L^{p+1}\otimes\Omega_{U}(\log D\cap U),\quad 0\leq p<w.

Consider the following diagram:

\displaystyle L^{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\theta}}{{\to}}L^{1}\otimes\Omega_{U}(\log D\cap U)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\theta\otimes{\rm Id}}}{{\to}}L^{2}\otimes\Omega_{U}^{\otimes 2}(\log D\cap U)\to\cdots.

Observe that there exists a minimal integer
n_{0}\leq w
such that
L^{0}
is mapped into

\ker\left(L^{n_{0}}\otimes\Omega_{U}^{\otimes n_{0}}(\log D\cap U)\to L^{n_{0}+1}\otimes\Omega_{U}^{\otimes n_{0}+1}(\log D\cap U)\right)\subset K\otimes\Omega_{U}^{\otimes n_{0}}(\log D\cap U),

where

K=\ker\left(\theta:\widetilde{H}^{w}\to\widetilde{H}^{w}\otimes\Omega_{U}(\log E)\right).

Since
n_{0}
is minimal and
K
is torsion-free, we obtain the inclusion

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}|_{U}\subset K\otimes\Omega_{U}^{\otimes n_{0}}(\log D\cap U).

This induces a nonzero morphism

\displaystyle\beta:\mathscr{L}|_{U}\otimes K^{\vee}\to\Omega_{U}^{\otimes n_{0}}(\log D\cap U).

Denote
r_{K}:=\mathrm{rank}(K)
, and taking
\otimes^{r_{K}}
on both sides yields a nonzero morphism

\displaystyle\beta:\mathscr{L}^{\otimes r_{K}}\otimes\det(j_{\ast}(K^{\vee}))

\displaystyle\subset\mathscr{L}^{\otimes r_{K}}\otimes(j_{\ast}(K^{\vee})^{\otimes r_{K}})^{\vee\vee}\to\Omega_{S}^{\otimes n_{0}r_{K}}(\log D),

where
j:U\to S
is the immersion. Since
K\subset\widetilde{H}^{w}
and
\theta(K)=0
, it follows that
-c_{1}(K)
is pseudo-effective by Proposition
3.18
. Moreover, since
\mathscr{L}
is big by assumption, the line bundle
\Omega_{S}^{\otimes n_{0}r_{K}}(\log D)
contains the big line bundle
\mathrm{Im}(\beta)
. This implies that
n_{0}>0
, and hence
\omega_{S}(D)
is big by
[
6
, Theorem 7.11]
.
∎

6.1.
Stratified hyperbolicity of
\mathscr{M}_{\rm slc}(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)

Every algebraic stack discussed in this section is defined over an algebraically closed field
k
of characteristic zero. For the purposes of this paper, we restrict our attention to Deligne-Mumford stacks (DM-stacks for short).

Let
\mathscr{X}
be a reduced, separated DM-stack of finite type over
{\rm Spec}(k)
. Let
\Delta
be an effective
\mathbb{Q}
-divisor on
\mathscr{X}
. Let
\pi:X_{0}\to\mathscr{X}
be an étale covering by a scheme
X_{0}
, and let
\Delta_{0}:=\pi^{\ast}(\Delta)
denote the pullback of
\Delta
via the étale map
\pi
. Such a pair
(X_{0},\Delta_{0})
is referred to as a
chart
of
(\mathscr{X},\Delta)
.

The pair
(\mathscr{X},\Delta)
is referred to as a
stacky semi-pair
if one of its charts
(X_{0},\Delta_{0})
is a semi-pair (Definition
5.6
). In this case, every chart of
(\mathscr{X},\Delta)
is a semi-pair.

Let
\mathscr{S}
be a smooth DM-stack. A
projective simple normal crossing stacky pair over
\mathscr{S}
is defined as a representable, projective, and simple normal crossing morphism
\mathscr{X}\to\mathscr{S}
, together with a simple normal crossing effective
\mathbb{Q}
-divisor
\Delta
on
\mathscr{X}
, such that every stratum of
(\mathscr{X},{\rm supp}(\Delta))
is (representable) smooth over
\mathscr{S}
.

Let
\mathscr{S}
be a reduced DM-stack. Let
\pi:S_{0}\to\mathscr{S}
be an étale cover from a reduced scheme
S_{0}
. A family of
(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)
-stable minimal models over
\mathscr{S}
consists of a representable projective morphism
f:\mathscr{X}\to\mathscr{S}
and two effective
\mathbb{Q}
-divisors
\mathscr{A}
and
\mathscr{B}
on
\mathscr{X}
, such that the base change family
(X_{0},B_{0}),A_{0}\to S_{0}
, as depicted in the following diagram,

\textstyle{(X_{0},B_{0}),A_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{0}}
\textstyle{(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{B}),\mathscr{A}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{S_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{\mathscr{S}}

is a family of
(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)
-stable minimal models over
S_{0}
. If
(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{B}),\mathscr{A}\to\mathscr{S}
is a family of
(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)
-stable minimal models and
T\to\mathscr{S}
is any étale covering map, then the base change family
(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{B}),\mathscr{A}\times_{\mathscr{S}}T\to T
is also a family of
(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)
-stable minimal models.

Definition 6.2
.

Let
(\mathscr{X},\Delta)
be a stacky semi-pair. A
semi-log resolution
is a representable, projective, and birational morphism
f:(\mathscr{X}^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})\to(\mathscr{X},\Delta)
such that, for any base change diagram of charts

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 22.29863pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-22.29863pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(X^{\prime}_{0},\Delta^{\prime}_{0})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-16.00891pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.8978pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\pi^{\prime}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 29.22241pt\raise 5.1875pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{g}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 46.29863pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 46.29863pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(X_{0},\Delta_{0})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 68.59726pt\raise-16.00891pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\pi}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 68.59726pt\raise-24.01782pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-19.83334pt\raise-32.01782pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(\mathscr{X}^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 28.95659pt\raise-25.90672pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{f}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 51.56947pt\raise-32.01782pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 51.56947pt\raise-32.01782pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{(\mathscr{X},\Delta)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces,

g
is a semi-log resolution of semi-pairs (Definition
5.7
).

Definition 6.3
.

Let
(\mathscr{X}_{1},\Delta_{1})\to\mathscr{S}
and
(\mathscr{X}_{2},\Delta_{2})\to\mathscr{S}
be two surjective morphisms from stacky semi-pairs to a smooth DM-stack
\mathscr{S}
over
{\rm Spec}(k)
. We say that
(\mathscr{X}_{1},\Delta_{1})
is strictly log birational to
(\mathscr{X}_{2},\Delta_{2})
over
\mathscr{S}
if there exists a common semi-log resolution
\pi_{i}:(\mathscr{X}^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})\to(\mathscr{X}_{i},\Delta_{i})
,
i=1,2
, over
\mathscr{S}
, such that the induced morphisms of fibers
\pi_{i,s}:(\mathscr{X}^{\prime}_{s},\Delta^{\prime}_{s})\to(\mathscr{X}_{i,s},\Delta_{i,s})
,
i=1,2
, are semi-log resolutions for every
s\in\mathscr{S}
.

Furthermore, we say that a morphism
f:(\mathscr{X},\Delta)\to\mathscr{S}
admits a
simple normal crossing strictly log birational model
if there exists a simple normal crossing family
f^{\prime}:(\mathscr{X}^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})\to\mathscr{S}
that is strictly log birational to
(\mathscr{X},\Delta)
over
\mathscr{S}
.

Definition 6.4
.

Let
f:(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{B}),\mathscr{A}\to\mathscr{S}
be a family of
(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)
-stable minimal models over a smooth DM-stack
\mathscr{S}
.
The morphism
f
is said to be
strictly birationally admissible
if
(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{B}+\mathscr{A})\to\mathscr{S}
admits a simple normal crossing strictly log birational model.

If
f:(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{B}),\mathscr{A}\to\mathscr{S}
is strictly birationally admissible and
S\to\mathscr{S}
is a morphism from a reduced scheme to
\mathscr{S}
, then the base change of
f
to
S
is birationally admissible.

Definition 6.5
.

Let
f:(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{B}),\mathscr{A}\to\mathscr{M}_{\rm slc}(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)
denote the universal family of
(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)
-stable minimal models. A
birationally admissible stratification
of
\mathscr{M}_{\rm slc}(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)
is a filtration by Zariski closed reduced stacks

\emptyset=S_{-1}\subset S_{0}\subset\cdots\subset S_{N}=\mathscr{M}_{\rm slc}(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)

satisfying that for each
i=0,\dots,N
, the pullback family of
f
to
S_{i}\setminus S_{i-1}
is strictly birationally admissible.

A stratum is defined as a connected component of
S_{i}\backslash S_{i-1}
for some
i\in\{0,\dots,N\}
.

Since the moduli of smooth varieties may not itself be smooth, we do not impose the condition that a birationally admissible stratification must be regular (i.e., that every stratum is smooth).
The subsequent lemma demonstrates that a birationally admissible stratification always exists and is uniquely determined by a functorial semi-log desingularization functor (
[
3
]
).

Lemma 6.6
.

Let
f:(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{B}),\mathscr{A}\to\mathscr{S}
be a family of stable minimal models over a reduced DM-stack
\mathscr{S}
of finite type over
{\rm Spec}(k)
. Then there exists a dense Zariski-open substack
U\subset\mathscr{S}
such that the restriction
f|_{U}
is strictly birationally admissible.

Proof.

Take a semi-log resolution
\pi:(\mathscr{X}^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})\to(\mathscr{X},\operatorname{supp}(\mathscr{A}+\mathscr{B}))
. By generic smoothness, there is a dense Zariski open substack
U\subset\mathscr{S}
such that
(\mathscr{X}^{\prime},\Delta^{\prime})
is a log smooth family over
U
and
\pi_{s}:(\mathscr{X}^{\prime}_{s},\Delta^{\prime}_{s})\to(\mathscr{X}_{s},\operatorname{supp}(\mathscr{A}+\mathscr{B})_{s})
is a semi-log resolution for every
s\in U
. This proves the lemma.
∎

A DM-stack is refer to as
hyperbolic
if for every generically finite morphism
T\to\mathscr{M}
from a smooth quasi-projective variety
T
, the variety
T
is of log general type.
The subsequent theorem is a direct consequence of Theorem
1.1
.

Theorem 6.7
.

Each stratum of a birationally admissible stratification of
\mathscr{M}_{\rm slc}(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)
is hyperbolic.

In general, the birationally admissible stratification of
\mathscr{M}_{\rm slc}(d,\Phi_{c},\Gamma,\sigma)
is not unique due to the non-uniqueness of the desingularization process. However, for the moduli stack
\overline{\mathscr{M}}_{g,n}
of genus
g
stable curves with
n
marked points (
2g-2+n>0
), the boundary simple normal crossing divisor
\partial\mathscr{M}_{g,n}:=\overline{\mathscr{M}}_{g,n}\setminus\mathscr{M}_{g,n}
induces a canonical stratification which is birationally admissible. The distinct strata in this stratification correspond precisely to the distinct topological types of the fibers.

Corollary 6.8
.

Every stratum of the canonical stratification of
\overline{\mathscr{M}}_{g,n}
is hyperbolic.
